PERFACE
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has emerged as one of the most transformative technologies of the
twenty-first century, influencing almost every sector of the global economy. Its rapid
development has created new opportunities for innovation, efficiency, stra tegic decision -
making, and sustainable growth. The fields of Commerce and Management are experiencing
significant transformation through the integration of Artificial Intelligence into business
processes, financial operations, marketing, human resource man agement, customer
relationship management, supply chain management, and strategic planning. Against this
rapidly evolving technological landscape, the National Level Seminar on “Application of
Artificial Intelligence in Commerce and Management”  provides an  important academic
platform for understanding, discussing, and exploring the emerging role of AI in
contemporary business and management practices. The seminar aims to bring together
academicians, researchers, industry professionals, practitioners, schola rs, and students to
exchange knowledge and perspectives on the applications and implications of Artificial
Intelligence. AI-enabled tools and technologies are increasingly supporting organizations in
analysing large volumes of data, identifying patterns, f orecasting market trends, improving
customer experiences, automating routine activities, and enhancing managerial decision -
making. In commerce, AI has applications in areas such as financial analytics, fraud
detection, accounting automation, taxation, audi ting, investment analysis, and digital
banking. In management, its applications extend to recruitment, employee analytics,
marketing intelligence, operations management, strategic management, and business
forecasting. The present seminar also recognizes th at the adoption of Artificial Intelligence is
not limited to technological advancement alone. It involves important considerations relating
to ethics, data privacy, cybersecurity, transparency, accountability, employment, digital skills,
and responsible te chnology adoption. Organizations need to develop appropriate strategies
for integrating AI while maintaining human judgement, professional responsibility, and
organizational values. Therefore, understanding both the opportunities and challenges
associated with AI is essential for students, educators, researchers, and business
professionals. This seminar serves as a valuable forum for presenting innovative ideas,
research findings, case studies, practical experiences, and emerging perspectives related to
AI-driven transformation in Commerce and Management. The contributions included in this
publication reflect diverse viewpoints on how AI can be utilized to improve organizational
effectiveness and create new avenues for business innovation. They also highligh t the
importance of developing AI literacy and future-ready competencies among management and
commerce graduates. We hope that the discussions and scholarly contributions presented
through this seminar will encourage further research, interdisciplinary col laboration, and
meaningful interaction between academia and industry. The knowledge shared during this
academic event can contribute to a better understanding of the changing business
environment and the evolving responsibilities of future professionals. W e sincerely
appreciate the contributions of all authors, researchers, resource persons, delegates,
organizing committee members, faculty members, and students whose valuable participation
has contributed to the success of this seminar. .                                                              Editors

AI-ENABLED WARFARE AND ITS IMPACT ON INTERNATIONAL TRADE
(A Study with Special Reference to the Iran–Israel War and Its Impact on International
Trade)
M. Siva Prakash
Assistant Professor
 Sriram College of Arts & Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
ABSTRACT
The increasing integration of Artificial Intelligence (AI) into modern warfare is transforming
the nature of armed conflicts and creating significant implications for the global economy.
AI-enabled warfare, including autonomous systems, intellige nt surveillance, cyber warfare,
and AI -assisted decision -making, has the potential to alter geopolitical relations, disrupt
supply chains, affect financial markets, and influence international trade flows.  This study
examines the impact of AI -enabled warfare on international trade, with special reference to
the Iran –Israel War and its broader economic implications. The study focuses on how
military conflicts and the use of advanced technologies can affect trade routes, transportation
and logistics, energy markets, commodity prices, foreign investment, and global supply-chain
stability.  Attention is given to the vulnerability of strategic trade routes and the potential
spillover effects of regional conflicts on countries that are indirectly connected through
international trade. The study adopts a descriptive and analytical approach based on
secondary data collected from international organizations, government reports, research
publications, economic databases, and other relevant sources. The study argues tha t AI -
enabled warfare can intensify economic uncertainty and create new forms of trade disruption
beyond the immediate geographical boundaries of a conflict. It also highlights the need for
stronger international cooperation, resilient supply chains, techno logical preparedness, and
appropriate policy frameworks to minimize the adverse effects of technology -driven conflicts
on global trade. The findings are expected to contribute to a better understanding of the
emerging relationship between artificial intell igence, warfare, geopolitics, and international
trade.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, AI-Enabled Warfare, International Trade, Iran–Israel War,
Global Supply Chains, Geopolitics, Economic Impact, Trade Disruption.
INTRODUCTION
The rapid advancement of Artificial Intelligence (AI) is transforming modern warfare using
autonomous drones, precision-guided weapons, cyber capabilities, advanced surveillance, and
AI-assisted decision -making. Unlike conventional conflicts, AI -enabled warfare has
consequences that extend beyond the battlefield, affecting global markets, financial systems,
transportation, energy markets, and international supply chains. The interconnected nature of
the global economy makes international trade particularly vulnerable to geopolitical conflicts.
Wars can disrupt trade routes, increase transportation and insurance costs, affect commodity
and energy prices, reduce investment confidence, and create supply -chain uncertainties. The
Iran–Israel conflict provides an important context for exa mining these effects, given the

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 2

strategic importance of West Asia to global energy supplies and international transportation.
This study examines how AI -enabled warfare can influence international trade and the wider
global economy, with particular focus o n supply-chain disruptions, energy markets, logistics,
commodity prices, foreign investment, and economic stability. It also highlights the need for
international cooperation, resilient supply chains, technological preparedness, and effective
policy measures to minimize the economic consequences of technology -driven conflicts. The
study is significant in understanding how the growing militarization of AI may shape the
future of warfare and the global trading system.
 Research Motivation:
While examining the  current geopolitical conflict between Iran and Israel, as well as the
Russia–Ukraine War, I observed a significant transformation in the nature and conduct of
modern warfare. Unlike the conventional wars of the past, contemporary conflicts are
increasingly being shaped by advanced technologies, sophisticated military strategies, and
AI-integrated weapon systems. The battlefield is no longer limited to conventional soldiers,
tanks, aircraft, and artillery; it now increasingly involves autonomous and semi -autonomous
drones, precision -guided missiles, cyber warfare, advanced surveillance systems, and AI -
assisted military technologies.  What particularly drew my attention was the growing role of
technology in determining the course and intensity of these conflic ts. Even with the military
and technological support provided by major powers such as the United States, conflicts
involving countries such as Ukraine and Israel demonstrate that technological superiority and
external support do not necessarily translate i nto quick or decisive victories. The increasing
sophistication of AI-integrated drones, missiles, surveillance systems, and other autonomous
technologies has fundamentally altered traditional concepts of warfare. Some of these
technologies possess capabili ties that, until recently, appeared largely confined to science -
fiction films.  Humanity is rapidly progressing towards a technologically advanced future
through Artificial Intelligence. However, the same technological progress raises serious
questions about its potential consequences. AI can function as a powerful tool for innovation,
economic development, productivity, and human welfare, but its integration into military
systems can also transform it into a highly destructive weapon. The increasing use of AI-
enabled drones and weapons has the potential to intensify conflicts and create consequences
that extend far beyond the battlefield.
These consequences are particularly important from an economic perspective. Modern
conflicts can disrupt international trade, global supply chains, transportation networks, energy
markets, investment flows, commodity prices, and business operations. Since national
economies are increasingly interconnected, the economic repercussions of a regional conflict
can spread across b orders and influence the global market. Thus, the impact of AI -enabled
warfare should not be viewed solely from a military or geopolitical perspective; it must also
be examined in relation to international trade, the world economy, and the global business
environment. This observation led to a fundamental question that forms the underlying
motivation of this study: Are we evolving through technology, or are we creating new ways

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 3

to destroy ourselves? More specifically, is Artificial Intelligence merely a too l for human
advancement, or is it gradually becoming a weapon capable of threatening global stability?
This paper attempts to explore these questions by examining the emergence of AI -enabled
warfare and its economic implications, with reference to the Iran –Israel conflict. The study
analyses how the increasing use of AI -integrated military technologies can influence
international trade, global markets, supply chains, energy prices, investment, and the broader
business landscape. Based on the analysis of ava ilable data and secondary sources, the study
further attempts to identify the potential risks and opportunities associated with the growing
militarization of Artificial Intelligence and provides recommendations aimed at reducing its
adverse economic and gl obal consequences. Ultimately, this study seeks to contribute to the
broader debate surrounding the dual nature of Artificial Intelligence: a technology capable of
accelerating human progress, but also a technology whose misuse could create unprecedented
risks for humanity and the global economy.
 Objectives of the Study
The present study aims to examine the relationship between AI -enabled warfare and
international trade, with special reference to the Iran–Israel War. The specific objectives are:
1. To examine the emergence and growing role of Artificial Intelligence in modern warfare.
2. To analyze the impact of AI -enabled warfare on international trade and global supply
chains.
3. To examine the economic impact of the Iran –Israel War on global trade and the  world
economy.
4. To analyze the effects of the conflict on energy prices, commodity markets, transportation
costs, and trade routes.
5. To assess the impact of AI -integrated drones, missiles, cyber warfare, and other advanced
technologies on the global business environment.
6. To examine the impact of geopolitical uncertainty on foreign investment, financial
markets, and international business activities.
7. To identify the major challenges posed by AI -enabled warfare to global economic and
trade stability.
8. To suggest suitable measures and policy recommendations to strengthen international trade
resilience against technology-driven conflicts.
9. To examine whether the increasing use of AI in warfare represents technological
advancement or creates new risks to global economic and human security.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE:
1) How Russia Is Reshaping Command and Control for AI -Enabled Warfare - Kateryna
Bondar (A Report of the CSIS Wadhwani AI Center).

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 4

This paper examines how Russia is transforming its command a nd control (C2) architecture
under wartime pressure, how these changes shape the country’s incremental move toward
battlefield-required software solutions, and what lessons U.S. policymakers can learn from
Russia’s experiences. Focusing on both strategic a mbitions and battlefield practice, the
takeaways below summarize how automated C2 systems, unmanned platform management
software, and emerging AI applications are being developed, adapted, and scaled within
Russia’s military ecosystem.
2) The Crisis in Ukraine: Implications of the War for Global Trade and Development - World
Trade Organization (2022).
The World Trade Organization (WTO, 2022) examined the implications of the Russia –
Ukraine War for global trade and development. The study highlighted the import ance of
Russia and Ukraine as suppliers of food, energy, and industrial inputs and showed that the
conflict created significant risks for food and energy security and global supply chains. WTO
simulations indicated that the war could substantially reduce g lobal trade and GDP growth
under adverse scenarios.
3) War Dims Global Economic Outlook as Inflation Accelerates - International Monetary
Fund (2022).
The International Monetary Fund (IMF, 2022) examined the broader economic consequences
of the Russia –Ukraine War and observed that the conflict generated supply shocks through
commodity markets, trade, and financial linkages. The disruption of supplies of oil, gas,
metals, wheat, and other commodities contributed to higher prices and inflationary pressures
across economies. The study demonstrated how the economic consequences of a regional
conflict can spread globally through interconnected markets.
4) One Year of War in Ukraine: Assessing the Impact on Global Trade and Development -
World Trade Organization (2023).
The WTO (2023) further assessed the impact of one year of the Russia –Ukraine War on
global trade. The study found that international trade demonstrated considerable resilience
despite the conflict, as affected countries were able to identify alternati ve sources of supply.
However, Ukraine's exports declined significantly, while prices of several commodities
affected by the conflict increased. The study also emphasized that excessive geopolitical
fragmentation of global supply chains could impose substa ntial long -term economic costs,
particularly on developing economies.
5) The Cost of Geopolitical Fragmentation- Bolhuis, M. A., Chen, J., & Kett, B. (2023).
Bolhuis, Chen and Kett (2023) examined the costs of geoeconomic fragmentation and argued
that geopolitical tensions and trade restrictions can disrupt the supply of critical commodities.
Their analysis showed that the Russia –Ukraine War contributed to significant increases in
energy, food, and mineral prices. The study also highlighted the growing impo rtance of
national security considerations in trade policy and strategic supply chains.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 5

6) Fragmentation and Commodity Markets: Vulnerabilities and Risks - International
Monetary Fund (2023).
The IMF (2023) examined the vulnerability of commodity markets to  geopolitical
fragmentation. The study found that commodity markets are particularly susceptible to
disruption because production is often geographically concentrated and many commodities
have limited short -term substitutes. Further fragmentation of commod ity trade could
therefore result in significant price volatility and uneven economic consequences across
countries.
7) The Potential Impact of the USA–Israel–Iran Military Conflict on Global Trade Flows and
Supply Chain Systems: A Systematic Literature Review- Msosa (2026).
More recent research has specifically begun examining the implications of the Iran –Israel
conflict for global trade and supply chains. A 2026 systematic literature review by Msosa
examined the potential effects of the USA –Israel–Iran military conflict on global trade flows
and supply -chain systems. The study reviewed peer -reviewed literature published between
2020 and 2026 and identified significant concerns regarding the potential disruption of
international trade and supply-chain networks.
 Research Gap
The existing literature predominantly focuses on the conventional economic consequences of
war, geopolitical fragmentation, commodity -price shocks, sanctions, and supply -chain
disruptions. Although increasing attention has been given to AI  and emerging military
technologies, there remains a need for an integrated economic analysis connecting AI -
enabled warfare, geopolitical conflict, international trade, global markets, and business
activity. In particular, the relationship between AI -integrated weapons and the indirect
economic consequences of the Iran –Israel conflict requires further examination. This study
attempts to address this gap by analyzing the emerging relationship between AI -enabled
warfare and international trade, while also dra wing comparisons with the economic
consequences observed during the Russia–Ukraine War.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY:
 Research Design
The present study adopts a descriptive and analytical research design to examine the
relationship between AI-enabled warfare and international trade, with special reference to the
Iran–Israel War. The study attempts to understand the economic and trade -related
consequences of technology -driven geopolitical conflicts and their potential implications for
the global economy.
 Nature of Study
The Study is primarily quantitative and qualitative in nature. Quantitative information is used
to examine changes in selected economic and trade indicators, while qualitative information

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 6

is used to understand the geopolitical, technological, and economic dimensions of AI-enabled
warfare.
 Sources of Data
The study is based mainly on secondary data. Data and information are collected from
reliable sources such as:
1. World Trade Organization (WTO)
2. International Monetary Fund (IMF)
3. World Bank
4. United Nations Conference on Trade and Development (UNCTAD)
5. International Energy Agency (IEA)
6. Government and international organization reports
7. Central bank and official statistical publications
8. Research articles and academic journals
9. Reputed newspapers and financial publications
10. Industry and trade reports
11. Relevant databases and official websites
 Scope of the Study
The study focuses on the economic consequences of AI -enabled warfare rather than
analyzing the technical or military effectiveness of individual weapons. Att ention is given to
its implications for international trade, global supply chains, energy markets, transportation,
investment, financial markets, and the broader business environment.
 Limitations of the Study
The study is subject to certain limitations. Th e Iran–Israel conflict is an evolving geopolitical
situation, and therefore complete long-term economic data may not yet be available. It is also
difficult to isolate the specific economic effects of AI -enabled warfare from the broader
effects of conventio nal military operations, sanctions, political decisions, and global
economic conditions. Furthermore, the availability and reliability of conflict -related data may
vary across sources.
DATA INTERPRETATION:
 Role of AI in Warfare:
AI allows for rapid processing and analysis of information from a variety of different sources
and customizable data access. Its adoption across the military domain has the potential to
increase situational awareness, facilitate real -time information sharing and enable more
informed decision-making in military operations.
A 2024 US Department of War release  outlined how the AI -enabled Maven Smart System
helps frontline  soldiers identify and strike military targets and assists chain -of-command
approval for strikes. NATO also  acquired a version of Maven Smart System from Palantir in

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 7

2025. The US military is now reportedly using its own version of Maven to help  provide
targeting information for its military operations in Iran. But it is unclear exactly how and to
what extent Maven and other AI tools are being used in Iran.  In the war in Ukraine, both
sides are using AI for data processing and target selection. U kraine’s deputy defense
minister said last  year that AI analyses more than 50,000 video streams from the front line
each month, which helps to ‘quickly pr ocess this massive data, identify targets, and put them
on a map.’
The New York Times  has reported that Israel used AI  as part of its process of identifying
potential targets for air strikes targeting Hamas in Gaza. The IDF has said that ‘information
systems are merely tools for analysts in the targ et identification process.’ Additional uses for
AI technology across the military domain include the training of military personnel through
virtual simulations, the automated scheduling of logistical supplies or the identification of
equipment maintenance needs via image -recognition systems. These are just some of the
potential uses.
Source: The Iran war highlights the creeping use of AI in warfare -
https://www.chathamhouse.org
 Drones in Israel–US–Iran War:
In the ongoing US –Israel–Iran war, there was an asy mmetry of air power between Iran and
the US–Israel combined forces. Yet, drones created multiple operational options for Iran. Iran
deployed various types of drones for Intelligence, Surveillance and Reconnaissance (ISR) and
for undertaking air strike miss ions against air defense systems, critical military and civil
infrastructure, oil refineries and airports.  Iranian drones destroyed oil fields and refineries of
Gulf partners of the US, as well as those of Israel. Air traffic was disrupted. High -value
military targets like radars, etc., were also found to be vulnerable to drone attacks. Defense
installations and critical energy, aviation and other infrastructure of the Gulf countries
equipped with advanced US and Israeli weapons were found vulnerable to Ira nian drone
attacks, and most of them suffered heavy losses.  The Gulf countries sought Ukrainian help in
countering Iranian drone attacks. Iran has so far destroyed 12 MQ -9B and 12 Hermes -900,
Hermes-450 and Heron UAVs. Iran was able to leverage vulnerabilities to land/crash-land the
Hermes-900 UAV of Israel with full weapon and sensor load. Iran has done a similar
takeover of US drones in the past. Three F -15 fighter aircraft of the US were shot down by
Kuwaiti F-18 fighters in friendly fire, highlighting g aps in Unmanned Traffic Management
and airspace integration technologies.
Iranian drones provided Iran with a capability that no other technology could have granted it.
Iran’s ability to achieve this stalemate comes from the following:
 Iran’s single-minded approach to technology sovereignty.
 Shahed and many other indigenously developed drones are now being imitated by the
US and other countries.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 8

 The narrative that Iran was dependent on Western suppliers, too, does not seem to be
proven, as Iran continues to  launch thousands of drones without the disruption of
suppliers.
 The Iranian military played a key role in drone technology development, which took
ownership and took the lead in the development of operationally useful drone
technologies.
 Iranian military and technology developers have been audacious in hijacking US and
Israeli drones and were pragmatic in adopting them for their development.
 The Iranian military did not believe in collaborations or licensed manufacturing of
Chinese drones, as was done by Saudi Arabia and others.
 Iran war-gamed counter-drone technology gaps and penetrated supposedly the most
advanced air defenses with continuous upgradation of drone, cyber and electronic
warfare technologies associated with drone development and drone hacking.
 The poorly guarded oil refineries, airports, vulnerable Hormuz Strait, and
vulnerabilities of economically progressing Arab States were ably exploited by Iran.
 The establishment of the Islamic Republic of Iran Ministry of Defense Export Centre
(Mindex) in 1989 and the Ministry of Defense and Armed Forces Logistics leading
the defense exports ensured continuity and technology development, upgradation and
their export, which seems to be the critical pillar of technology ownership by defense
forces and accounts for the success of their drone programme, among others.
The US deployed drones through Task Force Scorpion, including LUCAS loitering drones
(influenced by the Iranian Shahed design) and MQ -9B turboprop Medium Altitude Long
Endurance (MALE) UAVs, agai nst Iran. Israel deployed ISR and one -way loitering drones,
including Hermes-900 turboprop MALE UAVs. Counter -drone systems in the US and Israel
were expensive and had gaps in their capabilities. The US, Israel and its Gulf partners
deployed fighter aircraft and attack helicopters equipped with counter -drone systems against
the Iranian UAVs.
Source: https://idsa.in/publisher/comments/drones-in-israel-us-iran-war-key-takeaways
 Economic impact of the Iran–Israel proxy conflict:
The Iran–Israel proxy conflict  and the  direct hostilities in mid -2025 have strained Iran’s
finances, infrastructure, and human capital . Iran has incurred  direct costs  from military
expenditures and war damage , indirect costs  from decades of sanctions, an d shadow
costs such as  currency devaluation , high  inflation, lost  oil revenue, and the erosion of its
scientific and military workforce. These pressures have exacerbated pre -existing economic
weaknesses in Iran and also had regional spillover effects on energy markets and neighboring
economies. During the 2025 war, Israel suffered casualties with thousands injured and

4. Destruction of infrastructure
Israeli bombing raids hit critical infrastructure in Iran across 21 of the country’s 31
provinces. Targets included major airports (such as Tabriz Airport  and Tehran’s Mehrabad
Airport), oil and gas facilities, power grids, and stra tegically important industries. Strikes on
Iran’s oil infrastructure was limited but not negligible – for example, Israeli missiles struck
an oil refinery in southern Tehran (Shahr Rey refinery), causing a massive fire and forcing its
shutdown. They also hit fuel depots around the capital, and even a section of the giant  South
Pars gas field  (which provides ~80% of Iran’s gas) was damaged by missile strikes, leading
Iran to temporarily suspend part of its gas output.   The war targeted some "domestic" energy
assets rather than export terminals, so Iran’s export capacity was somewhat spared;
nevertheless, the immediate effect  was dramatic – Iran’s oil exports in the week of 18 June
2025 fell to roughly 102,000 barrels per day, less than half the recent average.  Tanker traffic
from Iran’s main export port ( Kharg Island ) virtually halted during the height of the
conflict. This translates to tens of millions of dollars in lost revenue for those weeks and
underscores how vulnerable Iran’s income is to security disruptions. Beyond energy, Israeli
strikes al so hit military and nuclear sites, research facilities, and government buildings in
Tehran (even the Defense Ministry and state TV headquarters were bombed).   Iran’s civilian
infrastructure did not escape damage either – power stations and communication ne tworks
suffered outages from the raids and accompanying cyber -attacks. The physical damage to
Iran will require major reconstruction. Conservative estimates put the immediate rebuilding
cost in the tens of billions of dollars for power grids, refineries, t ransportation networks, and
other facilities.
5. Military expenditures and losses
Over the 12 days, Iran launched hundreds of  ballistic missi les and drones at Israel. These
weapons are costly to replace. Analysts estimate that Iran’s medium -range ballistic missiles
cost on the order of  $1–3 million each. A single concentrated salvo on 1 October 2024 (prior
to the all -out war) involved about 180 missiles and was calculated to have cost Tehran
roughly $2.3 billion in missiles and related expenses.  This was an immense one -day expense
– equivalent to about 22% of Iran’s annual defense budget and over 50 million percent of

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 11

Iran’s GDP per capita.  During the June 2025 war, Iran fired 550 missiles and launched 1000
drones.
6. Nuclear program setbacks
A focal point of Israel’s offensive was  Iran’s nuclear infrastructure . Key nuclear sites
at Natanz, Fordow, and  Isfahan were struck, including with U.S. " bunker buster " bombs
on June 21, 2025 .[3] These attacks damaged Iran’s enrichment plants - for instance,  Israel
claimed to have heavily damaged Natanz’s underground halls containing advanced
centrifuges. Vital support infrastructure (power supplies, centrifuge workshops, etc.) was also
hit, likely knocking the facilities offline.
7. Human casualties and talent drain
At least 610 Iranian civilians were killed and over 4,700 injured by Israeli strikes, according
to Iran’s Health Ministry  Dozens of important personnel were also eliminated. The strikes
killed at least six top nuclear scientists and engineers who we re present at targeted
sites. Among them were figures like  Dr. Fereydoun Abbasi  (former head of the Atomic
Energy Organization) engineering.  Around 20 senior commanders we re assassinated in
precision strikes.  Those killed included some of the country’s highest -ranking officers –
notably General Mohammad Bagheri  (Chief of  Staff of Iran’s armed forces) and
Major General Hossein Salami  (Commander of the IRGC).  The death toll also encompassed
many IRGC regional commanders and missile experts.
8. Market effects
By late June 2025, with U.S. President  Donald Trump  warning of worse to come, the rial
traded well above 1,000,000 IRR per USD in street markets.  After the war the  Tehran stock
exchange saw selloffs in many sectors.  Gold prices fell by 2% after Trump announced
ceasefire.
9. Impacts on Israel
During the ceasefire, it was reported that the death toll was at 29 killed, over 3,238 injured
(including 28 in serious condition, 111 in moderate condition),  and over 9,000 others
displaced. According to the Israel Central Bank Governor the economic cost of the 12 days
war with Iran is $6bn.
Source: Wikipedia
 Economic Impact of the 2026 Iran War (Global level)
The article describes the 2026 Iran War as a major geopolitical and economic shock, with
effects extending well beyond Iran and Israel. The most important economic consequences
are linked to energy supplies, international trade, shipping, aviation, financial markets,
commodity prices, and global supply chains.
1. Energy crisis
The closure of the Strait of Hormuz is presented as the central economic issue. The article
states tha t the Strait carries roughly 20% of global seaborne crude -oil and LNG exports,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 12

making its disruption highly significant for the world economy. Oil prices rose sharply during
the initial phase of the conflict, with Brent crude reaching $118.35 per barrel on  31 March
2026 before falling to $71.57 by 1 July.
2. International trade and supply chains
The disruption to shipping through the Gulf affected the movement of oil, LNG, food,
fertilizers, minerals, and other commodities. Countries dependent on the regio n for imports
had to consider alternative suppliers and transportation routes. The article also notes that
vessels avoided some traditional routes, increasing the distance and cost of transporting
goods.
3. Aviation
Airspace closures across several Middle  Eastern countries severely disrupted international
aviation. Airlines were forced to take longer routes, increasing fuel consumption, operating
costs, travel times, and ticket prices. Several airlines suspended services to the region, while
thousands of flights were cancelled.
4. Financial markets
The conflict initially produced considerable volatility in global financial markets. Stock
markets declined, investors moved towards safe -haven assets such as gold, and some
emerging-market currencies came under  pressure. Higher energy prices also created
difficulties for central banks because they increased inflationary pressures while making
interest-rate reductions more difficult. However, the article also shows that the initial
financial-market shock did not necessarily persist. For example, the S&P 500 subsequently
recovered substantially from its March 2026 level by July.
5. Commodity and food markets
The conflict disrupted supplies of commodities such as Sulphur, urea, LNG, and helium. This
is particularly important because Sulphur and urea are inputs into fertilizer production, while
helium is important for semiconductor manufacturing. Disruptions to fertilizer supplies could
consequently increase agricultural production costs and food prices.
6. Impact on Europe
Europe faced significant risks because of disruptions to Middle Eastern energy supplies. The
article reports higher European gas prices and pressure on energy -intensive industries such as
chemical and steel manufacturing. Higher energy costs could  therefore affect European
industrial competitiveness and economic growth.
7. Impact on Asian economies
Asian economies are particularly important in the article because countries such as China,
India, Japan, and South Korea account for a large share of o il and LNG exports passing

through the region. Consequently, disruptions to energy transportation can increase import
costs and inflationary pressure in Asian economies.

The available data indicate that  the economic impact of AI -enabled warfare extends far
beyond the battlefield, particularly through energy markets, international trade, supply chains,
transportation, and financial markets.

Interpretation
 The data suggests that AI -enabled warfare creates multiple channels of economic
disruption. The use of drones, AI -assisted targeting, surveillance and cyber capabilities
can increase the speed and technological sophistication of warfare, while the resulting
conflict can disrupt physical and digital infrastructure.
 The Strait of Hormuz is particularly important because its disruption can affect a
substantial share of global energy trade. The movement  of oil and LNG through the
region means that a regional conflict can quickly become a global economic concern. The
rise in Brent crude to $118.35 per barrel during the 2026 conflict illustrates the sensitivity
of energy markets to geopolitical uncertainty.
 The Iran data also demonstrate the domestic economic cost of prolonged conflict and
geopolitical isolation. Currency depreciation, high inflation, reduced oil exports,
infrastructure damage and military expenditure can weaken economic stability and reduc e
resources available for productive investment.
 At the global level, disruption to shipping, aviation, energy, fertilizer and commodity
supplies can increase transportation and production costs. These costs may ultimately be
transferred to consumers throu gh higher prices. Financial -market volatility further
demonstrates how geopolitical uncertainty can influence investor behavior.
 Thus, the evidence suggests that the economic consequences of modern warfare are not
restricted to military expenditure or phys ical destruction. In an interconnected global
economy, technological warfare can generate indirect effects through energy markets,
international trade, supply chains, financial markets and business activity.
 Important academic note: The figures above shoul d be presented as reported estimates
rather than definitive causal measurements. In particular, the economic effects of the
Iran–Israel conflict cannot automatically be attributed to AI alone, because conventional
military operations, sanctions, geopolitic al decisions and market expectations also
influence these outcomes.

SUGGESTIONS:
Recommendation to the UNO:
The United Nations Organization (UNO) should establish an international agreement with its
member states to identify and designate key international trade routes and other critical
infrastructure that are essential to global trade and the world economy as “Protected Global
Economic Assets.” Like the protection provided to historically and culturally significant sites
under the World Herit age framework, these strategically important regions should receive
special protection under international law. Member states should agree that such designated
trade routes, ports, energy corridors, communication networks, and other critical
infrastructure must not be deliberately targeted or attacked during armed conflicts. This
framework could help safeguard the continuity of international trade, reduce global economic
disruptions, and strengthen the resilience of the global economy during periods of
geopolitical conflict.  Based on the analysis of AI -enabled warfare and its implications for
international trade and the global economy, the following suggestions are proposed:
1. Strengthen international cooperation:  Countries should strengthen diplomatic and
multilateral mechanisms to prevent the escalation of technology -driven conflicts and reduce
their spillover effects on the global economy.
2. Develop international AI governance:  International organizations should establish
appropriate frameworks governing  the development and military use of AI, particularly
autonomous and AI-assisted weapons.
3. Diversify energy sources:  Countries heavily dependent on West Asian energy supplies
should diversify their energy sources and suppliers to reduce vulnerability to geopolitical
disruptions.
4. Build resilient supply chains:  Governments and businesses should develop diversified
supply chains, maintain strategic inventories of essential commodities, and identify
alternative suppliers and transportation routes.
5. Protect critical infrastructure:  Greater investment should be made in protecting ports,
airports, energy facilities, financial systems, communication networks, and logistics
infrastructure from both physical and AI-enabled cyberattacks.
6. Strengthen cybersecur ity: Governments and businesses should improve cybersecurity
capabilities to protect digital systems that support international trade, banking, logistics, and
supply-chain management.
7. Promote technological self -reliance: Countries should develop domesti c capabilities in
critical technologies such as AI, semiconductors, drones, cybersecurity, and advanced
communication systems while maintaining responsible international cooperation.
8. Establish early -warning systems: International institutions should dev elop mechanisms
to monitor geopolitical risks and provide early warnings regarding potential disruptions to
energy supplies, trade routes, commodity markets, and supply chains.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 16

9. Support vulnerable economies:  International financial institutions should pr ovide
appropriate financial and technical assistance to developing and import -dependent economies
that are disproportionately affected by conflict-related energy and commodity shocks.
10. Encourage responsible AI development:  AI should be promoted primaril y for peaceful
applications such as healthcare, education, agriculture, disaster management, logistics, and
economic development, while adequate safeguards should be established against its
irresponsible militarization.
11. Improve business preparedness: Multinational corporations and other businesses should
incorporate geopolitical and technological risks into their risk -management strategies and
develop contingency plans for sudden trade and supply-chain disruptions.
12. Balance technological progress wit h human security:  The advancement of AI should
be accompanied by ethical considerations, international regulations, and human oversight so
that technological development contributes to human welfare rather than increasing risks to
global peace and economic stability.
Key Recommendation
The central recommendation of the study is that AI should be treated not merely as a
technological asset but as a dual -use technology requiring responsible governance.
International cooperation, resilient trade networks, dive rsified energy supplies, cybersecurity,
and meaningful human oversight can help ensure that AI contributes to economic
development while reducing the risks associated with its use in warfare.

THE IMPACT OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE ON HUMAN RESOURCE
ACTIVITIES: OPPORTUNITIES AND CHALLENGES
Dr. MU. Arumugam
Assistant Professor & Head
Department of B. Com (CS)
 Sriram College of Arts & Science
Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
Mrs. P. Ahila
 Full – Time Ph.D. Scholar
 Department of Economics, Presidency College, Chennai-05
Abstract
Human resources are a necessary asset in this competitive world for improving organizational
performance. To stand out from thei r rivals and boost performance, businesses must strive to
implement cutting -edge HR practices. In the not -too-distant future, more advanced
advancements like automation, augmented intelligence, robotics, and AI will replace the
traditional HR practices. It  has been demonstrated that AI will alter our lives. Professionals
today are more focused on optimizing the combination of human and automated work to
achieve a simple and intuitive work environment, from automating repetitive and time -
consuming tasks. The real challenge now lies within the HR department: how will they train
and re-transform their workforce in understanding AI and collaborating and working with AI
and robotics augmentation and amplification of human capabilities, AI has the potential to
dramatically transform the way we live and work. It provides them with enough time to
deliver enhanced employee performance.
Keyword: Artificial intelligence in HR
Introduction
Artificial intelligence (AI), also known as machine intelligence in computer scie nce, is
intelligence expressed by computers, in contrast to the natural intelligence of humans and
other animals. Computer science defines AI research as the study of "intelligent agents,"
which are any machines that can comprehend their surroundings and t ake actions to improve
their chances of success.  Various facets of human resources, including talent acquisition and
development, performance management, and employee engagement, are set to be
transformed by this potent technology, which is contributing t o the landscape of human
resource management transformation. This is happening not only locally or globally, but also
in the workplace itself. As a result of a widening range of HR -related issues and a growing
role for HR management in strategic business d ecision-making, HR management has
undergone significant change. HR practices that are tailored to each target are becoming
increasingly popular in today's workplace. The objective is to assist managers in improving
intergenerational collaboration. Companie s can use their "employee" databases effectively
through Human Resources Analysis to make the best decisions and boost operational

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 19

performance. Artificial intelligence based on "machine learning" technology promises to
revolutionize human resources departm ents on multiple levels at a time when business
leaders are preparing for a digital world that is gaining momentum. The major challenges
faced by any HR department are recruitment, training, career management, mobility,
compensation and benefits to attract  talent and high potential, treating and evaluating
nominations as quickly as possible, determining the suitability of the profile and position, and
predicting the candidate's added value to the company. The HR department will be able to
capitalize and ele vate its function to a higher strategic and decision -making level based on
this reflection and intuition. While data and algorithms allow for a more effective expression
of this intelligence, some may mistake them for intelligence in their decision -making. It is
essential to comprehend that data is not independent if it becomes unavoidable. Human
intelligence is the only thing that can truly add value to this information. Data does not
replace the intelligence and courage of HR; it is at their service.  HR has the tools to build
their future, supported by data. The challenge that comes with HR's taming of data is
challenging ready -made representations and preconceived notions in order to generate
propositions based on these observations.
Artificial Intelligence:
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is a rapidly evolving technology, made possible by the Internet
that will soon have major impacts on our daily lives.  In 1956, the term "artificial
intelligence" was established.  These characteristics enable AI to bring enormous
socioeconomic opportunities and challenges. When it comes to AI, there are a number of
things that need to be taken into consideration, such as its effects on the economy,
transparency, bias, and accountability, new uses for data, safety and security concerns, and
ethical questions. AI can also help create new ecosystems. A lack of photograph and
interpretability in executive, issues with data superiority and potential bias, safety and refuge
inferences, considerations regarding accountabi lity, and AI's potential disruptive effects on
social and economic structures are just some of the specific challenges AI faces in this
complex field. The Internet Culture has advanced a set of philosophies and commendations in
situation to what we believe  to be the core "abilities" that underpin the value the Internet
provides after evaluating the various considerations and comprehending the various
challenges. AI has established enlarged courtesy in recent years.  The Internet has enabled
invention, which  has carried AI closer to our normal lives. AI is at the forefront of many
current debates thanks to these advancements and interest in technology’s potential
socioeconomic and ethical effects.
Human Resource Management:
That subfield of management is concerned with employee recruitment, selection, growth, and
optimal utilization. As a result, human resource management ensures that each employee
contributes fully to the achievement of the organization's objectives. Since t he industrial
revolution, a number of significant interconnected advancements have led to the human
resource management we see today. The need for a person who could serve as a crucial link
between management and employees grew as trade union movements dev eloped. In the

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 20

beginning, a Labor Welfare Officer was responsible for this role, which was limited to
employee welfare activities. The factory system came into existence over time. Under one
roof, thousands of people were employed. There has been an increase in the need to employ
more workers. A person who went by the title "Personnel Officer" was also given the
responsibility of recruiting and supervising employees for the organization. His primary
responsibility was to find, select, and hire workers. Rapi d technological advancement
necessitated the development of new skills and employee training as time went on. The
human relations approach recognized that people are an organization's most valuable
resource. Human Resource Management had to take the place of the traditional notions of
labor welfare and personnel management. Human Resource Management is currently
responsible for all of these facets, including recruitment, selection, placement, and labor
welfare, among others. Human Resource Management's prim ary responsibilities include: (i)
figuring out how many and what kinds of workers are needed. (ii) Employee selection, hiring,
and retention. (iii) Offering employees training to help them perform better and advance in
their careers. Iv)) Protecting busine sses from legal issues v) Building friendly relationships
between management and the union. Every managerial role in any business is performed by
individuals. Without competent and effective employees, no business can function and
accomplish its goals. Emp loyees who are committed, honest, determined, and loyal are
essential for the organization's growth as well as its continued existence. The human
requirements of the organization are evaluated after deciding what and how to be done
through planning and dec iding the organization structure. The staffing function begins with
an assessment of the organization's needs before recruiting and selecting employees in
accordance with those needs. Along with marketing management and financial management,
staffing is also regarded as a distinct functional area of management. In addition, employees'
success depends on the stability and quality of their assigned jobs. The department of human
resources plays an important role in ensuring that this is the case. As a result, staffing is
referred to as both "a line and a staff activity," or a distinct functional area of management
and an essential management function.
Artificial Intelligence in Human Resource Management:
Executives in human resources are hopeful that incorporating AI into HR administration tasks
will enhance the employee experience as a whole. For effective people management, this will
provide more capacity, more time, more money, and more precise data. The use of AI studies
provides improved insight into how to  execute and operate as humans and learning machines
collaborate to produce an ever -increasing amount of HR data in the cloud. An organization's
success depends on how it intelligently combines people, processes, and technology to
provide transformational value at optimal cost. For reliable HR transactions and service
delivery, AI will assist in the efficient automation of numerous back -office functions. This
document provides information about intelligent automation through the technology -agnostic
chatbot and is focused on the capabilities of conversational AI for HR transactions.
Role of Artificial Intelligence in Human Resource Management
AI Converting the Forthcoming of HR -These include things like : Resume Screening and
Candidate Sourcing: The AI algor ithms are able to look at resumes and job descriptions to

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 21

find potential candidates based on qualifications, skills, or experience. Scheduling Interviews:
With the help of AI tools, interview scheduling can be automated by providing time estimates
based on the preferences of candidates and interviewers. This makes it easier to do the work
of administration and makes sure that people talk well.  Onboarding and Training: AI
chatbots can assist new employees with their first day of work by answering questions and
providing access to relevant data and resources. Using AI -Based Training Platforms, learning
experience can also be tailored to each student's requirements and skill levels.
Data-Driven Decision Making - HR professionals now have access to a vast amoun t of data
thanks to AI, allowing them to make decisions that are more objective and well -informed.
This comprises: Predictive Analytics: AI models can analyze historical data to predict
employee performance, turnover risk, and even potential skill gaps in the workforce. Human
resources teams are able to deal with problems before they happen and take specific steps to
improve.  Sentiment Analysis: AI and surveys, emails, and other employee communications
can be analyzed to understand employee sentiments and spot potential problems with morale,
engagement, or burnout. A positive working environment can be created with prompt
intervention and support thanks to early insight.  Optimization of Compensation and Benefits:
AI will be able to analyze market data, emp loyee demographics, and performance indicators
in order to recommend equitable and competitive compensation and benefits packages for the
workforce.
Personalized Employee Experiences - At every stage of the employment lifecycle, AI has
the potential to personalize the employee’s experience. This comprises: Personalized learning
paths and career development opportunities can be suggested by the AI -powered platform
based on individual skills, interests, and goals. Employees are made to feel valued and
supported in their professional development as a result. Performance Management: An AI -
supported feedback system can provide employees with continuous, individualized feedback
that enables them to realize their full potential and pinpoint areas for improvement.
Employee Engagement: Artificial intelligence -powered chatbots and virtual assistants can
answer questions, address concerns, and offer support to employees, fostering a sense of
community and connection round-the-clock.
Review of literature
Albert Christopher (2019) states that AI -based applications boost employee productivity in
his article titled "Use of Artificial Intelligence in Human Resource Management." While
focusing on employee needs and outcomes, it can study, foresee, analyze, and develop a more
skilled store. Discretion, flair gap, care, integration skills, and a lack of proven applications
are just a limit of the difficulties. Finding trustworthy learning data sets, employing the
appropriate implementation strategy, seeking clarity, removing bia s, and considering
unintended consequences are all important aspects of the careful management of AI systems.
The paper "To Study the Impact of Artificial Intelligence on Human Resource Management"
by Prasanna Vatsa and Kusuma Gullamjji (2019) makes it ab undantly clear that the
integration of HR practices with applicants based on AI has unquestionably a stronger impact

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 22

on improving the performance of the organization. The study shows that AI is everywhere in
human resources—in recruitment, training, onboar ding, performance analysis, retention, and
other areas—but that many businesses are still lagging in integrating AI into HR practices
due to the cost of integration.
Jennifer Johansson and Senja Herranen (2019): In the paper titled "The Application of
Artificial Intelligence in Human Resource Management," it is mentioned that AI in
recruitment is a new field and that few organizations have implemented AI throughout the
recruitment process. It also mentions that the main advantages of AI are faster quality and the
elimination of routine tasks, while the biggest challenge is how well -prepared businesses are
for new technologies in general.
Objective of the study
 The purpose of this study is to determine how artificial intelligence affects HR
management.
 To determine how AI -based software helps companies hire the best workers in the
industry
 To comprehend how AI-based software affects recruiters' work
Hypothesis of the study
 H0: Artificial intelligence has no significant effect on human resource management.
 H1: Artificial intelligence has a significant impact on human resource management.
Methodology of the study
The specific procedures or techniques used to identify, select, process, and analyze
information on a few topics are known as research methodolog y. Experiments, participant
observation, secondary data, and a variety of qualitative and quantitative research methods
are utilized by sociologists. A comprehensive, in -depth description of observations, including
the context of events and circumstances, is the goal of qualitative methods. The data used in
this study were both primary and secondary. 19 complete questionnaires were used for data
analysis after the data's reliability and plausibility were evaluated. To draw a logical
conclusion, primary and secondary data were interpreted and analyzed. The questionnaire's
internal consistency and reliability were assessed using Cronbach's alpha. The data were then
subjected to multiple linear regression analysis. The purpose of this analysis was to ascertain
not only whether or not the impact of AI on HRM and HR practices was significant but also
whether or not the research model was successful in doing so.
Purpose of the study
This study aims to determine whether or not AI will have an impact on human resource
management in the near future or how it currently affects HRM. However, the growth of AI
has been made possible by the successful use of machine learning over the past two  decades.
This study is significant because AI has begun to engage employees today. It is adding

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 23

strategic value to the organization and assisting staff members in time management. By
automating tedious, time-consuming, mundane, and repetitive tasks, AI ha s the potential and
capability to solve numerous business issues and shift industry productivity. One of a
company's most important functions is human resources. Utilizing AI technology in HR may
yield benefits in the short and long term. Some have already  been observed, while others are
anticipated to be observed soon.
Limitation of the study
The fact that only a small number of recruitment professionals responded to the survey and
questionnaire was the primary limitation of this study. Only 19 professionals responded to the
150 Google forms sent to them. However, if the data gathered is of high quality, a response
rate of 20% is acceptable. When filling out the questionnaires, participants may make
mistakes. To combat this, the questionnaires' q uestions were straightforward and left little
room for misunderstanding.
Analysis and interpretation
The questionnaire data analysis and interpretation are discussed in this section. 75 people
were asked to fill out the survey. 19 responses were received a nd analyzed after the integrity
and dependability of the data were checked. The respondents' phase, job, and society are
investigated.

Table 5 depicts the results of the regression test. This is helpful in determining the
significance of AI and its impact on HR practices and management. It also demonstrates the
connection between HRM and AI. According to table 5, the fact that the sig val ues for all of
the independent variables are lower than the standard significance value of 0.05 (familiarity
with AI introduction – 0.005 and agreement of AI software helping in finding the best talent
for the job – 0.011) demonstrates that these factors a re significant and have a significant
effect on HRM. However, the other two factors have values greater than 0.05, indicating that
AI-based HR practice software is not currently utilized by their organizations. Table 5 also
shows that, with the exception o f the use of in -house software or third -party software, the
beta values for the factors are positive. This suggests that there is a positive connection
between these elements and HRM and HR Practices. Therefore, the hypothesis H1 that AI
has a positive and  significant impact on HRM can be accepted based on the conducted
analysis. Therefore, the H1 (Alternate) hypothesis is accepted, and the H0 (Null) hypothesis
is rejected, indicating that AI has a significant impact on HRM.
Artificial Intelligence in HRM: Opportunities, Challenges, and Ethical Considerations
Through innovation in robotics, which encompasses AI, Artificial Intelligence (AI) has
opened up tremendous opportunities in the workplace.
Challenges
 1. Fairness and Bias: AI algorithms may inherit a  bias from previous HR data in areas like
recruitment, performance evaluation, and promotion, which could result in biased decisions.
To ensure fairness and reduce bias, algorithms need to be carefully designed and constantly
monitored.
2. Privacy Concerns: AI systems that look at employee data raise privacy concerns about how
data is collected, stored, and used. Human resources departments must adhere to stringent
data protection laws like the General Data Protection Regulation to ensure transparency and
employee consent.
3. Skill  Gap and Reluctance to Change: HR professionals must be trained in how to use
artificial intelligence tools and analyze their results in order for AI to be implemented in HR

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 27

management. Employees who are accustomed to traditional human resources procedures may
also find it challenging to resist change.
4. Overreliance on Technology: A decision-making reliance on artificial intelligence (AI) that
is insufficient may result in a lack of human judgment and intuition, which may overlook
crucial contextual factors that AI algorithms may miss.
5. Job  Displacement and Ethical Employment Practices: For certain roles, automation of
human resources functions may result in job loss.  Concerns have been raised regarding the
retraining or redeployment of affected employees and the adoption of AI in accordanc e with
ethical employment practices.
Opportunities
1. Efficient  Recruitment: AI has the potential to simplify the recruitment process by
analyzing resumes, locating potential candidates, and even conducting initial interviews with
chatbots or virtual assistants.
2. Data-Driven Decision Making: AI-powered analytics can process a large amount of human
resource data to improve workforce planning, talent acquisition, and retention decisions.
3. Personalized Learning and Development: AI algorithms can create i ndividualized learning
paths for employees based on their skills, preferences, and performance data to improve
training effectiveness.
4. Employee  Engagement: Chatbots and sentiment analysis tools can be used to measure
employee satisfaction and engagemen t, allowing HR to immediately address morale -
threatening issues.
5. Automated  Administrative Tasks: AI -driven tools can automate routine administrative
tasks like payroll processing, leave management, and performance evaluations, allowing
human resources professionals to concentrate on strategic initiatives.
Ethical Considerations
1. Transparency: AI algorithms should be transparent, providing clear explanations of how
decisions are made, particularly in recruitment and performance evaluations.
2. Account ability: To ensure that AI is utilized with appropriate caution in HRM by
establishing accountability mechanisms and addressing any unintended consequences or
biases.
3. Fairness: Ensuring that AI-driven decisions are equitable and fair by regularly audit ing the
algorithms, correcting biases, and giving affected people ways to appeal or get help.
 4. Data  Privacy: Employees' right to privacy must be respected by enforcing robust data
protection measures, obtaining informed consent, and limiting data collec tion for relevant
purposes.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 28

5. Enhancing employee well-being and ensuring that AI initiatives improve rather than hinder
employee satisfaction, engagement, and overall professional experience.
Conclusion
Based on the findings of this study, researchers would like to suggest that businesses come up
with a straightforward organizational strategy to incorporate AI into their recruitment
process. The researcher would like to suggest that intelligent AI technology should be used to
replace simple and time -consuming tasks for recruiters. However, the real source of concern
for the global workforce is the manner in which AI demonstrates its ability to reduce
employment in a variety of sectors worldwide. But the truth is that advanced technologies
aren't what will replace humans; rather, it's more about how humans can adapt to and make
use of these advances to increase wealth and prosperity. In a real sense, AI -based functions
would affect a certain percentage of workers. Leaders in HR and businesses need to think
about what their employees need and what the future holds for them. In addition, based on
our research, the majority of businesses successfully incorporate AI -related methods into
recruitment. However, AI will soon be used everywhere in human resources, inc luding
recruitment, training, onboarding, performance analysis, and retention, among other areas.
However, the majority of businesses are still lagging behind in incorporating AI into their HR
activities due to the costs associated with integration. In con clusion, the application of AI
ought to be viewed as a positive opportunity due to the fact that AI enhances life and
contributes to a better future if it is clearly understood and utilized appropriately.

GROWTH OF DIGITAL PAYMENTS IN INDIA- A TREND ANALYSIS
MS. S. Padmavathy
 Assistant professor
 Department of Corporate Secretaryship
 Sriram College of Arts and Science.
ABSTRACT
Digital payments have become an integral part of India's financial system and commercia l
activity. The transition from cash -based transactions to electronic modes has accelerated
through the expansion of mobile banking, internet banking, cards, Immediate Payment
Service (IMPS), National Electronic Funds Transfer (NEFT), Real Time Gross Settl ement
(RTGS), and particularly the Unified Payments Interface (UPI). This study examines the
growth of digital payments in India through secondary data obtained from official sources
such as the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), National Payments Corporation of  India (NPCI),
and Government of India publications.  The study is descriptive and analytical in nature. It
examines changes in transaction volume and value, identifies the contribution of UPI, and
discusses the factors supporting digital -payment expansion.  Official data indicate a
substantial increase in digital payment activity over the study period. Total digital payment
transactions rose from 4,370.68 crore in 2020 –21 to 16,443.02 crore in 2023 –24.
Government releases also report continued expansion during 2024–25. UPI has emerged as
a major retail -payment channel, supported by interoperability, QR -based acceptance,
smartphone usage, fintech innovation, and policy initiatives.  The study finds that digital
payment growth has strengthened convenience, speed , accessibility and formalization of
financial transactions. At the same time, cybersecurity, fraud, digital literacy, connectivity,
privacy and consumer protection remain important concerns. The paper concludes that
continued investment in secure infrastr ucture and inclusive digital -finance education is
necessary for sustainable growth.
KEYWORDSDigital Payments; UPI; Digital Banking; FinTech; Financial Inclusion; E -
Commerce; India; Trend Analysis; Secondary Data.
INTRODUCTION
The payment system is a fundam ental part of every economy because it facilitates the
exchange of goods, services and financial assets. Traditionally, cash and paper -based
instruments played a major role in India's payment environment. Technological
development, banking reforms, mobile connectivity and policy initiatives have
progressively changed this structure. Digital payment refers to the electronic transfer of
money from one person, account or institution to another without the physical exchange of
currency. Digital payment instrume nts in India include UPI, IMPS, NEFT, RTGS, debit
cards, credit cards, prepaid instruments and mobile -based payment applications.  The
growth of digital payments has been particularly visible in retail transactions. Consumers
can make payments through mobil e phones using bank applications or UPI -enabled
applications. Merchants can receive payments through QR codes without maintaining

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 30

sophisticated point -of-sale infrastructure. This has helped digital payments spread across
organized and small retail business es. UPI is especially significant because it enables
interoperable, account-to-account transactions through a common platform. It has reduced
friction in retail payments and has been adopted by individuals, merchants and service
providers. Its growth has also encouraged innovation in fintech services.
BACKGROUND OF DIGITAL PAYMENTS IN INDIA
India's digital payment ecosystem developed through several stages. Electronic clearing
systems and banking technology created the foundation for modern payment infrastr ucture.
NEFT and RTGS supported electronic bank transfers, while IMPS enabled faster interbank
transfers. The introduction of UPI subsequently created a unified architecture for convenient
retail payments. The expansion of Aadhaar -linked services, bank -account penetration,
smartphones, affordable data services and digital financial literacy further supported
adoption. Policy initiatives promoting digital transactions also increased public awareness
and merchant acceptance. The COVID -19 period accelerated th e use of contactless and
remote payment methods. However, the long -term growth of digital payments cannot be
attributed to one event alone; it reflects continuing technological, institutional and behavioral
changes.
STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM
The rapid expan sion of digital payments has transformed the way consumers, businesses
and institutions conduct financial transactions. While transaction data demonstrate strong
growth, it is necessary to systematically examine the magnitude and direction of this
growth u sing reliable secondary information. A trend analysis can show whether
transaction volume and value have increased consistently and can identify the growing
importance of UPI. The study therefore focuses on national -level digital -payment trends
rather than individual consumer attitudes. It uses official statistical sources to describe the
growth pattern and the factors and challenges associated with the digital -payment
ecosystem.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
1. Primary objectives
 To examine the growth of digital payment transactions in India.
 To analyze changes in the volume and value of digital payments over the selected
period.
2. Secondary objectives
 To study the growth and significance of UPI in retail digital payments.
 To identify major factors responsible for the expansion of digital payment
services.
 To examine challenges associated with digital payment adoption.
 To suggest measures for secure, inclusive and sustainable digital payment growth.

RESEARCH QUESTIONS
 What has been the trend in digital payment transaction volume in India?
 How has digital payment transaction value changed over the study period?
 What role has UPI played in the expansion of retail digital payments?
 What major technological and institutional factors have supported digital -payment
growth?
 What challenges may affect the continued expansion of digital payments?
SCOPE OF THE STUDY
The study covers India at the national level. The primary period considered is 2020 –21 to
2024–25 for selected aggregate indicators. Recently UPI indicators were d iscussed
separately where official information is available. The study does not attempt to estimate
individual-level adoption or consumer satisfaction.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
 Research Design
The study adopts a descriptive and analytical research design. It describes the development
of digital payments and analyses trends in selected numerical indicators.
  Nature of Data
Only secondary data are used. Secondary data is information already collected and
published by recognized institutions. For this study, emp hasis is placed on RBI
publications, NPCI statistics and Government of India releases.
  Period of Study
The principal trend period is 2020 –21 to 2024 –25. Because some recent official releases
provide figures only up to a particular month, such observation s are clearly identified as
provisional or partial-period figures.
Tools of Analysis
The study uses percentage growth, year -wise comparison, tabular presentation and
descriptive interpretation. The basic growth formula used is:
Percentage Growth = [(Curren t Year Value − Previous Year Value) / Previous Year
Value] × 100
SOURCES OF DATA
 Reserve Bank of India – Annual Reports and payment-system statistics.
 National Payments Corporation of India – UPI Product Statistics.
 Press Information Bureau – Ministry of Finance, Government of India.

 Official publications and reports relating to India's digital payment ecosystem.

REVIEW OF LITERATURE
Haque, Azeez and Akhtar (2025) examined UPI and financial inclusion in rural India
using primary data from rural areas of Aligarh district.
Their study found that factors including age, gender, education, occupation and income were
significant predictors of UPI usage. The study demonst rates that the growth of digital
payments is influenced not only by technological availability but also by socio -economic
characteristics.
Khurana, Ramesh Chandra, Janardhana Rao, Vijayakumar Thota, Raghu Ram Kaipa
and Venkata Ramana (2025)  investigated t he relationship between fintech adoption,
digital payments and financial inclusion in India using state -level panel data for 2015–2024.
Their study focused particularly on UPI adoption and banking access, highlighting the
importance of fintech in expanding financial inclusion.
Smitha K. S., Ranjitha K. B., Shreenidhi H. M. and Ankitha K. V. (2026)  examined the
growth of UPI-based digital payments and financial inclusion in India using secondary data
from RBI and NPCI for 2016 –2025. Through trend analysis, the study reported substantial
growth in UPI transactions and discussed its contribution to financial access in both rural
and urban areas.
Motheram and Buteau (2026 ) investigated the supply -side factors affecting digital
payment adoption across 711 India n districts during 2020 –2024. The study examined the
roles of merchant-network development and digital infrastructure within the UPI ecosystem,
providing evidence that supply -side conditions are important for the expansion of digital
payments.
Rao and Upa dhyaya (2026)  studied UPI payment -app acceptance among small -scale
merchants in India. Their research focuses on the role of digital public infrastructure and
merchant adoption, particularly in the context of financial inclusion. This adds a merchant -
side perspective to literature, complementing studies that primarily examine consumers.
Jaiswal and Maurya (2026) examined digital payments and financial inclusion with
particular reference to the preparedness of cooperative banks in the UPI era. Using
secondary data from sources including RBI, NABARD, the Ministry of Finance and the
World Bank, they highlighted technological integration, cybersecurity awareness and
institutional capacity as important issues for sustainable digital payment expansion.

INTERPRETATION OF TRANSACTION VOLUME
The table indicates a clear upward movement in transaction volume from 4,370.68 crore in
2020–21 to 16,443 .02 crore in 2023 –24. The annual growth rate was particularly high in
2021–22 and remained substantial in 2022–23 and 2023–24.
The growth in volume reflects increasing consumer familiarity with electronic payments,
expansion of merchant acceptance, growth of mobile applications, and improved
interoperability. It also reflects the increasing use of digital payments for small -value
everyday transactions.  Transaction value also increased between 2020 –21 and 2023 –24.
However, volume and value need not grow at i dentical rates because the average value of
individual transactions can change over time. Therefore, a comprehensive assessment should
consider both measures.
GROWTH OF UPI IN INDIA
UPI is one of the most important developments in India's retail digital pa yment system. It
provides an interoperable framework through which users can initiate bank-account payments
using mobile applications. Its design supports person -to-person and person -to-merchant
transactions and has enabled rapid expansion of QR-based payments.

Government of India information released in 2026 reported that UPI transaction volume
