EMPIRICAL STUDY ON AI GOVERNANCE IN COMMERCE: BALANCING
INNOVATION WITH FAIRNESS AND TRANSPARENCY
Mrs. K. Seethalakshmi
 Assistant Professor
 Department of commerce
 Christ Arts and Science College, kilachery
Abstract
Artificial intelligence (AI) is transforming commerce, economics, and management by
automating decisions, personalizing services, and optimizing operations, yet its rapid
diffusion amplifies concerns about fairness, privacy, transparency, and accountability. Across
sectors, researchers argue that responsible AI cannot be reduced to technical fixes; it requires
integrated ethical and governance frameworks that link algorithm design to corporate
governance, regulation, and social responsibility. Recent work identifies core dimensions of
AI governance accountability, transparency and explainability, fairness and inclusiveness,
privacy and safety, and risk and cybersecurity management a nd emphasizes that developers,
managers, and policymakers all share ethical duties toward consumers and wider stakeholders
1346. Studies highlight how biased data, opaque models, and weak oversight can entrench
discrimination and erode trust, especially in  data-intensive business analytics and financial
decision-making, reinforcing the need for fairness audits, explainable AI, bias mitigation and
continuous monitoring. Comparative analyses of national and corporate AI strategies show
diverse approaches to a llocating responsibility between states, firms, and self -regulatory
initiatives, but converge on the view that AI governance must be adaptive, participatory, and
risk based . Overall, literature frames responsible AI in business as a multidimensional
challenge at the intersection of technology, law, and organizational ethics, calling for
governance models that embed ethical principles into the full AI lifestyle and corporate
decision structures to protect well-being and sustain public trust.
Keywords: Artificial intelligence governance; Responsible AI; Fairness; Transparency;
Privacy; Accountability; Corporate governance; Business ethics; Algorithmic bias; Data
governance; Corporate social responsibility (CSR)
Introduction
AI Governance and Commercial Innovation: Research consistently shows that AI adoption in
commerce enhances efficiency, personalization, and predictive analytics. Studies by
Brynjolfsson & McAfee (2021) argue that AI -driven innovation accelerates competitiv e
advantage, yet governance frameworks are often underdeveloped. Scholars highlight that
unchecked innovation risks amplifying algorithmic bias and undermining consumer trust.
Fairness in Algorithmic Decision -Making: Fairness has emerged as a central theme  in AI
governance. Barocas, Hardt, & Narayanan (2019) emphasize that biased training data can
perpetuate discrimination in lending, hiring, and marketing. Empirical studies in financial
services reveal that fairness audits and bias mitigation strategies ar e critical to ensuring
equitable outcomes. Transparency and Explainability in Business AI: Transparency is widely

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 71

recognized as essential for accountability. Doshi -Velez & Kim (2017) argue that explainable
AI (XAI) enables stakeholders to understand and ch allenge algorithmic decisions. In
commerce, explainability is linked to consumer confidence, regulatory compliance, and
ethical corporate governance. Comparative analyses show that firms adopting interpretable
models report higher stakeholder trust.
Objectives of study
 To analyze the role of AI governance in fostering commercial innovation
Assess how governance structures influence the adoption of AI technologies in business
operations, customer engagement, and financial decision-making.
 To evaluate fairness in algorithmic decision-making within commerce
Investigate how biased data and opaque models affect consumer outcomes and identify
mechanisms for fairness audits and bias mitigation.
 To examine transparency and explainability in AI systems used by businesses
Explore the impact of explainable AI (XAI) on consumer trust, regulatory compliance, and
corporate accountability.
 To assess the integration of ethical principles into corporate governance frameworks
Study how firms embed AI ethics into corpora te social responsibility (CSR) and decision -
making structures.
 To compare national and corporate AI governance strategies
Analyze diverse approaches to responsibility allocation between governments, firms, and
self-regulatory initiatives, highlighting best  practices for adaptive and participatory
governance.
Scope of the study
Sectoral Coverage : The research examines AI applications in commerce, including retail,
financial services, e -commerce, and data -driven business analytics. It does not extend to
purely technological domains such as robotics or autonomous vehicles.
Governance Dimensions : The study is limited to five key dimensions of AI governance
fairness, transparency and explainability, accountability, privacy and safety, and risk
management. Other technical aspects such as algorithmic optimization or hardware design are
excluded.
Geographical Focus : Comparative insights are drawn from national and corporate AI
strategies across major economies (e.g., EU, U.S., and Asia). The analysis remains at a
regional and corporate level rather than global or individual consumer perspectives.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 72

Stakeholder Involvement : The scope includes the roles of developers, managers,
policymakers, and corporate boards in embedding ethical principles into AI systems. It
excludes consumer-level adoption studies or purely technical developer guides.
Methodological Boundaries: The study integrates qualitative literature review, comparative
policy analysis, and conceptual frameworks. It does not involve primary data collection s uch
as surveys or experiments, though it highlights empirical findings from existing research.
Limitations: The research does not attempt to design new algorithms or propose technical
fixes. Instead, it focuses on governance models, ethical integration, an d organizational
practices that sustain trust and fairness in AI-driven commerce.
Key Ethical Principles and Governance Frameworks
Review of Literature
Meta-analyses of global AI ethics guidelines show convergence around transparency, justice
and fairness,  non-maleficence, responsibility, and privacy, though implementation varies
widely (Shaban & Omoush, 2025; Birkstedt et al., 2023). Broader trustworthy/Responsible
AI frameworks extend these to seven requirements: human agency and oversight, robustness
and safety, privacy and data governance, transparency, diversity and fairness, societal and
environmental wellbeing, and accountability (Madanchian & Taherdoost, 2025; Rodríguez et
al., 2023; Lu et al., 2022).Corporate and intergovernmental AI governance fram eworks
operationalize these principles through accountability, explainability, inclusiveness, privacy
and security, and cyber -risk prevention, emphasizing that developers and maintainers of
AIcarryethical responsibilities toward consumers and society(Corrê a et al., 2022; Birkstedt et
al., 2023; Camilleri, 2023; Madanchian& Taherdoost, 2025; Rodríguez et al., 2023; Lu et al.,
2022).
Organizational AI Governance
Systematic reviews conceptualize AI governance (AIG) as a system of rules, practices, and
processes aligning AI use with organizational strategies, legal requirements, ethical
principles, and stakeholder expectations (Camilleri, 2023).
Four recurring themes are:
• Technology (robustness, explainability)
• Stakeholders and context (multiple affected parties, sector specifics)
• Regulation (soft law, hard law, risk-based regimes)
• Processes (oversight units, audits, life-cycle governance)
Analysis and Discussion
AI is driving innovation in commerce through rationalization, automation, and predictive
analytics, but unchecked growth raises ethical concerns. Fairness is a major issue, as biased

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 73

data can lead to discrimination in lending, hiring, and marketing. Transparency and ability to
explain are equally vital firms  that adopt interpretable models to build stronger consumer
trust and meet regulatory demands.  Corporate governance plays a key role in embedding
ethics into AI systems, ensuring responsibility lies not only with developers but also with
executives and boards. Comparative studies show varie d governance approaches: the EU
Favors risk-based regulation, the U.S. leans on self -regulation, and Asian economies adopt
hybrid models. Despite differences, all converge on the need for adaptive, participatory, and
risk-sensitive frameworks. Overall, the discussion highlights that responsible AI governance
is not a barrier to innovation but a foundation for sustainable progress, balancing efficiency
with fairness and transparency to protect trust and well being.
 Findings
 AI drives efficiency and persona lization but requires governance to prevent bias and loss
of trust.
 Fairness challenges persist, with biased data leading to discrimination in commerce.
 Transparency and explainability strengthen accountability and consumer confidence.
 Corporate governance and CSR are essential for embedding ethical oversight.
 Governance strategies differ across regions but converge on adaptive, participatory
models.
 Responsible AI is a foundation for sustainable innovation, balancing progress with
fairness and transparency.
Recommendations
 Integrate ethics into all stages of AI development and use.
 Conduct fairness audits regularly to detect and reduce bias.
 Adopt explainable AI to improve trust and accountability.
 Strengthen corporate oversight, embedding AI ethics into CSR.
 Use adaptive governance models that evolve with technology.
 Encourage collaboration among firms, regulators, and stakeholders.
 Ensure transparency in data governance to protect privacy and security.
 Promote global standards alignment for consistent ethical safeguards.
Conclusion
Artificial intelligence is transforming commerce by driving efficiency, personalization, and
innovation, but its rapid adoption raises concerns about fairness, transparency, and
accountability. Biased data and opaque models risk discrimination, while explainable AI and
fairness audits strengthen trust and compliance. Corporate governance and CSR frameworks
are essential to embed ethics into AI systems, ensuring responsibility lies with both technical
teams and executives. Comparative analyses show varied governance approaches across
regions, yet all converge on adaptive, participatory, and risk -based models. Overall,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 74

responsible AI governance is not a barrier but a foundation for sustainable innovation,
balancing progress with ethical responsibility to protect consumer trust and societal well-
being.

EMPIRICAL STUDY ON DIGITAL-FIRST MARKETING: FROM INFLUENCERS
TO AI DRIVEN BANKING.
Dr. K. Merina Roseline
 Assistant Professor & Head of the Department Commerce
 Christ Arts and Science College, kilachery
Abstract
Digital-first marketing has transformed the banking sector by moving customer engagement
from traditional branch -based communication toward social media, mobile platforms,
influences, personalized digital campaigns, chatbots and artificial intelligence (AI ). This
empirical study examines the relationship between influence marketing, digital engagement,
trust, AI -enabled banking services and customer adoption intention. The study proposes a
structured survey -based methodology targeting retail banking custome rs who actively use
digital banking channels. A conceptual framework is developed in which influence credibility
and digital marketing engagement influence perceived usefulness, trust and customer
experience, while AI -driven banking service quality contrib utes to adoption intention and
customer loyalty. The proposed empirical model can be tested using descriptive statistics,
reliability analysis, correlation, multiple regression and structural equation modelling. The
paper also discusses privacy, transparen cy, algorithmic bias and cybersquatting as important
issues in AI -driven banking. The study provides a practical framework for banks seeking to
integrate human -led influences communication with AI -based rationalization and service
delivery.
Keywords: Digital-first marketing; Influence marketing; Artificial intelligence; AI -driven
banking; Digital banking; Customer trust; Customer engagement; Customer experience;
Fintech; Banking adoption.
 Introduction
The rapid growth of smartphones, social media, mobile b anking and artificial intelligence has
changed the way financial institutions communicate with customers. Banking customers
increasingly discover financial products through digital platforms rather than relying
exclusively on branch visits, printed adverti sements or conventional sales channels. This
transition has created a digital -first marketing environment in which banks use social media,
search, mobile applications, influences, personalized content and data -driven communication
to reach customers  Influence marketing represents the human side of digital -first
communication. Influences can create awareness and explain financial products in a language
and format that is familiar to their followers. At the same time, AI -driven banking represents
the technolo gy side of the transformation. AI can support personalized recommendations,
conversational banking, fraud detection, customer service and automated decision support.
The combination of influences -led communication and AI -enabled service delivery creates a
new customer journey: awareness may begin with an influence, evaluation may occur
through digital content, and the final service experience may be delivered through an AI -
enabled banking platform.  Despite the rapid adoption of digital banking, customer tru st

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 76

remains important. Banking customers share sensitive financial information and therefore
may be concerned about privacy, security, transparency and the use of personal data.
Influence credibility may reduce uncertainty at the communication stage, while reliable AI-
driven services may influence trust and satisfaction after adoption. Therefore, examining
these variables together is useful for understanding digital -first marketing in contemporary
banking.
Statement of the Problem
Banks are investing heavily  in digital marketing and AI -enabled services, but customer
responses are not determined by technology alone. Customers may engage with a campaign
but still avoid adopting a banking service if they do not trust the institution, the influences or
the technology. Similarly, an AI-enabled application may be technically efficient but may not
produce a positive customer experience if its communication is unclear or impersonal. There
is therefore a need for an empirical framework that examines influencer credibil ity, digital
engagement, trust, AI service quality and adoption intention within one model.
Need for the Study
 To understand how customers respond to influencer-based financial communication.
 To examine whether digital marketing engagement is associated wi th customer trust and
adoption intention.
 To study the role of AI-driven banking service quality in customer experience.
 To identify the importance of privacy, transparency and perceived security in digital
banking.
 To provide practical guidance for banks integrating human-centered marketing with AI -
enabled services.
 Objectives of the Study
1. To examine the influence of the influencer credibility on customer trust.
2. To analyze the relationship between digital marketing engagement and customer adoption
intention.
3. To examine the effect of perceived usefulness of AI -driven banking services on adoption
intention.
4. To determine the relationship between AI service quality and customer satisfaction.
5. To study the role of customer trust in digital banking adoption.
6. To develop an empirical model linking influencer marketing and AI-driven banking.
 Research Questions
 Does influencer credibility significantly influence customer trust in digital banking
communication?
 Does digital marketing engagement influence customers' intention to adopt digital
banking services?
 Does perceived usefulness of AI-driven banking influence adoption intention?
 Does AI service quality influence customer satisfaction?

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 77

 Does customer trust mediate the relationship between digital marketing activities and
adoption intention?
Review of Literature
1. Vrontis, D., Makrides, A., Christofi, M., & Thrassou, A. (2021) examined social media
influencer marketing in Interna tional Journal of Consumer Studies, 45(4), 617 –644. Their
systematic review of 68 articles found that influencer credibility, engagement and
relationships with followers play an important role in shaping consumer attitudes and
behavior.
2. Ameen, N., Tarh ini, A., Reppel, A., & Anand, A. (2022) studied AI in customer -facing
financial services in the International Journal of Bank Marketing, 40(6), 1299 –1336. Their
review of 90 studies identified AI adoption, consumer behavior and technological
performance as major research areas and highlighted the need for more empirical research on
consumers, ethics and regulation.
3. Kalyani, S., & Gupta, N. (2023) examined AI and machine learning applications in
banking in Discover Artificial Intelligence, 3, Article 41.  Their systematic review analyzed
734 articles and highlighted how AI and ML contribute to digital banking, customer
convenience and trust, while also creating challenges related to cybersecurity and digital
risks.
4. Sardar, S., & Vijay, T. S. (2025) rev iewed social media influencers and purchase intention
in International Journal of Consumer Studies, 49(3), e70046. Their systematic review of 76
articles identified important research themes concerning influencers, consumer purchase
intention and the factors influencing consumer responses to influencer marketing.
 Research Methodology
Research Design: The study adopts descriptive and analytical research design.  Using
secondary data obtained from various journals and literatures.
Discussion
The proposed model treats digital-first banking as a customer journey rather than as a single
marketing activity. Influencers can create awareness and social proof, while bank -owned
digital channels provide detailed information and service access. AI then becomes part of the
service experience through chatbots, personalization and automated assistance. Trust
connects these stages because customers must trust both the communication source and the
banking platform.
For banking institutions, the findings from an actual survey  can help identify which aspects
of digital-first marketing require improvement. If influencer credibility is strongly associated
with trust, banks may focus on transparent partnerships and financially responsible content. If
AI service quality is strongly  associated with satisfaction, banks may prioritize response
accuracy, personalization and seamless escalation to human employees. If perceived security

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 78

is strongly associated with trust, communication about privacy and cybersquatting may
require greater emphasis.
Managerial Implications
 Banks can integrate credible financial influencers with education rather than purely
promotional content.
 Influencer partnerships should include clear disclosure and responsible financial
communication.
 AI-based customer service should provide accurate, understandable and timely responses.
 Banks should provide clear information about privacy, data use and security.
 Human support should remain accessible when AI systems cannot resolve complex
customer issues.
 Marketing and technology teams should jointly monitor customer experience across the
complete digital journey.
Ethical Considerations
Digital-first financial marketing requires careful attention to consumer protection. Influencers
should clearly disclose paid or sponsor ed relationships. Banks should avoid misleading
claims and ensure that financial information is understandable. AI systems should be
monitored for privacy risks, unfair outcomes, security vulnerabilities and inappropriate
recommendations. Customer data sho uld be handled according to applicable laws and
institutional policies. Survey respondents should participate voluntarily, and their responses
should be kept confidential.
Limitations
 The study may rely on self-reported responses, which can introduce response bias.
 A non-probability sample may limit generalization to the wider banking population.
 Digital banking technologies change rapidly, so findings may vary over time.
 Influencer exposure differs across social-media platforms and age groups.
 The pro posed paper requires primary survey data before statistical findings can be
reported as empirical results.
 Conclusion
Digital-first marketing is reshaping banking by connecting social influence, digital
communication and AI-enabled service delivery. Influencers can contribute to awareness and
perceived credibility, while AI can provide personalization, convenience and automated
customer support. Trust, perceived security, usefulness and service quality are therefore

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 79

central to the customer journey. The pro posed empirical framework provides a structured
method for examining these relationships through primary data. Future research can extend
the model by comparing age groups, banking segments, social -media platforms and different
types of AI-enabled financial services.
 References

Decision Processes, 50(2), 179–211.

information technology. MIS Quarterly, 13(3), 319–340.

information technology: Toward a unified view. MIS Quarterly, 27(3), 425–478.

An integrated model. MIS Quarterly, 27(1), 51–90.
5. Lou, C., & Yuan, S. (2019). Influencer marketing: How message value and credibility
affect consumer trust of branded content on social media. Journal of Interactive
Advertising, 19(1), 58–73.
6. Kaplan, A. M., & Haenlein, M. (2010). Users of the world, unite! The challenges and
opportunities of social media. Business Horizons, 53(1), 59–68.

AI-POWERED FRAUD DETECTION IN BANKING AND FINANCIAL SERVICES

Nisha. S
I M. Com (General), Pg Department of Commerce (General)
 Valliammal College for Women, (Affiliated to the University of Madras) Chennai, Tamil
Nadu, India

T. R. Vijayalakshmi, M.Com., M.Phil., Set., Ph.D.,
Assistant Professor, PG Department of Commerce (General),
 Valliammal College for Women,
(Affiliated To the University of Madras), Chennai, Tamil Nadu, India

ABSTRACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has emerged as a powerful tool for combating the growing threat
of fraud in banking and financial services. Wit h the rapid adoption of digital banking, mobile
payments, online transactions, and automated financial services, fraudulent activities have
become more sophisticated and difficult to detect using traditional rule -based systems. This
paper explores the role of AI-powered fraud detection in identifying and preventing financial
fraud through advanced technologies such as machine learning, deep learning, anomaly
detection, and predictive analytics. AI systems can analyze large volumes of transactional
data in real time, identify unusual patterns, detect suspicious behavior, and generate timely
alerts, thereby enabling financial institutions to respond quickly to potential threats. The
study also highlights the effectivene ss of AI in reducing false positives, improving fraud
detection accuracy, strengthening risk management, and enhancing customer trust and
security. At the same time, challenges such as data privacy, model transparency,
cybersecurity risks, biased datasets,  and the need for continuous model improvement are
examined. The paper concludes that responsible adoption of AI can significantly strengthen
fraud prevention and create a more secure, efficient, and resilient financial ecosystem.
Keywords: Artificial Inte lligence, Fraud Detection, Machine Learning, Banking, Financial
Services, Anomaly Detection, Predictive Analytics, Cybersecurity
INTRODUCTION
Traditional forms of financial fraud have increasingly been accompanied by digital and
internet-based offences, cr eating new challenges for banks, financial institutions, regulators
and customers. The expansion of digital payments, online banking, mobile applications and
technology-enabled financial services has increased the speed, scale and complexity at which
fraudulent activities can occur. Consequently, financial institutions are required to identify
suspicious activities more rapidly and continuously adapt their detection mechanisms to
changing patterns of financial fraud.  Artificial Intelligence (AI) has emerged  as an important
technological approach to addressing these challenges. AI and machine -learning techniques
are increasingly applied to fraud detection, risk assessment, transaction monitoring and other

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 81

financial services. AI -based systems can analyze large volumes of transactions, identify
unusual patterns and support real -time monitoring. Unlike conventional rule -based systems,
AI can learn from historical and behavioral data and assist in identifying complex
relationships that may not be easily detected t hrough predefined rules. The increasing use of
AI in fraud detection is also connected with the wider digital transformation of banking and
financial services. While AI can strengthen security and improve operational efficiency, its
use raises concerns rel ating to data privacy, model transparency, cybersecurity, biased
datasets, false positives and accountability. Therefore, the effectiveness of AI -powered fraud
detection depends not only on technological capability but also on responsible
implementation an d continuous monitoring.  Against this background, the present study
examines the role of AI-powered fraud detection in banking and financial services. It focuses
on how Artificial Intelligence, machine learning, anomaly detection and predictive analytics
can support fraud prevention while strengthening risk management, customer security and
trust.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
● To examine the changing nature of financial fraud in banking and financial services.
● To analyze the role and effectiveness of Artificial Intelligence in detecting and preventing
financial fraud.
● To examine the major AI technologies used for fraud detection, including machine
learning, deep learning, anomaly detection and predictive analytics.
● To identify the benefits and challenges associated with AI-powered fraud detection.
● To understand the importance of responsible AI adoption for strengthening customer
security and trust.

SCOPE OF THE STUDY
● The study focuses on the application of Artificial Intelligence in fraud detection within
banking and financial services. It covers digital banking transactions, online payments,
card transactions, account activities and other technology -enabled financial services
where suspicious behavior can be identified through data analysis. The study considers
the role of machine learning, deep learning, anomaly detection and predictive analytics in
detecting unusual patterns and supporting timely responses.
● The study also considers the broader issues associated with AI adoption, including data
privacy, model transparency, false positives, cybersecurity and the need for continuous
model improvement. The discussion is based on secondary information and is intended to
provide an academic overview of the role of AI in strengthening financial fraud
prevention.

IMPORTANCE OF THE STUDY
● The growth of digital banking and online financial transactions has created greater
opportunities for customers and financial institutions, but it has also increased exposure to

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 82

sophisticated fraudulent activities. Traditional systems that depend mainly on predefined
rules may not always identify new or rapidly changing fraud patterns. AI -powered
systems provide an additional layer of security by analyzing large volumes of data and
identifying unusual behavior in real time.
● The study is important because effective fraud detection can reduce financial losses,
improve risk management and protect customer information. AI can also help financial
institutions reduce the number of unnecessary alerts by identifying patterns more
accurately. At the same time, understanding the limitations of AI is essential because
incorrect decisions, privacy concerns or unexplained restrictions may affect customer
confidence.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE
● Moura, Barcaui and Payer (2025) examined trends in AI and financial fraud prevention
through a bibliometric review of peer -reviewed research. The study indicated substantial
growth in research on AI -based fraud prevention and highlighted the increasing
importance of intelligent technologies in financial security.
● Chen et al. (2025) reviewed deep learning applications in financial fraud detection and
highlighted the ability of deep learning methods to identify complex patterns. The study
also identified challenges involving imbalanced datasets, explainability and data privacy.
● Çil and Yildiz (2025) examined Explainable Artificial Intelligence in the financial sector
and highlighted the importance of transparency and interpretability in applications such as
fraud detection and financial decision-making.
● Fundira and Mbohwa (2025) analyzed ethical issues relating to AI in banking and
identified data privacy, algorithmic bias, transparency, fairness and regulatory oversight
as important concerns.
● Yaseen and Al -Amarneh (2025) examined the adoption of AI -driven fraud detection
among financial institutions in the United Arab Emirates and Qatar, considering trust,
transparency and perceived fairness.
● Zafar and Wu (2026) reviewed Explainabl e AI for fraud detection and highlighted
difficulties in balancing model complexity, explainability and highly imbalanced fraud
datasets.
● Khoozani, Seera and Lim (2026) reviewed graph learning and Explainable AI in credit
risk and fraud detection. Their findings indicate that graph-based approaches can improve
modelling of relationships among financial entities, while XAI can support
interpretability and auditability.

RESEARCH GAP
● Existing literature demonstrates that Artificial Intelligence has strengthened financial
fraud detection through machine learning, deep learning, real -time analytics and other
computational techniques. However, much of the existing research concentrates on
detection accuracy, model performance and technological efficienc y. Increasing attention
is now being given to explainability, transparency, privacy and ethical concerns.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 83

● Therefore, the research gap lies in understanding how AI -powered fraud detection can be
used effectively in banking and financial services while also maintaining responsible data
use, transparency, human oversight and customer confidence. The present study brings
these dimensions together in the context of fraud detection.

RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
● This study adopts a qualitative and analytical research app roach based on secondary data.
Information has been considered from peer-reviewed journal articles, systematic literature
reviews, academic publications, regulatory reports and other credible sources relating to
Artificial Intelligence, financial fraud detection and banking security.
● The study examines four broad dimensions: the changing nature of financial fraud, the
effectiveness of AI-based detection methods, the challenges associated with AI adoption,
and the implications of AI -powered fraud detection f or financial institutions and
customers.

CONCEPT AND IMPORTANCE OF AI-POWERED FRAUD DETECTION
● AI-powered fraud detection refers to the use of Artificial Intelligence techniques to
identify transactions, accounting activities or behavioral patterns that may indicate
fraudulent activity. These systems process large volumes of financial data and compare
current behavior with historical or learned patterns. When unusual behavior is identified,
the system can generate alerts for further investigation.
● The importance of AI -powered fraud detection has increased because digital financial
services generate large quantities of transactional data every day. AI can support
continuous monitoring and assist institutions in identifying suspicious activiti es faster
than manual processes. It can also support the classification of transactions according to
risk and help investigators focus on activities requiring immediate attention.
MAJOR TYPES OF FRAUD DETECTED USING AI
• Card and Payment Fraud – AI can identify unusual payment amounts, transaction locations,
frequencies and spending patterns.
• Account Takeover – Behavioral analysis can help identify suspicious login activity, device
changes and unusual account access.
• Identity and Application Fraud – AI can support the identification of inconsistent identity
information and unusual application behavior.
• Online Banking Fraud – Transaction monitoring can identify abnormal transfers, login
patterns and suspicious digital activity.
• Social Engineering a nd Suspicious Transactions – Behavioral and transactional signals can
be combined to identify activities that differ significantly from normal customer patterns.

AI TECHNOLOGIES USED IN FRAUD DETECTION
● Machine Learning: Machine learning models learn from historical transaction data and
can classify transactions according to the likelihood of fraud. Supervised learning can be

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 84

trained using known fraudulent and legitimate transactions, while unsupervised m ethods
can identify unusual patterns without requiring every transaction to be labelled.
● Deep Learning: Deep learning models can process complex and high -dimensional data
and may identify relationships that are difficult to detect through conventional anal ytical
methods. Their complexity can, however, create explainability challenges.
● Anomaly Detection: Anomaly detection identifies behavior that differs from established
patterns. It is useful when fraud patterns are new or when labelled examples are limited.
● Predictive Analytics: Predictive analytics uses historical and current data to estimate the
likelihood of future suspicious activity. It can support risk scoring and early identification
of potentially fraudulent transactions.

ROLE OF BANKS, FINANCIAL I NSTITUTIONS AND REGULATORY
AUTHORITIES
● Banks and financial institutions play a central role in implementing AI -powered fraud
detection. They are responsible for collecting and protecting relevant data, selecting
appropriate models, monitoring performance and ensuring that alerts are investigated
effectively. Institutions should also establish clear procedures for human review when
automated systems flag customer activity.
● Financial institutions need continuous monitoring because fraud patterns evolve rapid ly.
Model performance should be evaluated regularly, and data quality should be checked to
reduce biased outcomes and unnecessary false positives. Institutions should also
communicate appropriately with customers when transactions are blocked or accounts a re
restricted.
● Regulatory authorities have an important role in ensuring that AI adoption remains
consistent with financial security, privacy and consumer protection requirements.
Effective governance should address accountability, transparency, data prote ction,
cybersecurity and third-party technology dependencies.

AI-BASED FRAUD MONITORING
● AI-based fraud monitoring enables financial institutions to examine transactions and
customer behavior continuously. Systems can consider multiple signals such as
transaction value, time, location, device information, account history and transaction
frequency. Combining these signals can provide a broader view of risk than evaluating a
single transaction in isolation.
● Real-time monitoring is particularly important in di gital banking because fraudulent
transactions may occur within a short period. AI can support rapid risk scoring and alert
generation, allowing institutions to respond before additional suspicious activity occurs.

EARLY WARNING SYSTEMS
● AI can support early warning systems by identifying changes in customer behavior before
a significant loss occurs. Sudden changes in transaction frequency, unusual payment

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 85

patterns or unexpected access behavior may be treated as warning signals. Such syste ms
can help institutions prioritize high-risk activities and initiate additional verification.
● Early warning systems are most useful when alerts are reviewed using appropriate risk
thresholds. Excessive alerts can create operational pressure and customer i nconvenience,
while thresholds that are too relaxed may allow suspicious transactions to pass.

CUSTOMER AUTHENTICATION
● AI can strengthen customer authentication by analyzing behavioral and contextual
information. Device characteristics, login behavior, transaction patterns and other signals
can be used to identify whether an activity appears consistent with normal customer
behavior. Additional verification can then be requested when risk is higher.
● However, authentication systems should balance securit y with customer convenience.
Excessive verification or incorrect blocking of legitimate transactions can create
frustration. Clear communication and appropriate human support are therefore important
when automated systems affect customer access.

RBI INITIATIVES
● The Reserve Bank of India has an important role in strengthening the security and
resilience of the Indian banking and financial system. Regulatory and supervisory
measures relating to digital banking, payment security, cybersecurity  and risk
management create the broader environment in which financial institutions develop fraud
prevention mechanisms.
● AI-based fraud detection can support these objectives by strengthening transaction
monitoring, identifying suspicious patterns and impr oving the speed of response. At the
institutional level, AI adoption should remain aligned with applicable regulatory
requirements, data protection responsibilities, cybersecurity controls and customer
protection principles.

BENEFITS OF AI-POWERED FRAUD DETECTION
• Real-time monitoring of large volumes of transactions.
• Identification of complex and unusual behavioral patterns.
• Faster generation of alerts and support for timely intervention.
• Improved risk assessment and fraud investigation.
• Potential reduction of unnecessary false -positive alerts when models are properly designed
and monitored.
• Strengthening of customer security and confidence in digital financial services.

DIGITAL TRANSFORMATION AND AI-BASED FRAUD DETECTION
● Digital transformation has changed the way financial institutions deliver services and
manage risk. Mobile banking, internet banking, digital wallets and online payment

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 86

platforms generate large volumes of data that can be analyzed using AI. This provides
opportunities for more continuous and adaptive fraud monitoring.
● The use of AI also changes fraud management from a largely reactive process towards a
more data-driven and preventive approach. Instead of waiting for a fraud incident to be
reported, institut ions can identify unusual activity and take preventive measures.
However, digital transformation also expands the attack surface and therefore requires
strong cybersecurity, data governance and continuous system improvement.

AI AND FINANCIAL SERVICES
● AI-powered fraud detection is part of a wider transformation in financial services.
Artificial Intelligence is increasingly associated with risk management, customer service,
credit assessment, compliance and transaction monitoring. In fraud prevention, its va lue
lies in the ability to process large volumes of information and support decisions at speed.
● For financial institutions, effective AI adoption can improve operational efficiency and
support better allocation of investigation resources. For customers, st ronger fraud
detection can reduce exposure to unauthorized activity. At the same time, responsible
implementation is necessary to ensure that security measures do not create unnecessary
barriers to legitimate financial activity.

CHALLENGES AND BARRIERS
•Data Privacy: Fraud detection systems require access to large quantities of customers and
transaction data. Responsible collection, storage and use are essential.
•Data Quality and Bias: Poor -quality or biased datasets can affect model performance and
may contribute to unfair outcomes.
•False Positives: Legitimate transactions may be incorrectly identified as suspicious, creating
inconvenience for customers and additional investigation work.
•Model Transparency: Complex AI models may be difficult to explai n, particularly when
automated decisions affect customer access to financial services.
•Cybersecurity Risks: AI systems themselves must be protected against manipulation, attacks
and unauthorized access.
•Continuous Model Improvement: Fraud techniques chan ge over time, requiring regular
monitoring, retraining and evaluation of AI models.
FINDINGS
•AI-based technologies can strengthen financial fraud detection by analyzing large volumes
of transactions and identifying unusual patterns.
•Machine learning, dee p learning, anomaly detection and predictive analytics provide
different approaches for identifying and assessing suspicious activities.
•Real-time monitoring and early warning systems can support faster responses to potential
fraud.
•The effectiveness of AI depends on data quality, model adaptability, continuous evaluation
and appropriate human oversight.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 87

•Privacy, explainability, cybersecurity, biased datasets and false positives remain important
challenges in AI-powered fraud detection.
•Responsible adop tion of AI can support stronger financial security while maintaining
customer confidence.

SUGGESTIONS
•Financial institutions should combine AI-based detection with human review for high-risk or
disputed cases.
•AI models should be continuously monitored,  tested and updated to respond to changing
fraud patterns.
•Strong data protection and cybersecurity practices should be maintained throughout the AI
system lifecycle.
•Institutions should use explainable AI methods where automated decisions significantly
affect customers.
•Training data should be regularly assessed for quality and potential bias.
•Customers should receive clear and timely communication when transactions are blocked or
flagged for additional verification.
•Regulatory and institutional gover nance should clearly define accountability for AI -driven
fraud detection systems.

CONCLUSION
 The rapid growth of digital banking and financial services has created new opportunities as
well as new forms of financial fraud. AI -powered fraud detection prov ides an important
technological approach to this changing environment by enabling real -time transaction
monitoring, anomaly detection, behavioral analysis and predictive risk assessment. The study
shows that AI can strengthen fraud prevention and improve t he efficiency of financial
institutions, but its effectiveness depends on responsible implementation. Data privacy,
cybersecurity, model transparency, false positives, biased datasets and continuous model
improvement must be addressed through appropriate g overnance and human oversight.
Therefore, the future of AI -powered fraud detection lies not only in developing more
advanced models but also in ensuring that those models are secure, transparent, accountable
and appropriately supervised. Responsible adopti on of AI can contribute to a more secure,
efficient and resilient banking and financial services ecosystem while protecting customer
confidence.

AI-BASED RECRUITMENT AND SELECTION: EFFICIENCY, FAIRNESS, AND
ETHICAL ISSUES
Mrs. V. Srilekha, M.Com., MBA., M.Phil
, Assistant Professor, PG Department of Commerce
Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Thiruvallur – 602024.
ABSTRACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is transforming recruitment and selection by automating job
advertising, candidate sourcing, résumé screening, ranking, communication, scheduling,
assessment, and decision support. These tools red uce administrative workload and speed up
data-driven hiring, but they also raise concerns about fairness, algorithmic bias, transparency,
privacy, accountability, and human oversight. Systems trained on historical hiring data may
reproduce or amplify past discrimination, and candidates may struggle to understand or
challenge automated outcomes. This conceptual paper examines applications, efficiency
benefits, fairness concerns, ethical issues, implications for HR professionals, and measures
for responsible implementation. It argues that AI can support recruitment only when
accompanied by governance, transparency, data protection, bias monitoring, and meaningful
human oversight.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Recruitment, Selection, Human Resource Managem ent,
Algorithmic Bias, Fairness, Ethics, Employment, AI Governance
INTRODUCTION
Recruitment and selection are critical HR functions because employees drive organizational
performance, innovation, and competitiveness. Digital technologies have reshaped th ese
activities: AI can process large volumes of applications, identify qualifications and keywords,
answer candidate queries through chatbots, schedule interviews, and give recruiters decision -
support information. Because employment decisions determine peo ple’s access to jobs and
economic opportunity, they are especially sensitive. The central issue is therefore not merely
whether AI makes hiring more efficient, but how organizations can use it while protecting
fairness, human dignity, privacy, and equal employment opportunity.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
 Explain AI-based recruitment
 Examine its major applications
 Discuss efficiency benefits
 Examine fairness and algorithmic bias
 Identify ethical issues,
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
The paper follows a conceptual, liter ature-review approach based entirely on secondary
sources: academic literature, books, reports, institutional publications, policy documents, and

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 90

professional literature. No primary data, surveys, interviews, or statistical tests were used.
The literature was reviewed thematically around AI applications, recruitment efficiency,
candidate experience, algorithmic bias, fairness and discrimination, privacy, transparency and
explainability, accountability, human oversight, and future implications.
CONCEPT AND EVOLUTION OF AI IN RECRUITMENT
AI refers to computational systems that perform tasks normally requiring human intelligence,
such as pattern recognition, prediction, language understanding, and content generation. In
recruitment it draws on machine learning,  natural language processing, predictive analytics,
generative AI, chatbots, automated résumé screening, recommendation algorithms, and
computer-based assessments. A key distinction is between AI assistance, where systems
support HR professionals, and full y automated decision-making, where systems rank or filter
candidates with little human input.  Recruitment has evolved through three stages: traditional
(newspaper advertisements, agencies, paper applications, manual screening, face -to-face
interviews); dig ital (job portals, email applications, digital résumés, online databases); and
AI-enabled (automated sourcing, screening, matching, chatbots, scheduling, skills
assessment, recommendations, and analytics). This evolution increases the information
organizations can process, and with it the importance of responsible data management and
algorithmic governance.
APPLICATIONS OF AI IN RECRUITMENT AND SELECTION

advertisement

descriptions; suggests platforms to
reach applicants

platforms, professional networks,
and internal or past -applicant
databases

skills, certifications, and keywords

EFFICIENCY BENEFITS
• Less administrative work: automation of repetitive tasks frees HR staff for activities
needing human judgment.
• Faster screening: thousands of applications for a single vacancy can be processed far
faster than manually.
• Scalability: large, diverse applicant pools across multiple locations become manageable.
• Continuous availability: chatbots and automated systems answer candidates outside
working hours.
• Data organization: candidate data is structured and patterns are identified, supporting
workforce planning.
• Potential cost reduction: less time for repetitive work, though implementation, training,
monitoring, and governance also carry costs.
FAIRNESS AND ALGORITHMIC BIAS
A fair system should evaluate candidates on job -relevant qualifications rather than irrelevant
personal characteristics, but this is not straightforward. An AI system can produce unfair
outcomes even when protected characteristics are excluded, becaus e other variables
(employment history, educational background, geography, language patterns, career gaps)
can act as proxies that interact with social and demographic patterns. Removing gender or
ethnicity from a dataset therefore does not guarantee an unb iased algorithm. Algorithmic bias
means systematically unfair or disadvantageous outcomes for certain individuals or groups,
and it can enter at several stages:
Type of bias Explanation
Historical data bias Systems trained in past hiring decisions may rep roduce earlier
discriminatory or unequal practices.
Data representation
bias
Poorly represented groups in training data may be evaluated less
accurately.
Design bias Designers’ choice of variables and criteria may give excessive
weight to factors unrelated to job performance.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 92

Measurement bias Tools may measure the wrong thing, such as familiarity with a test
format rather than real job capability.
Feedback loops Employees selected by the algorithm become new training data,
reinforcing existing patterns.
ETHICAL ISSUES AND EQUAL EMPLOYMENT OPPORTUNITY
• Privacy: applicant data (contact details, education, work history, skills, assessment and
interview results) must be handled responsibly and in line with data -protection
requirements.
• Transparency: candidates may not know whether AI is used, what data is analyzed, how
they are ranked, or how recommendations influence decisions.
• Explainability: organizations should be able to give rejected applicants a meaningful
explanation, which complex models make difficult.
• Accountability: responsibility cannot be transferred to an algorithm; organizations
remain answerable for governance, monitoring, and correcting problems.
• Human oversight: recruiters can weigh context an algorithm misses, but oversight must
be meaningful rather than merely formal.
• Consent and data usage: candidates may not understand how their data is later
processed, so data practices must be clearly communicated.
AI can standardize procedures, but standardization does not guarantee equality: if  data or
criteria are biased, automation can make discrimination more systematic. Organizations
should therefore examine selection criteria, training data, model performance, candidate
outcomes, accessibility, and human review procedures, attending to both  technological
performance and social consequences.
FINDINGS
• AI is becoming an important recruitment technology that automates repetitive tasks and
processes large volumes of applications, improving speed and scalability.
• AI does not automatically guarantee fairness; historical data can introduce bias into
automated systems.
• Transparency, privacy, and responsible handling of candidate data are significant
requirements.
• Human oversight remains important, HR professionals need AI and data -literacy skills,
and continuous monitoring and ethical governance must accompany adoption.
Suggestions
• Use AI mainly as a decision-support tool, with criteria directly tied to job requirements.
• Evaluate systems regularly for bias and review outcomes across relevant groups.
• Maintain human oversight and provide mechanisms for human review.
• Protect candidate information through strong data governance and be transparent about
AI use.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 93

• Establish clear accountability, train HR professionals in AI literacy and ethics, evaluate
vendors carefully, and update governance policies as technology and regulation develop.
CONCLUSION
AI is changing how organizations recruit and select. It can automate repetitive tasks, process
large volumes of candidate information, support matching, imp rove communication, and
assist HR professionals. However, efficiency should not be the only measure of success:
fairness, privacy, transparency, accountability, and human oversight are equally important
because employment decisions have serious consequence s for individuals. AI systems reflect
the limitations of their training data, design, measurement, and implementation, so they
should not be assumed objective. Responsible use requires continuous evaluation, job -related
criteria, data protection, and meani ngful human involvement. The future of recruitment is
likely to be collaboration between AI and HR professionals rather than the replacement of
human judgment.

resources management: Challenges and a path forward. Ca lifornia Management
Review, 61(4), 15–42.

Commission.
5. International Labour Organization. (2024). Mind the AI divide: Shaping a global

THE IMPACT OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE ADOPTION ON START-UP
PERFORMANCE: AN EMPIRICAL STUDY
Mrs. L IRINE DEVI
 Part-Time Ph.D. Research Scholar, Department of Economics
Sriram College of Arts & Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur – 602024
ABSTRACT
Artificial Inte lligence (AI) has become an important technology in the modern business
environment. Startups are increasingly using AI for marketing, customer service, business
analytic content creation, decision -making, process automation and product development. AI
enables startups to improve efficiency, reduce repetitive work, understand customer
requirements and support business decisions.  The present study examines the impact of
Artificial Intelligence adoption on startup performance. It also identifies the major
applications and challenges associated with AI adoption among startups. Primary data was
collected from 100 startup entrepreneurs, founders, owners and managers in Chennai City
through a structured questionnaire. Percentage analysis was used to analyze the co llected
data. The illustrative findings indicate that AI adoption is associated with improvements in
productivity, innovation, decision -making and overall startup performance. The study also
identifies lack of skilled employees, implementation cost, data p rivacy and technical
knowledge as major challenges in AI adoption. The study suggests that startups should
provide AI-related training, strengthen data security and integrate AI with human knowledge
and business strategy.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence,  AI Adoption, Startup Performance, Entrepreneurship,
Innovation, Business Growth.
INTRODUCTION
Artificial Intelligence has emerged as one of the important technologies influencing modern
business and entrepreneurship. AI refers to technologies that enable computer systems to
perform activities such as learning, analyzing information, identifying patterns, generating
content and supporting decision -making. Startups operate in a highly competitive
environment with limited resources and a strong need for innov ation. Artificial Intelligence
can help startups improve their business activities by automating repetitive tasks, analyzing
customer information, supporting marketing activities and providing useful business insights.
AI applications are increasingly used  in digital marketing, customer relationship
management, market research, financial analysis, content creation, forecasting, recruitment
and customer service. Generative AI has also created new opportunities for startups in
content creation, product development and business communication. AI adoption can provide
several benefits, but startups may also face challenges. These include implementation cost,
shortage of skilled employees, lack of technical knowledge, data privacy, cyber security and
difficulties in integrating AI with existing business processes. Therefore, effective AI
adoption requires proper planning, employee training and responsible use of technology. The

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 95

present study focuses on selected startups in Chennai City and examines the adoption of AI
and its perceived impact on startup performance.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE
Chalmers, MacKenzie and Carter (2021): Chalmers, MacKenzie and Carter examined the
relationship between Artificial Intelligence and entrepreneurship. Their study highlighted the
role of AI in opportunity identification, idea generation, business development and scaling of
entrepreneurial ventures.
McElheran et al. (2024): McElheran and colleagues examined AI adoption among firms and
highlighted the increasing use of AI technologies in business activities. AI adoption was
associated with innovation, productivity and growth-oriented business strategies.
Liu and Wang (2024): Liu and Wang studied the role of generative AI resources in
entrepreneurial activities. Their study indicated that effective AI integration and collaboration
can support entrepreneurial performance and innovation.
Pati et al. (2024): Pati and colleagues examined Artificial Intelligence implementation in
Indian startups. Their study highlighted opportunities created by A I in innovation,
competitiveness and business growth, while also identifying challenges related to skills and
implementation.
Calvino, Reijerink and Samek (2025): Calvino, Reijerink and Samek discussed the effects of
generative AI on productivity, innovati on and entrepreneurship. Their work highlighted the
importance of human-AI collaboration, employee skills and responsible implementation.
Rezazadeh et al. (2025): Rezazadeh and colleagues examined the role of generative AI in
startup growth strategies. The  study highlighted applications of AI in product development,
sales, marketing, personalization and operational efficiency.
Research Gap
Previous studies indicate that AI can support entrepreneurship, innovation and business
growth. However, the level of A I adoption and its perceived benefits may differ according to
the resources, skills and technological capabilities of startups. Therefore, the present study
attempts to examine AI adoption and its perceived impact on startup performance based on
primary data from selected startups in Chennai City.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
1. To study the level of Artificial Intelligence adoption among startups.
2. To identify the major applications of AI in startup businesses.
3. To examine the perceived impact of AI adoption on startup performance.
4. To study the contribution of AI to startup innovation.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 96

5. To identify the major challenges faced by startups in adoption of AI.
6. To provide suggestions for effective AI adoption among startups.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
Research Design
The study follows descriptive research design.
Area of the Study
The study was conducted among selected startups in Chennai City, Tamil Nadu.
Sample Size
The sample consists of 100 respondents.
Sampling Method
Purposive sampling method was adopted to selec t startup entrepreneurs, founders, owners
and managers who have knowledge of or experience with Artificial Intelligence.
Sources of Data
Primary Data: Primary data were collected through a structured questionnaire.
Secondary Data:  Secondary data were colle cted from books, journals, research articles,
reports and other relevant sources.

2. The largest age group is 25–35 years, accounting for 42% of respondents.

5. About 48% of respondents report high or very high levels of AI adoption.
6. Content creation is the most commonly reported application of AI, followed by
marketing and promotion.
7. About 72% of respondents report significant or moderate improvement in startup
performance after adoption of AI.
8. Lack of skilled employees is the major challenge in AI adoption, followed by
implementation cost and data privacy concerns.
SUGGESTIONS
 Startups should provide regular training programmes to improve the AI -related skills
of employees.
 Startups should select affordable and suitable AI tools according to their business
requirements.
 Proper data privacy and cybersecurity measures should be adopted while using AI
applications.
 Entrepreneurs should use AI for productivity, innovation, customer service and
business analytics.
 Startups should maintain human supervision while using AI-generated information for
important business decisions.
 Continuous evaluation should be conducted to determine whether AI adoption is
improving productivity and business performance.
 Startup incubators and entrepreneurship development institutions can provide AI
awareness and training programmes.
 AI should be integrated with human creativity, knowledge and entrepreneurial
judgement.
CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence has become an importa nt technological resource for modern startups.
The study highlights the increasing awareness and adoption of AI among selected startups in
Chennai City.  The illustrative findings indicate that startups use AI for content creation,
marketing, customer servi ce, process automation and business analytics. A considerable
proportion of respondents perceive improvements in startup performance after adopting AI.
The findings also indicate that AI can support innovation and business decision -making. At
the same time , startups face challenges such as lack of skilled employees, implementation
costs, data privacy and lack of technical knowledge.
Therefore, effective AI adoption requires appropriate training, proper planning, data security
and continuous evaluation. The  study concludes that Artificial Intelligence can support
startup performance when it is appropriately integrated with business strategy, employee
skills and human decision-making.

DIGITAL MARKETING AND BRAND AWARENESS
Ms. S. Safreena
 Assistant professor, Department of Corporate Secretaryship
Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu
ABSTRACT
Digital marketing has become an important method for businesses to promote their products
and build brand awareness. It uses digital channels such as social media, search engines,
websites, email, online a dvertisements, and content platforms to communicate with
customers. Brand awareness means the ability of customers to recognize or remember a
particular brand. Digital marketing helps businesses reach a wider audience, communicate
with customers, and provi de information about their products and services. Research
literature has examined the relationship between digital marketing activities and brand
awareness through channels such as social media, email marketing, content marketing, and
search marketing. This paper explains the meaning of digital marketing and brand awareness,
major digital marketing methods, their relationship, benefits, challenges, and future scope.
Keywords: Digital Marketing, Brand Awareness, Social Media Marketing, Online
Advertising, Content Marketing, SEO, Customer Engagement, Brand Recognition.
1. INTRODUCTION
Digital technology has changed the way businesses communicate with customers. Today,
businesses use websites, social media, search engines, and other online platforms to promote
their products and services.  Digital marketing helps businesses reach customers quickly and
share information about their brands. It is especially useful for businesses that want to
increase their online presence and build customer relationships.
2. OBJECTIVES
The main objectives are:
 To understand the meaning of digital marketing.
 To understand the concept of brand awareness.
 To study the relationship between digital marketing and brand awareness.
 To identify the major digital marketing methods.
 To understand the benefits of digital marketing.
 To identify the challenges in building brand awareness.
 To study the future scope of digital marketing.
3. REVIEW OF LITERATURE
 Sreenivas (2024) studied the impact of digital marketing on brand awareness through a
review of existing literature. The study found that digital marketing tools such as social
media, online advertising, and content marketing can support businesses in creating brand
awareness.

  Bohara et al. reviewed research on online marketing and brand awa reness. Their study
highlighted the importance of digital platforms in helping businesses communicate with
customers and improve brand visibility.
 Ahmed et al. studied interactive digital media and brand awareness. The study examined
digital channels such as social media, email, and mobile marketing and their role in
customer interaction and brand awareness.
 Chaffey explained that digital marketing helps businesses communicate with customers
through digital channels and measure customer responses. Digital marketing can therefore
support businesses in building and maintaining their online presence.
 Kotler and Keller explained the importance of brand awareness in marketing. Customers
need to recognize and remember a brand before considering it during the buying process.
SOURCE OF DATA
This paper is completely based on secondary data.
The information has been collected from:
 Books
 Research journals
 Published research papers
 Literature reviews
 Business reports
 Websites
 Online articles
 Digital marketing publications
A published literature review on digital marketing and brand awareness specifically describes
its research as being based on secondary research using literature, internet sources,
newspapers, magazines, articles, studies, reports, and websites.
3. MEANING OF DIGITAL MARKETING
Digital marketing is the promotion of products and services through digital channels.  It
includes social media, websites, search engines, email, mobile applications, online
advertisements, and other digital platforms.  Digital marketing allows businesses to
communicate with customers and share information about their products and services.
4. MEANING OF BRAND AWARENESS
Brand awareness refers to the ability of customers to recognize or remember a brand when
they see its name , logo, product, or other brand information.  For example, when customers
see a particular logo or advertisement and immediately identify the company, it shows brand
awareness. Brand awareness is important because customers are more likely to consider
brands that they recognize.
5. TYPES OF DIGITAL MARKETING
5.1 Social Media Marketing
Businesses use social media platforms to promote products, share information, and interact
with customers.

5.2 Search Engine Optimization (SEO)
SEO helps businesses improve the visibility of their websites in search engine results.
5.3 Content Marketing
Businesses create useful content such as blogs, videos, articles, and images to attract
customers.
5.4 Email Marketing
Businesses use email to send information, updates, offers, and other messages to customers.
5.5 Online Advertising
Businesses use digital advertisements on websites, search engines, social media, and other
platforms.
5.6 Influencer Marketing
Businesses may work with online influencers to introduce their products a nd services to their
audiences.
6. ROLE OF DIGITAL MARKETING IN BRAND AWARENESS
Digital marketing helps businesses increase brand awareness in several ways:
 Reaches a large number of people.
 Improves online visibility
 Help businesses communicate with customers.
 Provides regular brand information.
 Encourages customer interaction.
 Help create a consistent brand image.
 Make it easier to measure marketing activities.
Research on interactive digital media has examined channels such as social media, email, and
mobile marketing in relation to brand awareness.
7. BENEFITS OF DIGITAL MARKETING FOR BRAND AWARENESS
7.1 Wider Reach
Businesses can reach customers beyond their local area through digital platforms.
7.2 Customer Engagement
Customers can like, comment, share, review, and interact with brand content.
7.3 Cost Flexibility
Digital marketing provides businesses with different advertising and promotional options
according to their budget.
7.4 Easy Measurement
Businesses can track views, clicks, engagement, and other digital marketing activities.

7.5 Faster Communication
Businesses can share new products, offers, and information quickly.
8. DIGITAL MARKETING AND CUSTOMER BEHAVIOUR
Digital marketing can influence how customers discover and interact with brands . Customers
can search for products, read reviews, watch videos, compare products, and communicate
with businesses online. Social media content quality and interaction have also been studied in
relation to brand awareness and purchase-related behavior.
9. CHALLENGES
Digital marketing also has some challenges:
 High Competition – Many brands compete for customers' attention.
 Changing Technology – Digital platforms and trends change frequently.
 Data Privacy – Customer information must be handled responsibly.
 Cybersecurity – Businesses need to protect their digital systems.
 Negative Reviews – Negative online feedback can affect a brand's image.
 Content Creation – Businesses need regular and useful content.
10. FUTURE OF DIGITAL MARKETING AND BRAND AWARENESS
The future of digital marketing will continue to develop with new technologies.  Artificial
Intelligence, data analytics, automation, personalized content, video marketing, and social
media are expected to play important roles in digital marketing.  Businesses will increasingly
focus on providing relevant content and better digital experiences to customers.
11. FINDINGS
Based on the secondary data reviewed, the following findings are identified:
1. Digital marketing helps businesses improve brand awareness.
2. Social media is an important channel for reaching and engaging customers.
3. Content marketing helps businesses provide useful information and build customer
interest.
4. Online advertising helps businesses reach specific customer groups.
5. SEO helps improve the online visibility of a brand.
6. Regular digital communication can help businesses maintain customer engagement.
7. Customer reviews and online interactions can influence brand perception.
8. Businesses need to continuously update their digital marketing strategies because customer
behavior and technology change over time.
12. SUGGESTIONS
Based on the findings, the following suggestions are given:

1. Businesses should maintain an active presence on suitable digital platforms.

3. Businesses should use social media regularly to communicate with customers.
4. SEO should be used to improve website visibility.
5. Customer feedback and online reviews should be monitored regularly.
6. Businesses should protect customer data and maintain online security.
7. Digital marketing performance should be measured regularly.
8. Businesses should update their digital marketing strategies according to changing customer
needs.
13. LIMITATIONS
The study has the following limitations:
1. The study is completely based on secondary data.
2. No primary data was collected through questionnaires or interviews.
3. The study depends on information available in published sources.
4. Digital marketing trends change rapidly, so some information may become outdated.
5. The study covers digital marketing and brand awareness in general and does not focus on
one particular company.
6. Different industries may experience different results from digital marketing.
7. The study does not include detailed statistical analysis based on primary customer data.
14. CONCLUSION
Digital marketing has become an important tool for building brand awareness. Social media,
SEO, content marketing, email marketing, and online advertising help businesses
communicate with customers and improve their online visibility.  Brand awareness helps
customers recognize and remember a brand. Therefore, businesses can use suitable digital
marketing methods to create awareness and maintain customer engagement . Digital
marketing will continue to develop as technology changes, making it an important part of
modern business.
