IMPACT OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE ON DIGITAL ADVERTISEMENT
Ms. J. Ranjitha M.Com., B. Ed
 Assistant Professor
 PG Department of Commerce
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Thiruvallur - 602024
ABSTACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is transforming digital advertising by enabling businesses to
analyze consumer data, identify target audiences, personalize advertisements, automate media
decisions, generate advertising content, and evaluate campaign performance. The increasing
application of machine learning, predictive analytics, recommendation systems,
programmatic advertising, and generative AI has changed the design, delivery, and evaluation
of digital advertisements. This study aims to examine the impact of AI on digital advertising,
focusing on AI-based personalization, targeted advertising, predictive analytics, AI-generated
advertising content, an d automated ad placement. The study is expected to provide an
understanding of the role of AI in improving digital advertising effectiveness and consumer
responses in an increasingly automated advertising environment.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Dig ital Advertising, AI -Based Personalization, Targeted
Advertising, Generative AI, Advertising Effectiveness, Consumer Engagement, Purchase
Intention.
INTRODUCTION
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has become an important technology in the development of digital
advertising. The growth of social media, search engines, e -commerce platforms and other
digital channels has created large amounts of consumer data. AI enables advertisers to
process this information and identify consumer interests, preferences, online act ivities and
possible purchasing behavior. As a result, advertising is gradually moving from broad
audience communication to more personalized and data-driven communication. AI is being
applied at different stages of digital advertising, including audience identification,
advertisement personalization, content creation, media planning, ad placement and campaign
evaluation. The emergence of generative AI has further expanded the role of AI in
advertising. Tools such as ChatGPT can support the development of a dvertising ideas, text,
creative concepts and campaign activities. The traditional machine -learning applications
towards understanding how generative AI contributes to advertising creation and consumer
response.
However, the effectiveness of AI -based adve rtising cannot be understood only through
technological efficiency. Consumers may respond differently depending on the relevance,
personalization, transparency and credibility of advertisements. Therefore, the present study
focuses on the impact of AI on d igital advertising, with particular attention to AI -based
personalization, targeted advertising, predictive analytics, AI -generated advertising content
and automated ad placement.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 108

BACKGROUND OF THE STUDY
Digital advertising has changed significantly with the development of artificial intelligence.
Traditional advertising generally depends on predefined audience segments and human
decisions regarding advertising content and media selection. AI introduces da ta-driven
systems that can continuously analyze consumer information and support advertising
decisions. One important application is AI -based personalization. AI can use consumer
information and previous online behavior to provide advertisements that are m ore closely
related to individual interests. Targeted advertising is another major application of AI.
Machine-learning systems can identify potential audiences and support the delivery of
advertisements to selected consumer groups. Research on AI -based targeted advertising has
associated improved targeting precision with consumer engagement and purchase -related
responses.
AI also supports predictive analytics, allowing advertisers to use available data to anticipate
consumer behavior and campaign performan ce. This can help businesses make decisions
regarding audience selection, advertising content and resource allocation.  The development
of generative AI has introduced another dimension. AI can assist in producing advertising
text, images, videos and creati ve ideas. A recent field experiment found that personalized
video advertisements created using generative AI generated higher engagement than the
comparison advertising formats used in that experiment.   Finally, AI can support automated
ad placement and ca mpaign optimization. Programmatic advertising uses automated systems
to make advertising decisions based on available data. Thus, AI is increasingly involved not
only in creating advertisements but also in deciding where, when and to whom they should be
delivered. This study attempts to provide an integrated view of how different AI applications
influence digital advertising effectiveness, consumer engagement and purchase intention.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
 To examine the influence of AI -based personalization and targeted advertising on the
effectiveness of digital advertisements.
 To analyze the role of predictive analytics and automated ad placement in improving the
efficiency of digital advertising campaigns.
 To examine the influence of AI -generated advertising content on consumer engagement
and advertising response.
 To assess the relationship between AI -driven digital advertising and consumers' purchase
intention, considering advertising relevance, credibility and transparency.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE
Li (2019) – Artificial Intelligence and Advertising introduction discusses the emergence of
AI as an important area within advertising research. The study highlights the increasing need

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 109

to understand how intelligent technologies are changing advertising and  identifies AI and
advertising as an emerging research field.
Qin and Jiang (2019) – AI in the Advertising Process examined how AI was being used in the
Chinese advertising market. They identified four major stages supported by AI: consumer
insight discovery, advertisement creation, media planning and buying, and advertising impact
evaluation. Their study suggests that AI can reorganize the traditional advertising process and
improve operational efficiency.
Gołąb-Andrzejak (2023) – Generative AI and ChatG PT examined the use of generative AI
and ChatGPT in the creation of digital advertising campaigns. The study considered how
these technologies can contribute to different stages of campaign development and
highlighted the importance of cooperation between marketers and AI tools. The research used
literature analysis, secondary data and an expert interview and identified the need for further
empirical research.
Wilson, Johnson and Brown (2024) – AI in Digital Marketing examined AI applications in
digital marketing, including targeting, personalization, predictive analytics, content creation
and customer engagement. It also discussed challenges such as privacy, algorithmic bias,
implementation costs and the need for skilled employees.
Kapoor and Kumar (2025)  – Generative AI and Personalized Video Advertising investigated
personalized video advertisements created with generative AI through a field experiment.
Their study compared GenAI -based personalized video advertisements with personalized
image advertiseme nts and generic non -personalized video advertisements. The GenAI
personalized video treatment produced higher engagement in the experiment.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
This paper is completely based on secondary data from various journals and literature.
DATA INTERPRETATION
AI-based Personalization and Targeted Advertising
AI-based personalization uses consumer information to provide advertisements that are
relevant to individual interests. Targeted advertising uses AI to identify audiences that may
be more interested in a particular product or service.
Predictive Analytics and Automated Ad Placement
Predictive analytics enables advertisers to analyze historical and current data to identify
possible consumer behavior and campaign outcomes. Automated ad placement  can then use
these insights to determine suitable advertising opportunities.
AI-generated Advertising Content and Consumer Engagement
Generative AI can produce advertising copy, images, video concepts and other creative
material. This can reduce the time required for content development and allow businesses to
produce multiple versions of advertisements.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 110

AI Advertising and Purchase Intention
AI-driven advertising suggests that perceived relevance, credibility and transparency can play
important roles in co nsumer engagement.  This makes purchase intention a useful outcome
variable for your study. It also gives your research a stronger consumer -behavior perspective
rather than making it only a technology-focused study.
FINDINGS
AI-based personalization is expe cted to increase the perceived relevance of digital
advertisements. AI -supported targeted advertising is expected to help advertisers reach
consumers who are more likely to be interested in the advertised product or service.
Predictive analytics and automa ted ad placement are expected to improve the efficiency and
timeliness of advertising decisions. AI -generated advertising content is expected to support
faster content development and increase consumer engagement when the content is relevant
and creative. Consumer responses may depend on the level of personalization. Excessive
personalization may create privacy or intrusion concerns, while appropriate personalization
may improve advertising acceptance.  Advertising relevance, credibility and transparency are
expected to influence the relationship between AI -based advertising and consumer
engagement. Higher consumer engagement is expected to be associated with stronger
purchase intentions.
SUGGESTIONS
 Use personalization carefully: Advertisers should provide relevant advertisements without
making consumers feel that their privacy is being excessively monitored.
 Improve transparency: Businesses should provide appropriate information about the use
of AI and consumer data in advertising.
 Combine AI with human creativity: AI can support content generation, but human review
can help maintain originality, emotional appeal and brand consistency.
 Monitor AI -generated content: Advertisers should check AI -generated text, images and
videos for accuracy, misleading information and inappropriate content.
 Use predictive analytics responsibly: AI predictions should support marketing decisions
rather than completely replacing human judgement.
 Evaluate consumer response continuously: Businesses should measure engagement,
relevance, credibility and purchase-related responses instead of evaluating AI advertising
only through technical performance.
 Protect consumer data: Appropriate data -management and privacy practices should be
maintained when AI systems use consumer information.
CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence is changing digital advertising by supporting audience targeting,
personalization, predictive analysis, content generation and automated advertising decisions.
The reviewed literature shows tha t AI has the potential to improve different stages of
advertising, from understanding consumers to creating and delivering advertisements.

Commerce and Management

However, the impact of AI cannot be evaluated only from the perspective of technological
efficiency. Consumer perceptions are also important. Relevance, credibility, transparency and
the level of personalization can influence how consumers respond to AI -driven
advertisements. Recent research demonstrates that the relationship between AI advertising
and consumer engageme nt involves both positive opportunities and potential consumer
concerns. Therefore, the study proposes an integrated examination of AI -based
personalization, targeted advertising, predictive analytics, AI -generated advertising content
and automated ad plac ement. By connecting these AI applications with advertising
effectiveness, consumer engagement and purchase intention, the study can provide a broader
understanding of the impact of AI on digital advertising.

ROLE OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN COMMERCE AND MANAGEMENT
H. Banumathi,
Assistant Professor, Department B. Com (A/F)
Sriram College of Arts & Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024
I. T.S. Faridha Begum,

ABSTRACT
Business processes, decision -making, and operational efficiency are all being revolutionized
by artificial intelligence (AI), which is transforming management and commerce. AI-powered
technologies, such as machine learning, natural language processing, and predictive analytics,
are reshaping commerce by enhancing customer experiences, optimizing supply chains, and
improving marke ting strategies.  AI makes it easier to make decisions based on data,
automates routine tasks, and helps with financial forecasting, workforce management, and
strategic planning in management. Ethical concerns, data privacy issues, workforce
displacement, and the requirement for regulatory frameworks are just a few of the challenges
associated with AI adoption. Concerns regarding managerial practices' transparency and
accountability are also raised by the opaqueness and complexity of AI -driven decision -
making. AI presents significant opportunities for future advancements in spite of these
obstacles. It is anticipated that upcoming innovations like AI -driven business intelligence,
ethical AI frameworks, and explainable AI will address existing issues while al so increasing
productivity and efficiency. The role that artificial intelligence (AI) currently plays in
business and management, as well as the major obstacles it faces, are examined in this paper,
as are potential future opportunities to use AI to achiev e sustainable business growth and
competitive advantage.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence
INTRODUCTION
The rapid development of Artificial Intelligence (AI) has ushered in a new era of change in a
number of different fields, with management and commerce e merging as two of the most
significantly impacted fields. The term "AI" refers to the process by which machines,
particularly computer systems, imitate human intelligence processes so that they can learn,
reason, and make decisions. Through applications li ke personalized marketing, predictive
analytics, intelligent supply chain management, and customer service automation, AI is
reshaping conventional business models in the commercial sector. In addition to improving
operational effectiveness, these technolo gies open up new opportunities for customer
engagement and revenue generation. By providing real -time data analysis, trend forecasting,
and performance monitoring, AI enables management decision -making to be more informed
and timelier. It enables businesse s to improve resource allocation, foster innovation, and
streamline administrative processes. Ethical considerations, data privacy concerns, and the
possibility of job displacement due to automation are just a few of the difficulties associated
with incorporating AI into business and management, despite the benefits of doing so. The

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 113

purpose of this study is to investigate the changing nature of AI's role in business and
management, the issues that are associated with it, and the opportunities it presents for strategic
advantage and growth in an increasingly competitive global market.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE
Handan Derya Ercan (2024) describes how the advertising ecosystem has evolved over time
to accommodate technological advancements. Advertising is significan tly affected by
unprecedented advancements in artificial intelligence (AI) technology. Advances in AI have
benefited four main advertising processes: capturing consumer insights, designing new
advertisements, planning and purchasing media, and measuring th e impact and effectiveness.
As a result, it's critical to keep up with the most recent issues and trends in advertising and to
acknowledge AI's influence and application. To this end, a comprehensive systematic
literature review (SLR) was carried out to in vestigate studies that investigate the function and
development of AI in advertising and were published between the years 2005 and 2023. The
literature on programmatic, computational, and intelligent advertising was the focus of the
review. In addition to highlighting current AI advertising research topics, the study's findings
highlight emerging fields like manipulated advertising, Generative AI, and consumer
concerns, including ethical issues.
Muhammad Yasir Mustafa (2024): Despite the growing use of Artificial Intelligence in
Education (AIED), there are still a few issues that need to be addressed. As a result,
systematic reviews aimed at synthesizing the AIED findings in the literature have be en
carried out by researchers. However, the focus, stakeholders, educational level, region, and
other aspects of these AIED reviews vary widely. Because of this, it has been challenging to
comprehend the AIED landscape as a whole. By systematically meta -synthesizing the AIED
literature reviews, this study moves one step closer to filling this research gap. Specifically,
the technology-based learning model was used to include and analyze 143 literature reviews.
It is important to note that China and the Unit ed States have conducted the majority of AIED
research. Additionally, higher education received the most attention when discussing AIED,
while special education received less attention. The findings also show that AI is primarily
used to assist educators a nd students, with less attention paid to other educational
stakeholders (such as school leaders or administrators). The study provides a possible path for
the AIED research agenda in the future, making it easier to implement AIED that is both safe
and effective.
Fathiya Al -Kamzari and Norlidah Alias (2025) conducted a systematic literature review
(SLR) to investigate the applications, advantages, and difficulties of incorporating artificial
intelligence (AI) into the teaching of physics in secondary school s. The review was
conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and
Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) guidelines and included English -language peer-reviewed articles
from the Scopus and Web of Science databases. Double screening an d analysis confirmed
that only relevant, high -quality studies were included. Through adaptive learning systems,
virtual labs, and intelligent tutoring tools, AI has the potential to personalize physics
education, increase student engagement, and accommodat e a wide range of learning needs,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 114

according to the findings. However, significant obstacles were identified, including teacher
readiness, concerns regarding data privacy, and balancing the reliance on AI with the
promotion of critical thinking. To address these issues, educators must participate in
extensive professional development and employ transparent, ethical data management
practices to maintain trust and transparency. Specific teacher training programs and the
creation of AI usage policies that are r esponsible are among the recommendations. The long -
term effects of AI on critical thinking and problem -solving abilities in secondary school
physics should be the subject of future research.
Dr. Kaushinder Kumar Bhawna Wadhva (2026): Artificial intelligen ce is now used by online
shopping platforms to study customer behavior and provide individualized product
recommendations. Businesses use machine learning tools, recommendation software, and
automated chat systems to show ads that are relevant to their cus tomers and respond
immediately to their questions. This paper gathers and analyzes previous research on AI -
powered marketing strategies and e -commerce site security flaws. We examine how
intelligent security tools are able to detect unusual activities more  quickly than traditional
systems. We also talk about privacy concerns like how much customer data businesses should
collect and whether users really gave their consent. According to our analysis, AI can boost
sales and safety at the same time. However, in  order to maintain customer trust, businesses
must construct robust defenses and adhere to ethical guidelines.
APPLICATIONS OF AI IN COMMERCE
Marketing with AI
 Customer Segmentation: AI tools can sort people into groups based on their preferences,
age, location, shopping habits, and other factors. For instance, artificial intelligence (AI)
can be used by an online shopping site to identify which demographic favors sportswear
and then send them relevant advertisements or offers. This saves money and time b y
allowing you to target the right customers.
 Recommendation: A lot of websites, like Amazon and Netflix, use AI to give customers
recommendations based on what they've already seen or purchased. Recommendation
engines are the name given to these. For ins tance, if someone purchases a phone, the
website might suggest a screen protector or phone cover. This makes shopping more
personal and enjoyable while also increasing sales.
 AI chatbots and customer service: AI chatbots are able to answer questions at an y time.
They can answer frequently asked questions, assist customers in tracking their orders, and
even suggest products. This reduces the need for large customer support teams and
enhances customer service.
AI in Supply Chain Management
The global interes t and investment in AI technologies to enhance e -commerce operations is
reflected in the fact that China-based institutions are leading in this research area, as the study
demonstrates. AI in Logistics Management Due to its potential to increase productivi ty and
resilience, the use of artificial intelligence (AI) in supply chain management (SCM) has
received a lot of attention. The contributions of AI to SCM, particularly in logistics,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 115

marketing, supply chain, and production.  The study emphasizes that supp ly chain demand
forecasting, inventory management, and overall decision -making capabilities can all be
enhanced using AI tools like predictive analytics and machine learning. Furthermore, discuss
the role of AI in developing resilient supply chains, noting  that early adopters of AI -enabled
SCM have reported reductions in logistics costs by 15%, improvements in inventory levels by
35%, and enhancements in service levels by 65%.  These findings highlight AI's
transformative potential for enhancing supply chain operations and gaining an advantage over
rivals.
Finance and AI
Numerous transactions, sensitive data, and numbers are exchanged in the financial sector.
Financial services are made safer, smarter, and faster with the help of AI.
 Fraud: AI systems can continuously monitor unusual behavior. AI can, for instance,
detect a sudden expenditure of a significant amount of money in a foreign country as a
potential fraud and notify the bank. To determine whether a transaction is suspicious,  AI
examines previous data and patterns.
 Robo-Advisory: Artificial intelligence can also provide investment advice. Robo -advisors
are online platforms that suggest investments based on a user's goals, income, and risk
tolerance. Because they are simple to  use, accessible at any time, and frequently less
expensive than human advisors, these platforms are gaining popularity.
 Credit: AI is used by lending institutions and banks to determine who is a good candidate
for a loan. AI can assist in making quick an d equitable loan decisions by analyzing
behavior, income, and previous payments.
AI in Quick Commerce (Q-Commerce)
 In the retail industry, Q -commerce, which stands for quick product delivery, has emerged as
a significant trend. Q -commerce platforms' opera tional efficiency is significantly improved
thanks to AI. Protonshub Technologies' (2024) article says that AI can help with marketing
and inventory management by using past data to predict customer preferences. Businesses
can effectively create promotions and stock appropriately to attract customers by anticipating
popular items. Additionally, explores the transformative impact of AI on automating business
processes in e -commerce, focusing on enhancing efficiency and customer experience.  In
order to proce ss and analyze data from e -commerce transaction records and customers'
browsing histories, the research makes use of deep learning and machine learning as the
primary analytical tools. This demonstrates the significance of AI in streamlining operations
and increasing customer satisfaction.
Management and AI
Management applications of AI Organizational management is being transformed by artificial
intelligence (AI). It is helping managers make better decisions, automate repetitive tasks, and
improve overall efficiency.  From hiring employees to planning large projects, AI plays a key
role in various aspects of management.  We will investigate the applications of AI in HRM,
strategic decision-making, operations, and project management in this section.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 116

Artificial Intelligence in HRM
The hiring, training, management, and retention of employees are all aspects of human
resource management. AI is reducing manual labor and enabling HR professionals to make
decisions more quickly and effectively.
 Resume screening: Screening resumes for job openings is one of HR's most time -
consuming tasks. Today, AI tools can quickly scan hundreds or thousands of resumes
automatically. Based on keywords, qualifications, experience, and job roles, these tools
can find the best candidates. For instance, if a company is looking for a data analyst, the
artificial intelligence system will go through all of the applications and select candidates
who possess the necessary skills, such as Excel, SQL, Python, and so on. This ensure s
fairness in the selection process and speeds up the process of finding the right person.
 Recruitment Chatbots: Chatbots powered by AI are also being used to interact with job
applicants. They are able to schedule interviews, gather fundamental data, and  respond to
frequently asked questions about the position. This makes applicants' experiences better
and saves HR teams a lot of time.
 Predictive Attrition Analysis: Predicting which employees are likely to leave an
organization is another useful applicat ion of AI in human resources. AI systems look at
things like employee satisfaction, performance, attendance, and even responses to surveys
given by companies. The HR department can take measures to keep employees by
offering them new roles, improved suppor t, or incentives if the AI tool detects a pattern
that suggests they might leave soon. This aids in the reduction of employee turnover,
which can be costly and disruptive to the business.
 Education and Training: AI can also be used to customize employee e ducation. AI can
suggest courses, videos, or training programs based on each employee's role and skill
level. This helps employees advance in their careers and makes learning more efficient.
 The Role of Artificial Intelligence in Strategic Decision -Making Strategic Management
entails establishing objectives, evaluating the surrounding environment, making crucial
choices, and preparing for the future. By providing insights based on data, AI aids
managers in a useful way.
 Data Analysis and Insights: Today's  businesses collect a lot of data from customers,
rivals, and internal processes. This data can be quickly and accurately analyzed with the
aid of AI tools. They are capable of recognizing relationships, patterns, and trends that
humans might not be able to see. For instance, artificial intelligence (AI) could be used by
a retail company to determine which products sell well in particular cities or seasons. The
management can better plan inventory, pricing, and marketing strategies with this
information.
 Scenario Planning and Forecasting: AI can use current data to simulate various business
scenarios and forecast their outcomes. A company might want to introduce a new
product. It can also predict potential risks and profits. Top management uses this kind of
predictive insight to make decisions that are better and based on evidence.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 117

 Competitive Intelligence: AI can look at competitor websites, news articles, product
launches, and customer reviews to see what they are doing. By gaining a real -time
understanding of market changes, businesses can stay one step ahead.
 Customer Sentiment Analysis: AI tools can look at online reviews, comments on social
media, and other forms of feedback to learn how customers feel about a brand or product.
This aids in the develo pment of product enhancements and marketing messages. In a
nutshell, AI enables management to remain informed, eliminate guesswork, and make
well-thought-out decisions.
AI in Operations and Project Management
AI in Project Management and Operations Plannin g, producing, and delivering goods or
services effectively is the focus of operations management. Project management is about
ensuring that specific goals are achieved on time and within budget.  By reducing delays,
saving money, and increasing productivity, AI enhances these two areas.
 Task Automation: AI can perform routine communication, data entry, and report
generation tasks that are repetitive. For instance, AI systems can collect, analyze, and
generate performance reports without the need for human i ntervention in a manufacturing
company. As a result, managers are able to concentrate less on daily paperwork and more
on planning and making decisions.
 Predictive Maintenance: AI is used to predict when machinery and equipment in an
industry will break d own or require maintenance. Predictive maintenance is the term for
this. The system notifies the manager ahead of time so that early maintenance can be
performed rather than waiting for a breakdown. This saves money and prevents
downtime.
 Project Scheduli ng and Planning: AI tools can help with project planning and
management. In order to estimate how much time and money will be needed, they look at
data from previous projects. Additionally, they monitor ongoing work and notify
managers of any risks or delays. Some tools even suggest how tasks can be rescheduled to
avoid bottlenecks.  Project failures are reduced, coordination is improved, and deliveries
are made on time.
 Supply and Inventory Optimization: AI helps good-s-handling businesses monitor
inventory levels and anticipate future requirements. For example, an AI system can
analyze sales data to tell a retail manager when to reorder products and how much to keep
in stock.  This prevents stockouts and overstocking.
 Workforce Planning - AI also aids in the assignment of the appropriate tasks to the
appropriate employees based on their availability and skills. As a result, projects are
completed more quickly, and human resources are utilized more effectively.
Ethical Challenges and Considerations
Despite the substantial advantages of AI integration, there are a number of issues and ethical
considerations that must be addressed. The success of AI initiatives is dependent on the

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 118

collaboration of human expertise and machine capabilities. They emphasize the i mportance
of human involvement in AI systems.
They argue that rather than attempting to replace human decision -making, AI systems should
be developed to complement it. In addition, the privacy of data, algorithmic bias, and
transparency of AI applications  are frequently raised concerns. Maintaining consumer trust
and compliance with regulations necessitates ensuring that AI systems operate in an ethical
manner and do not perpetuate existing biases. The creation of solid ethical frameworks and
continuous monitoring of AI systems to reduce potential risks are necessary for addressing
these issues.
Objectives of the study
 To comprehend the application of Artificial Intelligence in quick commerce.
 To determine the difficulties encountered when utilizing AI in quick commerce.
 To investigate how AI can speed up quick commerce while also increasing efficiency and
customer satisfaction.
 Challenges of AI Implementation in Commerce
  a) Expensive early-stage implementation costs for many businesses because AI adoption
can necessitate significant expenditures on infrastructure upgrades, legacy -system
modernization, maintenance, and specialist talent.
 b) Concerns about data privacy: AI systems use a lot of data, and IBM and the OECD
have done research that says AI can put privacy, security, and governance at risk if data
isn't properly controlled and protected.
 c) Integration with existing systems: Retail and CRM studies indicate that implementing
AI is frequently challenging due to the technical and organizational friction caused by the
integration of AI with legacy systems, workflows, and data structures.
 d) Issues with ethics and bias: Since the literature consistently warns that AI can
reproduce bias fr om training data and result in unfair outcomes, ethics -by-design and
responsible AI governance are becoming increasingly prominent in CRM and commerce
research.
 e) Displacement of the workforce: While AI may increase productivity, it may also
replace or reshape some jobs, raising concerns regarding job displacement, reskilling, and
labor reallocation. Change management issues are also mentioned in retail AI adoption
studies, according to IMF research, which demonstrates that the effects vary by sector and
worker group.

The regression test's findings are presented in Table 4. This is useful for determining the
significance of AI and how it affects management and business. Additionally, it demonstrates
the link between AI and management and business. The fact that the sig values for all of the
independent variables are lower than the standard significance value of 0.05 (familiarity with
AI introduction – 0.005 and agreement with AI software aiding in product discovery – 0.011,
as shown in table 4) indicates that these factors are significant and  have a significant impact
on business and management. However, AI-based commerce and management are utilized by
their organizations because the other two factors have values greater than 0.05. The beta
values for the factors are also positive, as shown in  Table 4, with the exception of the use of
in-house software or third -party software. This suggests a positive connection between these
components and management and commerce. Based on the results of the analysis, it is
possible to accept the hypothesis H1  that AI has a positive and significant impact on
management and commerce. As a result, the H1 (Alternate) hypothesis is accepted and the
H0 (Null) hypothesis is rejected, indicating that AI has a significant effect on management
and business operations.

Forthcoming Forecasts of Artificial Intelligence in Business and Organization
The field of business and management is being rapidly reshaped by artificial intelligence
(AI), and its future holds even greater promise. AI is anticipated to become more powerfu l,
user-friendly, and integrated into business procedures across all sectors as technology
advances. AI will not only aid in decision -making in the coming years, but it will also play a
crucial role in driving innovation, growth, and competitive advantage.
 Customer Services That Are Smarter and More Personalized AI will enable businesses in
the future to provide highly personalized customer experiences. AI systems will be able to
predict customer needs with greater accuracy and tailor products, services, a nd marketing
messages accordingly through advanced data analysis. E -commerce platforms, for
instance, will make use of AI to suggest not only products but also complete shopping
experiences based on the customer's mood, previous actions, and even location.
Customers will be more satisfied and likely to stay with you as a result.
 Operations of a Business on Their Own Many Routine and repetitive business operations
will be entirely handled by AI systems as automation increases. AI will streamline tasks
with greater speed and fewer errors, including inventory management, invoice processing,
and customer support queries. Schedule management, KPI tracking, and real -time report
generation will all be made easier by AI -based virtual assistants in management. Human
managers will be able to concentrate on innovation, strategy, and creativity as a result of
this.
 Improvements in Decision Making By providing real -time insights, risk assessments, and
predictive analysis, AI will play a larger role in strategic decision -making. Using AI,
managers will be able to choose the best course of action by simulating various business
scenarios. Businesses will be able to remain adaptable in a rapidly changing environment
as a result of the faster, more accurate, and evidence -based decision -making that will
result from the integration of AI and big data.
 Advancement of AI in HR Management Future applications of AI include improving
training programs, predicting career paths, managing employee wellness, and resume
screening. HR man agers can use AI to gain a deeper comprehension of employee
behavior, engagement levels, and potential concerns before they escalate into major
problems. Personalized learning journeys will also be offered by AI -driven platforms,
which will encourage employee development and productivity.
 AI in Sustainable and Ethical Business Operations AI will support sustainable practices as
businesses become more aware of their social and environmental responsibilities. In
supply chain management, for instance, AI can assist businesses in minimizing waste,
optimizing energy consumption, and ensuring ethical sourcing. It can also assist in
monitoring carbon footprints and achievement of sustainability objectives, making
businesses more environmentally friendly and responsible.
 New Skills and Positions While AI may automate a number of existing positions,  it will
also open up new possibilities. Professionals with expertise in AI -aided strategic
planning, data analysis, ethical AI governance, and AI management will be in high

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 122

demand in the future. Workforce reskilling and preparing for an AI -driven future w ill
require collaboration between businesses and educational institutions.
Future Prospects
The application of AI in management and commerce looks promising in the future, with
ongoing advancements anticipated to further alter business operations. Supply c hains that are
more open, productive, and responsive could be created by combining AI with other emerging
technologies like the Internet of Things (IoT) and blockchain. In addition, the creation of
explainable AI models aims to address issues related to trust and transparency by enhancing the
interpretability of AI-driven decisions. In addition, it is anticipated that the use of AI in Q -
commerce will expand as a result of the rising demand for personalized customer experience
and rapid delivery services. It is likely that businesses will gain a competitive advantage in the
market if they successfully utilize AI technologies to optimize their operations and meet
changing consumer expectations.
Conclusion
By improving efficiency, decision-making, and customer engagement, artificial intelligence
is reshaping business and management. AI is proving to be a useful tool in a variety of
business domains, from automating marketing strategies and financial operations to
improving supply chain and human resource functio ns. It facilitates smarter strategic
planning, personalized customer experiences, and faster data processing. However, despite
AI's enormous potential, its widespread use comes with a number of drawbacks, including
high implementation costs, data security concerns, and the requirement for skilled
professionals. The application of AI in business and management looks promising in spite
of these concerns. Businesses of all sizes can use AI to gain a competitive edge and provide
better value to their stakeholde rs and customers with constant technological advancements
and awareness.

BRAND MANAGEMENT AND RURAL MARKETING OPPORTUNITIES IN INDIA
V. Bhagyalakshmi
 Assistant Professor
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu.
ABSTRACT
This study examines the intersection of Brand  management and rural marketing
opportunities in India, highlighting the transition of rural regions from traditional, unbranded
consumption to brand -conscious markets. As urban markets approach saturation, the Indian
rural landscape presents a transformat ive frontier for fast moving consumer goods (FMCG).
The main object of the study is to analyze and present marketing of consumer products in
rural areas. The study also investigates the strategic imperatives of brand management and
the structural parameter s of rural marketing opportunities in India. Using the secondary
empirical data and market growth projections the research evaluates how the traditional 4 A’s
framework- Affordability, Availability, Acceptability and Awareness – replace conventional
marketing mixes to establish long term brand loyalty. Findings reveal that digital integration,
improved physical connectivity and the rise of local opinion leaders significantly accelerate
brand sickness and consumption growth.
Keywords: Brand Management, Rural  Marketing, India, FMCG consumption, 4 A’s
Framework, Market penetration.
INTRODUCTION
Rural Marketing in India is on high rise since the past decade and major multinational
corporations want to tap into this potential market which accounts for 68 percent of the
Indian population lives in these areas, there is a shift from urban markets into rural markets
the reasons for this shift are many, to name a few- the saturation of the urban markets, sudden
uprise in the number of rural counterparts. For many years , most companies focused mainly
on urban markets because of better roads, education and easy access to customers. However,
rural India has changes significantly in recent years. Rural Consumers are now earning more
educated and using wider range of product s then before. After the green revolution rural
marketing and its paradigms changed through the early 60’s to the mid-90’s. Rural customers
differ from urban customers because their buying decisions are influenced by local culture,
word of mouth, price sen sitivity and seasonal income patterns. Because of this companies
must design special marketing strategies suited to villages. Better transportation has made it
easier for companies to distribute goods to remote areas. Additionally mobile phones and the
internet have reached many villages, allowing rural consumers to access information, watch
advertisements and even purchase products online.  Corporations who want to enter the rural
market in India, to be victorious in the rural market you need to understand the rural
consumer first and then sell them your products and services. Research on Rural marketing
has been of keen interest to scholars because of the methods which various organizations
have tried to implement in their marketing mix of the price place p romotion product and has
developed into the 4 A’s of rural marketing as Availability, Affordability, Acceptability,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 124

Awareness. The population of rural consumers is 64.3 percent in 2025 (The global Economy)
with 941 million people living in rural areas.
RESEARCH OBJECTIVES
 To study the rural market and the current opportunities of brand management in rural
India.
 To understand the innovation undertaken by marketers towards rural marketing and brand
management.
 To understand the strategies, implement in rural marketing with brand management.
LITEREATURE REVIEW
 Brand marketing and rural marketing have emerged as important areas of study in the
Indian marketing environment. India’s rural market represents a significant consumer
base, with changing lifestyles, increasing literacy, better connectivity, and growing access
to digital technologies creating new opportunities for businesses. Researchers have
therefore examined how companies can build strong brands while adapting their
marketing strategies to rural consumers.
 Kotler and Keller emphasized that brand marketing  involves creating a distinctive
identity and value proposition that helps consumers differentiate one product from
competing products. A strong brand can create awareness, trust, perceived quality, and
customer loyalty. In the Indian context, these factor s become particularly important in
rural markets, where consumers may rely heavily on brand reputation and
recommendations from family, friends, retailers, and community members.
 The 4As model of rural marketing has been a more consumer -oriented approach towards
understanding the rural consumer. The 4As of Rural Marketing are an effort for
sustainability in the rural markets, this model is more suitable and successful for rural
markets.  For acceptance the products need to be of good quality, high value and  of
maximum utility in a rural environment. The rural customer is concerned over the pricing
of the products as they do not earn equally to their urban counterparts, hence need to be
able to afford products for their maximum value and minimum price available.
Awareness should be done with keeping in mind the rural perspective and their point of
view, and acceptability means to introduce distribution methods who’s impl emented
along with the help of rural consumers can be successful is benefiting the consumers as
well as the companies (Naidu, 2017).
 The study by Asha Thomas says that when it comes to the understanding of marketing
management it’s more advisable to study  the marketing environment to be successful in
implementing a marketing strategy.  The author emphasis on how no two villages are
identical hence each needs a special grasp of attention individually. Innovation in the
rural marketing practice by the ICICI Bank to customize their rural ATMs to biometric
authentication for its rural consumers, Philips developed the television model
'Vardaan'(Thomas, 2013)

 The project 'Shakti' by HUL has been one of the most successful distribution models in
rural marketing wi th the help of Self -Help Group.  The sales of consumer electronics
from rural markets for LG and Samsung have also shown a significant increase.
Acceptability of the product in rural market is important hence any organization when
trying to sell its goods and services needs to offer products which will be accepted by
these consumers and they are attracted to such as mobile companies customizing products
for rural consumers, LGs Sampoorna TV.  Awareness among the rural population is
crucial for making the co nsumer aware of the products and services they provide, Coca
Cola uses the combination of Television and radio to reach a rural household (Shah &
Desai, 2013).
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
The literature review has indicated sufficient sources and theory in unders tanding the current
scenario of rural marketing in India. The present study is based on secondary data obtained
from journals books and the internet to find relevant information.
RESEARCH GAP
Although considerable research has examined rural marketing in I ndia, there remains scope
for further research on the relationship between brand -building and rural consumer
behavior in the digital era. More research can examine how social media, digital payments,
e-commerce, regional-language content, and changing aspi rations influence brand preference
among rural consumers. There is also a need for comparative studies across different states
and rural consumer segments because rural India differs significantly in terms of culture,
income, infrastructure, and purchasing behavior.

IMPORTANT STATISTICAL EVIDENCE
A p articularly useful indicator for your study is rural FMCG consumption. NielsenIQ
reported that during January –March 2025, India's FMCG sector grew 11% in value, while
rural markets grew approximately four times faster than urban markets. Rural markets
outperformed urban markets for the fifth consecutive quarter. There is also evidence that rural
consumers are moving beyond only low -priced products. In 2025, rural India accounted for
51% of the volume share of “affordable premium” FMCG products, compared wit h 45% in
2021. This is relevant to brand management because it indicates an opportunity for
companies to build brands around quality and value, not only low prices. (Economic times)

Opportunities in Rural Marketing and Brand Management in India
India’s rural market offers significant opportunities for companies because of rising
consumer aspirations, improving connectivity, expanding digital access, and increasing
demand for branded products.
1. Growing Rural Consumer Base
Rural India represents a large consumer market for FMCG, automobiles, banking, insurance,
telecommunications, healthcare, education, and consumer durables.  Companies can develop
products specifically for rural consumers.
2. Digital Marketing
Increasing smartphone and internet usage creates opportunities for brands to reach rural
customers through:
 YouTube and short-video platforms
 Social media
 WhatsApp-based communication
 Regional-language advertising
 Digital payments and e-commerce
3. Affordable Packaging and Pricing
Many rural consumers are price conscious. Brands can increase penetration through:
 Small-size packages
 Low-unit-price products
 Refill packs
 Value-for-money products
4. Regional and Local Branding
India's rural markets differ considerably by language, culture and traditions. Companies can
build stronger brands through regional -language advertising, local festivals, cultural event s
and locally relevant messages.
5. Expansion of E-Commerce
Improved internet connectivity and logistics are creating opportunities for brands to sell
products beyond conventional village retail stores. E -commerce can give rural consumers
access to a wider range of branded products.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 128

6. Rural Distribution Networks
There is an opportunity to expand distribution through:
 Local retailers
 Rural distributors
 Self-help groups
 Village entrepreneurs
 Mobile retail outlets
 Rural markets and weekly haats
A strong distribution network can improve both product availability and brand visibility.
7. Premium and Aspirational Brands
Rural consumers are increasingly exposed to national and international brands. This creates
opportunities for companies to introduce premiu m products at accessible prices, particularly
in personal care, food, consumer electronics and automobiles.
8. Agriculture-Related Businesses
Companies can develop brands around the rural economy through:
 Agricultural equipment
 Seeds and Farm Inputs
 Irrigation products

considerable potential in rural areas. B uilding trustworthy and easy -to-understand brands is
particularly important in these categories.

11. Sustainable and Local Products
There are opportunities for brands based on local materials, traditional products,
environmentally friendly packaging and sustainable production. Local producers can also use
branding to expand beyond their immediate villages.
12. Data-Driven Rural Marketing
Companies can use customer and market data to understand differences between rural regions
and create more targeted strategies. Instead of treating “rural India” as one market, brands can
segment consumers according to income, occupation, location, language, lifestyle and
purchasing behavior.
SUGGESTIONS
Branding is a Costlier Affair: Since branding is much costlier, it has to be created at the
aggregators or organizational level. XI.SUGGESTIONS FOR RURAL PRODUCT
BRANDING: i. Utilization of Geographic Indication (GI) labels the sign utilized on items
with a particular geological beginning and have beginning-based characteristics and notoriety
in the locale assist with advancing the country’s items as brand.
i) Making brand Ambassadors by the Government: Unlike other FMCG items, to
advance marking in horticulture, the public authority ought to use the top Government
Officials (particularly IAS Officers) and Ministers as Brand Ambassadors. The GI
labels, supplements, and quality boundaries might be utilized as their excellent
highlights of the items. Further, th ey found area celebrities as brand clergymen to
propel commonplace things thriving in the state like Karnataka, wherein the brand
'Nandini' of Karnataka Milk Federation (KMF) has gotten the most raised reputation
in the state.
ii) Highlighting the Product Features: Making mindfulness about brands by featuring the
elements like natural nature, nourishment, beginning (like Madhubani painting,
Alfanso mango, Litchi honey, and so on), custom, and so forth.
iii) Strengthening digital rural marketing: Companies should co mbine traditional rural
promotion with mobile, social -media and digital marketing in regional languages.
Rural connectivity has expanded considerable as of May 2025, 97.65% of Indian
villages had mobile connectivity and 96.80% had 4G connectivity (press in formation
Bureau)
iv) Use local language and Cultural understanding: Brand communications should
reflect local language, traditions and consumer behavior. Regional storytelling, local
festivals and community -oriented communication can help brands establish
familiarity.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 130

v)   Promote rural products through E - Commerce: There is an opportunity not only
to sell products to rural consumers, but also to take rural products to national and
international markets. For Example, the government’s e -Saras initiative provides
online market access for self - help Group products, it reported more than 34 lakhs
product sales and over 8000 products from 30 states.
vi) Build trust -based brands: Rural consumers may plcae  considerable importance
on quality, reliability, value for money and relationship with retailers. Companies
should therefore focus on consistent quality and transparent communication rather
than only promotional offers.
CONCLUSIONS
The literature indicates that rural India provides substantial opportunities for brand marketers.
Successful rural marketing requires more than simply extending urban marketing strategies to
villages. Companies need to understand local consumer needs, provide appropria te prices and
pack sizes, strengthen distribution, communicate in regional languages, build trust, and
increasingly integrate digital channels. The major opportunity is to combine affordability +
accessibility + local relevance + digital communication + tr ust. Companies that
understand the differences among rural consumers can build stronger and more sustainable
brands in India. Thus, the combination of strong brand positioning and locally relevant
rural marketing strategies can play an important role in expanding business opportunities in
India.

RESEARCH TRENDS IN COMMERCE AND MANAGEMENT: DATA ETHICS
AND IMPACT
G. Mageshwari
 Guest Lecturer, B.com (Department of Computer Application)
ABSTRACT
Commerce and management are rapidly evolving fields influenced by technological
advancements, changing consumer behavior, globalization, sustainability, and emerging
business models. Recent research has increasingly focused on Artificial Intelligence (AI), Big
Data analytics, digital transformation, ecommerce, data ethics, sustainability, Environmental,
Social and Governance (ESG) practices, innovation, and human resource management are
created significant benefits, but it has also raised ethical concerns r egarding privacy, data
security, consent, transparency, fairness, and accountability. This study examines the
importance of data ethics and its impact on commerce and management. The research
emphasizes that organizations must balance the commercial value of data with ethical
responsibilities toward customers, employees, investors, and other stakeholders. Therefore,
integrating data ethics into commerce and management is essential for building trust,
improving decision making, ensuring responsible corporate  practices, and achieving
sustainable organizational growth in the digital economy.
Keywords: Data Ethics, Commerce, Management, Artificial Intelligence, Big Data, Data
Privacy, Consumer Trust, Data Governance, Corporate Governance, Digital Transformation.
INTRODUCTION:
Data has become one of the most valuable resources for modern organizations. Commerce
and management organizations now depend heavily on data, artificial intelligence, big data
analytics, and digital platforms. This has created new research opportunities i n areas such as
Artificial Intelligence (AI), Big Data, Data Privacy, Responsible AI, Consumer Trust, ESG,
and Digital Transformation. Therefore, Data Ethics and Impact has become an important
research area in commerce and management. Research in this fiel d examines how
organizations can use data and technology responsibly while protecting stakeholders and
maintaining trust.
1. REVIEW OF LITERATURE:
The study of research trends in this area helps identify emerging topics, changing
business practices, and the impact of responsible data management on business performance,
consumer behavior, corporate governance, and sustainable development. At the same time,
the use of data creates ethical issues related to privacy, security, fairness, transparency, and
consumer trust. Hence, data ethics has emerged as an important research area. This can
explore the major research trends in data ethics and their impact on commerce, management,
organizations, consumers, and society

5.Data ethics
Data ethics refers to the moral principles that guide how organizations collect, store,
process, analyze, and use data. Organizations now have access to unprecedented amounts of
information about customers, employees, patients, and citizens. While this dat a creates
opportunities for better services and smarter decisions, it also raises serious questions about
privacy, fairness, transparency, and accountability.
5.1(a) The 5 C’s of data ethics
The 5 C’s of data ethics are commonly described as Consent, Cla rity, Consistency,
Control, and Consequences. Together, these principles encourage organizations to collect and
use data responsibly while respecting user rights. They also help businesses build trust by
making data practices more transparent and accountable.
5.1 (b)Why Data Ethics Is Now a Career Skill
Five years ago, data ethics was a niche academic topic. Now it's showing up in job
descriptions, product reviews, and regulatory filings. Organizations that ignore it don't just
face fines. They face user  attrition, regulatory scrutiny, and the kind of press coverage that
doesn't go away.
For anyone building a career in data science, machine learning, or product management,
understanding data ethics isn't optional anymore. It's part of the job. Ethical judgment is what
makes you someone the organization actually trusts with real decisions. The field is evolving
fast. Frameworks considered rigorous three years ago are already being challenged by
practitioners who've seen how they play out in production. Tha t means the discipline is
maturing.  If you're serious about working with data responsibly, start with the principles,
understand the regulations relevant to your context, and build the habit of asking "what could
go wrong here" before asking "how do we build this faster."
5.2 IMPACT OF STUDY
It helps businesses manage risks associated with large -scale data collection and analysis.
Issues such as surveillance, reidentification, bias amplification, and function creep become
more significant as datasets gr ow. Ethical practices help organizations maintain customer
trust while reducing reputational and regulatory risks.
5.3 Data ethics are important  in artificial intelligence  Artificial intelligence systems learn
from historical data and can influence decisions at scale. Data ethics helps organizations
identify bias, improve transparency, and maintain accountability. Without ethical oversight,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 136

AI systems can unintentionally rei nforce discrimination or make decisions that negatively
affect individuals.
5.4 BIG TECH & ETHICS OF AI: A GROWING REGULATORY CHALLENGE
5.4(a) Digital monopoly and unfair competition: Allegations  against dominant AI firms
(Google, Microsoft, OpenAI) for  Anti-competitive practices such as control over data,
algorithms, and markets, suppresses smaller players and Violate principles of fairness, equal
opportunity, and distributive justice
5.4(b) Privacy and sensitive information : If sensitive information, such as unpublished
research, patient records, or Business documents, is uploaded, there is a risk of breach of
trust, lack of consent and violation of transparency Norms.  E.g. South Korea suspended new
downloads of the Chinese AI  app Deep Seek after the company admitted it, had not fully
complied with the country’s privacy rules.
  5.4 (c) Privatization of Public Knowledge:  Monopolistic firms turn.  freely available data
into proprietary products.
• Corporate Profit vs. Ethics: Pursuit of market dominance at the expense of creators’
rights and societal fairness Highlights lack of moral integrity in corporate practices.
• North-South Divide:  Global South creators’ works are often used without
safeguards, while Global North firms Dominate profits. This deepens digital colonialism
and economic inequality.
• UNESCO’s 2021 Recommendation on the Ethics of AI: These are first-ever global
standard on AI ethics. India has also adopted them.
• Other Global  Efforts: India is a party to the G20 AI Principles, 2023 Bletchley
Declaration of U.K. AI Summit, and 2025 Paris AI Action Summit’s joint statement on
Inclusive and Sustainable Artificial Intelligence for People and the Planet.
