EMERGING TRENDS OF AI IN FINANCE, BANKING AND INVESTMENT
S. Shyamala
 Assistant Professor
 Department of B. Com (Computer Applications)
 Sriram College of Arts and Science.
ABSTRACT
The rapid evolution of financial technology is fundamentally reshaping the global landscape,
driven directly by breakthroughs in digital banking systems and advanced artificial
intelligence. Seamless modern protocols like UPI and digital payments have expa nded retail
access to financial services, while cryptocurrency and digital assets have introduced new
models for value transfer and investment. At the same time, an increasingly interconnected
financial ecosystem creates new operational, cyber and systemic  vulnerabilities, making
proactive risk management and strong cybersecurity essential.  This paper examines how
technology-driven innovations are changing traditional banking and investment models. It
discusses artificial intelligence, machine learning, dig ital banking, UPI and digital payments,
blockchain, cryptocurrency and digital assets, crowdfunding, robo -advisory, alternative
investments, open banking, embedded finance and sustainable finance. It also considers the
risks associated with these developme nts, including cyber threats, data privacy, fraud, model
risk, financial exclusion and regulatory challenges.  The study highlights the need to balance
innovation with consumer protection, responsible use of data, financial stability and effective
regulation. The central theme is that the future of finance will depend not only on adopting
new technologies, but also on developing trustworthy systems, skilled human resources and
appropriate governance frameworks.
Keywords: FinTech, Digital Banking, Artificial Intelligence, Cryptocurrency and Digital
Assets, UPI and Digital Payments, Cybersecurity, Crowdfunding, Risk Management,
Alternative Investment, Blockchain, Robo-Advisory, Sustainable Finance.
1. INTRODUCTION
Finance is one of the most rapidly changing areas of the modern economy. Traditionally,
banking and investment depended heavily on physical branches, paper-based records, face-to-
face services and established financial intermediaries. The growth of the internet,
smartphones, cloud computing, data an alytics and artificial intelligence has changed this
structure. Financial services can now be delivered instantly through mobile applications,
digital payment systems, online investment platforms and automated advisory tools. Financial
technology, commonly called FinTech, refers to the use of technology to design, improve or
deliver financial products and services. FinTech is not limited to payments. It includes digital
lending, wealth management, insurance technology, blockchain applications, regulatory
technology, cybersecurity, financial data platforms and many other areas.  In India, the rapid
growth of digital payments and UPI has become a major example of this transformation.
Customers can transfer money, pay merchants and access financial services thro ugh mobile

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 141

devices. Banks and financial institutions increasingly use analytics and automation to
improve customer service, detect fraud and manage risk.  Investment is also changing.
Investors can access securities through online platforms, use automated p ortfolio tools,
explore alternative assets and obtain information almost instantly. However, technology also
creates new challenges. Cyberattacks, online fraud, privacy concerns, algorithmic bias,
volatile digital assets and technology concentration can af fect consumers and institutions.
Therefore, the study of emerging trends in finance, banking and investment requires both an
innovation perspective and a risk perspective. The objective is not merely to identify new
technologies, but to understand their ec onomic usefulness, limitations, regulatory
requirements and implications for the future.
2. OBJECTIVES
 To explain the meaning and importance of emerging trends in finance, banking and
investment.
 To examine the impact of FinTech on traditional financial institutions.
 To understand the growth of digital banking, UPI and digital payment systems.
 To study the applications of artificial intelligence and machine learning in financial
services.
 To introduce blockchain, cryptocurrency and digital assets and their financial
implications.
 To examine emerging investment methods such as robo -advisory, crowdfunding and
alternative investments.
 To understand major cybersecurity, fraud and operational risks in digital finance.
 To discuss regulatory, ethical and consumer -protection issues associated with financial
innovation.
 To identify opportunities and challenges for banks, investors, businesses and students.
 To provide a balanced view of the future direction of finance and financial services.
3. METHODOLOGY
The paper fo llows a descriptive and analytical approach. It draws on concepts from finance
and banking along with information from regulatory publications, institutional reports,
academic literature and publicly available financial technology resources.
SCOPE
The scope of this paper covers contemporary developments in financial services, with
particular attention to banking, payments, investment and financial technology. The
discussion includes both global trends and developments relevant to the Indian financia l
ecosystem.
4. EMERGING TRENDS IN BANKING
4.1 Digital Banking
Digital banking provides financial services through internet and mobile channels. Customers
can check balances, transfer funds, pay bills, apply for products and communicate with banks

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 142

without visiting a branch. Digital onboarding can reduce paperwork and improve
convenience, although identity verification and cybersecurity remain important.
4.2 Mobile Banking
Mobile banking has made smartphones an important access point for financial services.
Banking applications increasingly provide payments, card management, personal finance
information, investments and customer support in a single interface.
4.3 Open Banking and API-Based Services
Application Programming Interfaces, or APIs, allow different software systems to
communicate. Open banking frameworks can enable authorized sharing of financial
information between banks and third -party service providers. This can encourage innovation
and personalized financial services, but it also requires strong consent management,
authentication and data protection.
4.4 Embedded Finance
Embedded finance refers to financial products being integrated into non -financial digital
platforms. Examples include payment facilities in online shopping, financing at the point  of
sale and insurance offered within digital services. This trend can make financial services
more convenient but raises questions about transparency, suitability and responsibility.
4.5 Banking-as-a-Service
Banking-as-a-Service enables technology busines ses to offer selected banking or payment
functions using regulated financial infrastructure and APIs. It illustrates the movement from
traditional product distribution toward modular financial services.
4.6 Neobanks and Digital-First Institutions
Digital-first financial service providers use technology to deliver services without relying
primarily on traditional branch networks. Their value proposition often includes simplified
onboarding, mobile interfaces and personalized services. Their operations must n evertheless
comply with applicable financial and consumer-protection requirements.
5. EMERGING TRENDS IN INVESTMENT
 Investment has become more accessible because of online brokerage platforms, digital
advisory services, real -time information and mobile applications. Technology has
lowered some barriers to participation, although investors still need to understand risk,
costs, liquidity and suitability.
 Robo-advisory – automated systems can use investor information and predefined models
to provide portfolio suggestions or portfolio-management services.
 Exchange-traded and low -cost investment products – digital platforms have increased
access to diversified market instruments.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 143

 Alternative investments – investors increasingly explore private markets, infrast ructure,
real assets and other assets beyond conventional listed securities.
 Crowdfunding – online platforms can connect businesses or projects with many potential
funders, subject to the applicable legal framework.
 Digital assets – tokenized assets and cr yptocurrencies have created new categories of
financial and technological experimentation.
 Sustainable and responsible investing – environmental, social and governance
considerations are increasingly incorporated into investment analysis and product design.
 Fractional ownership models – technology can allow investors to gain exposure to certain
assets in smaller amounts, where legally permitted.
 The growing availability of information does not remove investment risk. Market
volatility, behavioral biases, misinformation, liquidity constraints and product complexity
remain relevant. Investors should distinguish between convenience of access and quality
of investment decisions.
6. ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE AND MACHINE LEARNING IN FINANCE
Artificial intellig ence (AI) and machine learning (ML) are among the most important
technological developments affecting finance. AI systems can process large volumes of data,
identify patterns and automate tasks. Financial institutions use such capabilities in areas
including customer support, fraud monitoring, credit assessment, risk management and
investment research.
 Fraud detection – machine-learning models can identify unusual transaction patterns for
further review.
 Credit assessment – alternative data and predictive m odels may support credit -risk
analysis, subject to responsible-use requirements.
 Customer service – AI assistants can handle routine queries and guide customers to
relevant services.
 Investment research – AI tools can summarize information, identify patter ns and support
analysts, while human oversight remains important.
 Risk management – models can assist in scenario analysis, monitoring and early -warning
systems.
 Process automation – repetitive back -office activities can be automated, improving
efficiency and reducing manual errors.
AI also creates risks. Poor -quality data can produce unreliable results. Models can contain
hidden biases or fail under changing market conditions. Generative AI may produce incorrect
information, and financial institutions must  manage confidentiality, explainability,
accountability and human oversight. Therefore, AI governance is becoming an important
component of modern financial management.
7. DIGITAL PAYMENTS AND UPI
Digital payments allow money to be transferred electronical ly without the physical exchange
of cash. In India, the Unified Payments Interface (UPI) has become a major part of the retail

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 144

payment ecosystem. It supports convenient account -to-account transactions through
participating in financial institutions and payment applications.
 Instant or near-instant payment experience for many retail transactions.
 QR-based merchant payments that can be used by small and large businesses.
 Person-to-person transfers using mobile applications.
 Integration of payment functionality into commerce and service platforms.
 Digital transaction records that can support reconciliation and financial management.
The expansion of digital payments also creates responsibilities. Users must protect
authentication credentials, verify payment requ ests and remain alert to social -engineering
fraud. Financial institutions need strong monitoring, customer education, secure infrastructure
and effective dispute-resolution mechanisms.
8. BLOCKCHAIN, CRYPTOCURRENCY AND DIGITAL ASSETS
Blockchain is a type of distributed ledger technology in which transactions are recorded
across a network according to a defined protocol. It can provide shared records and
programmable transactions without requiring every process to depend on a single database
administrator. Cryptocurrencies are digital assets that use cryptographic techniques and
distributed networks. Their prices can be highly volatile, and their legal and regulatory
treatment differs across jurisdictions. Digital assets can include a wider range of tokenize d
representations and blockchain-based instruments.
 Smart contracts – programmable rules can automatically execute certain transactions
when specified conditions are met.
 Tokenization – rights or claims related to assets can potentially be represented digitally.
 Cross-border experimentation – distributed networks may support new approaches to
value transfer.
 Decentralized finance (DeFi) – blockchain-based applications can offer financial
functions without traditional intermediaries, although they introduce significant technical,
legal and market risks.
The development of digital assets raises questions concerning investor protection, market
integrity, taxation, money laundering controls, consumer awareness, technology risk and
financial stability. The appropriate regulatory approach differs across countries and continues
to evolve.
9. CYBERSECURITY AND RISK MANAGEMENT
As finance becomes more digital, cybersecurity becomes a core financial -management issue.
Banks and FinTech companies hold valuable financial a nd personal information, making
them targets for cybercrime. A successful cyber incident can cause financial loss, service
disruption, reputational damage and loss of customer trust.
 Phishing and social engineering – attackers manipulate users into reveali ng credentials or
approving fraudulent transactions.
 Malware and ransomware – malicious software can disrupt systems or compromise
information.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 145

 Identity theft – stolen personal information can be misused to access accounts or obtain
financial services.
 Data breaches – unauthorized access to sensitive customer or institutional data can create
significant consequences.
 Third-party risk – financial institutions depend on technology vendors and cloud services,
creating interconnected operational dependencies.
Risk management therefore needs a technology -aware approach. Institutions should identify
critical systems, maintain access controls, use strong authentication, monitor transactions, test
incident-response plans, train employees and customers, and maintain business continuity
arrangements. Financial risk management also continues to include credit risk, market risk,
liquidity risk, operational risk and compliance risk. Technology can improve monitoring but
cannot eliminate uncertainty. Human judgment, governance and independent control remain
necessary.
10. CONCLUSION
Emerging trends in finance, banking and investment are transforming the way individuals,
businesses and financial institutions create, deliver and use financial services. Digital
banking, UPI, artificial intelligence, blockchain , digital assets, robo -advisory, crowdfunding
and alternative investments illustrate the breadth of this transformation.  The benefits of
financial innovation include greater convenience, faster transactions, improved data analysis,
new business models and wider access to financial services. However, these benefits are
accompanied by cybersecurity threats, privacy concerns, operational dependencies,
investment risks, regulatory complexity and ethical questions.  The future of finance will
therefore depend on achieving a practical balance between innovation and responsibility.
Financial institutions must invest in secure technology, skilled human resources, effective
governance and customer protection. Regulators must encourage useful innovation while
addressing systemic and consumer risks. Investors and customers must also develop digital
and financial literacy.  In conclusion, technology is not replacing the fundamental principles
of finance; rather, it is changing the way those principles are applied. Sound fi nancial
management, risk awareness, transparency, accountability and trust remain essential even as
the tools and platforms of finance continue to evolve.
11. REFERENCES

financial technology, digital payments and financial stability.
 National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI). Information and resources relating to

 International Monetary Fund (IMF). Research and publications on financial technology,
digital money, financial stability and digital finance.
 Bank for International Settlements (BIS). Research and reports on technology, banking,
payments, digital finance and financial regulation.
 World Bank. Resources on digital financial servic es, financial inclusion and digital
economy developments.

ONLINE REVIEWS AND CONSUMER PURCHASE DECISIONS
Ashwitha. L
I BCOM (CS)
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
MS. S. Padmavathy
 Assistant professor
 Department of corporate secretaryship
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
Abstract
The rapid growth of e -commerce has significantly changed the way consumers search for
information and make purchase decisions. Online reviews hav e become an important source
of information for consumers before purchasing products and services through digital
platforms. This study examines the relationship between online reviews and consumer
purchase decisions using secondary data. The study analyse s published research articles,
industry reports, e-commerce reports, and other reliable secondary sources to understand how
review ratings, review volume, review content, credibility, and positive or negative feedback
influence consumer purchase behavior. The study also reviews the changing role of online
reviews across different product categories and digital platforms. The findings from the
secondary evidence indicate that consumers generally consider online reviews and ratings
when evaluating product qua lity, reliability, and value before making a purchase. Positive
and credible reviews can contribute to consumer confidence, while negative reviews may
influence consumers to reconsider their purchase decisions. The study concludes that online
reviews have become an important component of digital consumer decision -making and have
significant implications for businesses seeking to build consumer trust and improve their
online presence.
Keywords: Online Reviews, Consumer Purchase Decision, Consumer Behavior, E -
Commerce, Online Shopping, Digital Marketing, Consumer Trust.
INTRODUCTION
The rapid development of information and communication technology has transformed the
traditional marketplace into a digital marketplace. Consumers can now search for products,
compare prices, read product information, evaluate alternatives and make purchases without
physically visiting a store. The expansion of e -commerce has therefore increased the
importance of digital information in consumer decision -making. One of the most infl uential
forms of digital information is online consumer review. Online reviews are opinions,
experiences, ratings and comments shared by consumers about products, services, sellers or
brands on digital platforms. They can be found on e -commerce websites, s ocial media
platforms, review websites and other digital channels.  Before making an online purchase,
consumers often look at star ratings, written reviews, photographs, videos and the
experiences of previous buyers. These reviews can provide information th at may not be fully

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 147

available through advertisements or product descriptions. Consequently, online reviews can
influence consumers’ perceptions of product quality and their level of confidence in
purchasing a product. The importance of online reviews is pa rticularly significant because
consumers face uncertainty when purchasing products online. In a physical store, consumers
may be able to inspect a product directly, communicate with sales personnel and physically
evaluate the product. Online shopping does not provide the same level of direct product
experience. Reviews can partially reduce this information gap by providing experience from
previous purchasers. Online reviews can influence different stages of the consumer decision -
making process. During the i nformation-search stage, consumers may use reviews to
understand product features and performance. During the evaluation stage, reviews can help
consumers compare alternatives. At the purchase stage, favorable reviews may increase
confidence, while negativ e reviews may discourage purchases. However, not every online
review has the same influence. The number of reviews, average rating, review quality,
reviewer credibility, recency and consistency of information can affect how consumers
interpret reviews. Consumers may also question reviews when they suspect that they are fake,
biased or commercially manipulated.  Therefore, understanding the relationship between
online reviews and consumer purchase decisions is important for both researchers and
businesses. Th is study analyses existing secondary evidence relating to online reviews and
consumer purchase decisions and discusses their implications for e-commerce businesses.
STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM
The growth of online shopping has resulted in a large amount of co nsumer-generated
information. Consumers are exposed to thousands of reviews and ratings while shopping
online. Although these reviews can assist consumers in making informed decisions, the large
volume of information can also create confusion. Another important concern is the credibility
of online reviews. Consumers may encounter exaggerated, misleading or fake reviews.
Negative reviews may also have a stronger psychological impact than positive reviews in
certain situations. Businesses therefore face the c hallenge of managing online reputation
while maintaining transparency. The present study attempts to examine how existing research
and secondary evidence explain the relationship between online reviews and consumer
purchase decisions.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
The major objectives of the study are:
 To understand the concept and importance of online consumer reviews.
 To examine the role of online reviews in consumer purchase decisions.
 To analyze the influence of review ratings and review volume on consumer behavior.
 To examine the importance of review credibility and authenticity.
 To study the influence of positive and negative online reviews on purchase decisions.
 To identify the implications of online reviews for e-commerce businesses.
 To identify major challenges associated with online consumer reviews.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 148

LITERATURE REVIEW
 Pocchiari, Proserpio and Dover (2025)  conducted a comprehensive literature review of
approximately 200 studies on online reviews. They organized the literature into four
stages: review cre ation, engagement, impact and insight. The study found that online
reviews can influence consumer choices, product demand and firm reputation, while fake
reviews, behavioral biases and promotional reviews can reduce their reliability. The
authors also high lighted the growing importance of text analysis and emerging
technologies for extracting consumer insights from reviews.

 Wang et al. (2025) examined approximately 1.6 million Amazon reviews to understand
review helpfulness. Their findings indicate that c onsumers do not evaluate reviews in
isolation; instead, they compare review content with product descriptions and other
reviews. The study suggests that differences in topics and language can affect perceived
helpfulness, with the effect varying according to product price.

 Pooja and Upadhyaya (2024) s ystematically reviewed 69 empirical studies concerning
online-review credibility. They identified five broad groups of factors affecting
credibility: source characteristics, review characteristics, consumer c haracteristics,
interpersonal factors on social -media platforms and product type. Their findings
emphasize that consumers assess the credibility of reviews before relying on them for
purchase decisions

 Kumar, Mukherjee and Rana (2024)  examined fake review s and their role in
influencing buying decisions. Their research highlights that consumers may be influenced
by manipulated or fake reviews, making authenticity an important issue in understanding
online-review-based purchase behavior
RESEARCH QUESTIONS
The study addresses the following questions:
 How do online reviews influence consumer purchase decisions?
 What characteristics of online reviews are important to consumers?
 Does review rating influence consumer purchase intention?
 How does the number of reviews affect consumer confidence?
 Why is review credibility important in online shopping?
 What challenges are associated with online consumer reviews?
SCOPE OF THE STUDY
The study focuses on online reviews and their relationship with consumer purchase de cisions
in the context of e-commerce and online shopping.
The study covers:

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 149

 Online product reviews
 Star ratings
 Positive and negative reviews
 Review volume
 Review credibility
 Consumer trust
 Purchase intention
 Online shopping behavior
 E-commerce platforms
The study is based entirely on secondary sources and does not involve direct collection of
responses from consumers.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
Research Design
The study adopts a descriptive and analytical research design based on secondary data.
Sources of Data
The study uses secondary information collected from:
 Research articles
 Academic journals
 Books
 Government publications
 Industry reports
 E-commerce reports
 Institutional publications
 Published research studies
 Relevant websites and databases
Method of Analysis
The collected literature and secondary information are analyzed using:
 Descriptive analysis
 Comparative analysis
 Literature synthesis
 Trend interpretation
 No primary questionnaire data have been used in this study.
Period of Study
The study reviews relevant literature and publishes information relating to online reviews and
consumer behavior, with emphasis on recent developments in e-commerce.
CONCEPTUAL FRAMEWORK
Concept of Online Reviews
An online review is a consumer -generated evaluation of a product, service, brand or seller
that is published through a digital platform. Reviews may contain written comments,
numerical ratings, photographs, videos and other forms of feedback.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 150

Online reviews are a form of electronic word -of-mouth (eWOM). Unlike traditional word-of-
mouth communication, online reviews can reach a large number of consumers and remain
available for an extended period.  Reviews can provide consumers with practical information
about:
 Product quality
 Product performance
 Durability
 Value for money
 Seller reliability
 Delivery experience
 Customer service
 Product usability
Thus, online reviews can act as an information source during the consumer decision -making
process.
Consumer Purchase Decision
Consumer purchase decisions refer to the proces s through which consumers identify a need,
search for information, evaluate alternatives, make a purchase and assess the outcome.
The traditional consumer decision-making process generally includes:
Need Recognition → Information Search → Evaluation of Alt ernatives → Purchase
Decision → Post-Purchase Evaluation
Online reviews can influence several stages of this process.
For example, a consumer looking for a smartphone may compare different models by
examining product specifications, prices, ratings and customer reviews. Reviews may provide
information about battery life, camera quality, software performance and actual user
experience. This information can influence the consumer’s evaluation of alternatives.
Role of Online Reviews in Purchase Decisions
Online reviews can perform several important functions in the consumer decision -making
process.
 Information Function
Reviews provide additional information beyond the product description. Consumers can learn
from the experiences of previous purchasers.
 Risk Reduction
Online shopping involves uncertainty because consumers cannot always physically examine
products before purchase. Reviews may reduce perceived risk by providing evidence from
other consumers.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 151

 Trust Building
Reviews can increase consumer confidenc e when they appear genuine, detailed and
consistent.
 Product Evaluation
Consumers can compare products using ratings and review comments. Products with
consistently positive feedback may receive greater consideration.
 Purchase Intention
Positive reviews ca n contribute to favorable attitudes towards a product and may increase
purchase intention.
 Post-Purchase Information
Reviews also help consumers evaluate whether their expectations were met and provide
feedback to future buyers.
Major Characteristics of Online Reviews
 Review Rating
Star ratings provide a quick summary of customer opinions. Consumers may use average
ratings as an initial indicator of product quality.
 Review Volume
The number of reviews can influence perceptions of product popularity and mark et
acceptance. A product with a large number of reviews may attract more attention than a
product with very few reviews.
 Review Valence
Review valence refers to whether the review expresses a positive, negative or neutral opinion.
Positive reviews can strengthen favorable perceptions, whereas negative reviews can identify
potential problems
 Review Quality
Detailed reviews may be more useful than short comments because they provide specific
information about the consumer’s experience.
 Review Credibility
Consumers are more likely to rely on reviews when they perceive the reviewer and the
information as credible.
 Review Recency
Recent reviews may be considered more relevant because product quality, sellers, delivery
systems and product versions can change over time.
ANALYSIS AND DISCUSSION
Based on the review of secondary literature, online reviews have become an important source
of information for consumers in digital marketplaces.  The secondary literature indicates that
consumers do not necessarily depend on one factor alone. Instead, they often consider

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 152

multiple characteristics simultaneously. For example, a product with a high rating but only a
small number of reviews may be evalu ated differently from a product with a slightly lower
rating but thousands of detailed reviews. Similarly, consumers may give greater attention to
negative reviews when the review identifies a serious problem related to product quality or
reliability
Positive Reviews and Consumer Purchase Decisions
Positive reviews can create favorable perceptions of a product. Consumers may interpret
positive experiences from previous customers as an indication that the product is likely to
satisfy their own needs.
 Positive reviews can contribute to:
 Higher product confidence
 Lower perceived risk
 Greater trust
 Better product evaluation
 Higher purchase intention
However, excessive positive reviews without sufficient detail may not always be perceived as
credible. Therefore, authenticity and information quality are important.
Negative Reviews and Consumer Purchase Decisions
Negative reviews provide consumers with information about potential weaknesses. They may
discuss:
 Poor product quality
 Delayed delivery
 Defective products
 Unsatisfactory customer service
Review Credibility and Consumer Trust
Credibility is one of the most important aspects of online reviews. Consumers need to
determine whether a review represents genuine customer experience.
Factors that may increase perceived credibility include:
 Detailed explanations
 Verified purchase indicators
 Balanced opinions
 Supporting photographs or videos
 Consistency across multiple reviews
 Reviewer history
 Recent customer experiences

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 153

The presence of suspicious or repetitive reviews can reduce consumer trust in an online
platform.
Fake Reviews: A Major Challenge
The growth of online reviews has also created opportunities for manipulation. Fake reviews
may be generated to artificially increase or decrease perceptions of a product or business.
Fake reviews can create several problems:
 Mislead consumers.
 Damage consumer trust.
 Create unfair competition.
 Genuine businesses affect.
 Reduce the reliability of online platforms.
Therefore, e-commerce platforms need effective systems to identify  suspicious reviews and
maintain transparency.
Implications for E-Commerce Businesses
Businesses can use online reviews as an important source of market information.
 Encourage Genuine Feedback
Businesses should encourage genuine customers to provide honest  feedback rather than
attempting to generate artificial positive reviews.
 Respond to Negative Reviews
Professional responses to complaints can demonstrate customer orientation and provide an
opportunity to resolve problems.
 Improve Product Quality
Repeated negative comments can help businesses identify weaknesses in products or services.
 Monitor Online Reputation
Businesses should regularly monitor ratings and customer comments across digital platforms.
 Maintain Transparency
Businesses should avoid practices that manipulate ratings or create misleading reviews.
FINDINGS OF THE STUDY
Based on the secondary literature reviewed, the major findings are:
 Online reviews are an important source of information for online consumers.
 Consumers use reviews to reduce uncertainty associated with online purchases.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 154

 Review ratings can influence consumers’ initial evaluation of products.
 The number of reviews can affect perceptions of product popularity and experience.
 Positive reviews can contribute to favorable product perceptions.
 Negative reviews can highlight potential risks and weaknesses.
 Review credibility is important for developing consumer trust.
 Detailed reviews may provide greater information value than very short reviews.
 Consumers may consider multiple reviews rather than relying on a single review.
 Fake and manipulated reviews represent an important challenge for digital marketplaces.
 Businesses can use customer feedback to identify areas for improvement.
 Effective review management can support consumer confidence and online reputation.
SUGGESTIONS
Based on the study, the following suggestions are made:
 E-commerce platforms should strengthen systems for identifying fake reviews
 Consumers should evaluate multiple reviews instead of depending on a single review
 Consumers should consider both positive and negative feedback.
 Businesses should encourage genuine customer feedback.
 Businesses should respond constructively to negative reviews.
 Review platforms should provide greater transparency regarding review authenticity.
 Consumers should consider the recency and relevance of reviews.
 Businesses should use repeated customer complaints to improve product and service
quality.
 E-commerce platforms should provide clear mechanisms for reporting suspicious
reviews.
 Digital consumers should develop greater awareness of online review manipulation
CONCLUSION
Online reviews have become an important component of modern consumer decision -making.
The growth of e -commerce has increased consumers’ dependence on digital infor mation
when evaluating products and services. Reviews can provide information about product
quality, performance, reliability and customer experience that may not be available through
conventional advertisements.

GROWTH OF ONLINE SHOPPING IN INDIA: AN OVERVIEW
Sharanya
 I B.COM (CS)
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
MS. S. Padmavathy
 Assistant professor, Department of corporate secretaryship
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
ABSTRACT:
    Online shopping has emerged as an important component of the Indian retail sector,
transforming the way consumers purchase goods and services. The rapid growth of internet
penetration, smartphone usage, digital payment systems, improved logistics, and e-commerce
platforms has contributed significantly to the expansion of online shopping in India. This
study analyses the growth and development of online shopping in India using secondary data
collected from reliable sources such as government reports, ind ustry reports, research
publications, and publicly available statistical databases. The study examines trends in online
retail sales, internet and smartphone adoption, digital payments, consumer participation, and
the expansion of ecommerce. The analysis h ighlights the major factors contributing to the
growth of online shopping, including convenience, wider product availability, competitive
pricing, discounts, doorstep delivery, and increasing digital literacy. At the same time,
challenges such as cybersecu rity concerns, product quality issues, delivery problems, return
policies, and the digital divide continue to affect consumer participation. The study concludes
that online shopping has significant growth potential in India and is likely to play an
increasingly important role in the country’s retail economy.
keywords:  Online Shopping, E -Commerce, India, Consumer Behavior, Digital Payments,
Internet Penetration, Online Retail, Secondary Data Analysis, E -Commerce Growth, Digital

INTRODUCTION
The development of information and communication technology has significantly changed
the way businesses operate, and consumers purchase products and services. One of the major
developments in the retail sector is the growth of online shopping. Online shopping refers to
the purchase of products or services through internet -based platforms using computers,
smartphones or other digital devices. In India, online shopping has grown rapidly because of
increasing internet access, smartphone penetration, digit al literacy, affordable data services
and the expansion of digital payment systems. E -commerce platforms have made it possible
for consumers to purchase products from different locations without physically visiting a
store. The Indian retail market has traditionally been dominated by physical stores. However,
the expansion of e -commerce has created an additional channel for consumers and
businesses. Online shopping provides consumers with convenience, wider product choices,
price comparisons and home delive ry facilities. The development of digital payment systems
has also contributed to the growth of online shopping. Consumers can use UPI, debit cards,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 157

credit cards, internet banking and digital wallets to complete transactions. The increasing use
of digital payments has made online transactions faster and easier.  The COVID-19 pandemic
further increased the importance of online shopping as restrictions on movement and
temporary closure of physical stores encouraged consumers to purchase products through
digital platforms. Although physical retail recovered after the pandemic, many consumers
continued using online channels because of their convenience. Therefore, analyzing the
growth of online shopping is important for understanding changes in the Indian retail sector
and consumer behavior.
STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM
The rapid development of online shopping has transformed the Indian retail environment.
Consumers increasingly use online platforms for purchasing both essential and non -essential
products. At the same time, traditional retailers are facing increased competition from digital
platforms. The growth of online shopping is influenced by several factors, including internet
access, smartphone usage, digital payments, consumer awareness, product variety,
convenience and promotional offers. Despite its rapid development, online shopping also
faces several challenges. These include cybersecurity risks, privacy concerns, fake reviews,
counterfeit products, delivery delays, product quality issues and return and refu nd problems.
Therefore, the present study attempts to analyze the growth of online shopping in India using
secondary data and to identify the major factors and challenges associated with its
development.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
The major objectives of the study are
 To study the growth of online shopping in India.
 To examine the factors responsible for the growth of online shopping.
 To study the relationship between internet and digital technology and online shopping
growth.
 To understand changing consumer preferences towards online shopping.
 To examine the role of digital payments in online shopping.
 To identify the major challenges associated with online shopping.
 To suggest measures for sustainable development of online shopping in India.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
 Research Design
The study follows descriptive and analytical research design. It examines the development of
online shopping in India using information obtained from secondary sources.
 Nature of Data
The study is based entirely on secondary data. No primary survey was conducted.

CONCEPT OF ONLINE SHOPPING
Online shopping is the process of purchasing products or services through internet -based
platforms. Consumers can search for products, compare prices, read product descriptions and
reviews, make payments and receive products at their preferred location.
Online shopping can be carried out through:
- E-commerce websites
- Mobile applications
- Social commerce platforms
- Online marketplaces
- Brand websites
- Quick-commerce platforms
The majo r difference between traditional shopping and online shopping is that online
shopping allows the consumer to complete most stages of the purchasing process digitally.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 159

GROWTH OF ONLINE SHOPPING IN INDIA
India has experienced substantial growth in digital co mmerce. The expansion has been
supported by several technological and economic developments.
Major growth drivers include:
 Growth of Internet Users
Increasing access to the internet has expanded the potential customer base for online
businesses.
 Smartphone Penetration
Smartphones have made online shopping accessible to consumers across different age groups
and geographical areas.
 Affordable Mobile Data
The availability of relatively affordable mobile internet has encouraged consumers to use
online platforms regularly.
 Digital Payments
UPI, cards, internet banking and mobile wallets have made online transactions more
convenient.
 Wider Product Choice
Online platforms provide access to products from different brands and sellers in one place.
 Convenience
Consumers can shop from home at any time without travelling to physical stores.
 Discounts and Promotional Offers
Online platforms frequently use discounts, cashback, coupons and seasonal sales to attract
consumers.
 Home Delivery
Doorstep delivery has made online shopping particularly convenient for consumers.
MAJOR CATEGORIES OF ONLINE SHOPPING
Online shopping in India covers a wide range of products and services.
 Fashion and Apparel
Clothing, footwear, accessories and beauty products are important categories in o nline
shopping.
 Electronics
Mobile phones, computers, televisions, accessories and other electronic products are
frequently purchased online.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 160

 Grocery and Household Products
Online grocery platforms have expanded significantly, particularly in urban areas.
 Beauty and Personal Care
Cosmetics, skincare, haircare and personal-care products are increasingly purchased online.
 Books and Educational Products
Books, stationery and educational materials can be easily purchased through online
platforms.
 Food and Restaurant Services
Online food-ordering platforms have changed the way consumers purchase prepared food.
 Travel and Other Services
Travel bookings, hotel reservations, entertainment and other services are also increasingly
accessed online.
FACTORS RESPONSIBLE FOR THE GROWTH OF ONLINE SHOPPING
 Convenience
Convenience is one of the most important advantages of online shopping. Consumers can
purchase products from their homes at any time.
 Price Comparison
Online platforms allow consumers to compare prices offered by different sellers.
 Product Variety
Consumers can access products from different brands and sellers through a single platform.
 Digital Payment Infrastructure
The development of UPI and other digital payment systems has simplified online
transactions.
 Internet and Smartphone Usage
The growth of smartphones and internet connectivity has expanded access to e-commerce.
 Discounts
Online platforms use discounts and promotional campaigns to attract consumers.
 Consumer Reviews
Product ratings and reviews provide consumers with additional information before
purchasing a product.
 Improved Logistics
The expansion of delivery networks has enabled e -commerce companies to reach consumers
in more locations.
ROLE OF DIGITAL PAYMENTS IN ONLINE SHOPPING
Digital payments hav e become an important part of the online shopping ecosystem.
Consumers can make payments through:

Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 161

- UPI
- Debit cards
- Credit cards
- Internet banking
- Mobile wallets
- Cash on delivery
Among these methods, UPI has significantly simplified person -to-merchant and online
transactions.
Digital payments provide several benefits:
 Faster transactions
 Reduced dependence on cash
 Easy transaction tracking
 Convenient mobile payments
 Greater accessibility
 Integration with e-commerce applications
The growth of digital payment infrastructure has therefore supported the expansion of online
shopping.
CHANGING CONSUMER BEHAVIOUR
Online shopping has changed traditional consumer behaviour.
Consumers increasingly:
- Search for products online before purchasing.
- Compare prices across platforms.
- Read customer reviews.
- Watch product demonstration videos.
- Look for discounts and offers.
- Use digital payment methods.
- Expect faster delivery.
- Purchase products through mobile applications.
The consumer decision-making process has therefore become more information -oriented and
digitally influenced.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 162

ROLE OF ONLINE REVIEWS
Online reviews are an important source of information for consumers. Consumers often read
ratings and reviews before purchasing unfamiliar products.  Positive reviews may increase
consumer confidence, while negative reviews may encourage consumers to reconsider a
purchase. However, fake reviews and manipulated ratings can reduce consumer trust. E -
commerce companies therefore need effective systems to id entify fraudulent reviews and
protect consumers.
IMPACT OF COVID-19 ON ONLINE SHOPPING
The COVID -19 pandemic created significant changes in consumer purchasing behaviour.
During periods of restrictions, consumers increasingly relied on online platforms for
groceries, medicines, household products, food and other goods.  The pandemic accelerated
digital adoption among consumers and businesses. Small businesses also explored online
channels to continue reaching customers.  Although physical retail activity incr eased again
after restrictions were removed, the pandemic contributed to the development of long -term
digital shopping habits.
CHALLENGES OF ONLINE SHOPPING IN INDIA
Despite its rapid growth, online shopping faces several challenges.
 Cybersecurity
Online transactions can expose consumers to fraud, phishing and other cyber risks.
 Privacy Concerns
Consumers may be concerned about the collection and use of personal information.
 Product Quality
Consumers cannot physically inspect products before purchase.
 Fake Reviews
Fake ratings and reviews can influence consumer decisions.
 Counterfeit Products
The presence of counterfeit products can reduce consumer trust.
 Delivery Problems
Delayed delivery, damaged products and incorrect deliveries can negatively affect cus tomer
satisfaction.
 Return and Refund Issues
Complicated return policies can discourage consumers.
 Digital Divide
Consumers with limited internet access or digital literacy may not fully benefit from online
shopping.
SECONDARY DATA ANALYSIS
Secondary data analysis can be used to understand the development of India’s digital -
commerce ecosystem.

INTERPRETATION:
The broad development of India’s digital infrastructure has created favorable conditions for
the expansion of online shopping. Internet access, smartphones, digital payments and
logistics are interconnected components of the online-shopping ecosystem.
FINDINGS OF THE STUDY
 The major findings of the study are:
 Online shopping has become an important component of India’s retail sector.
 Increasing internet access has expanded the online consumer base.
 Smartphone usage has contributed significantly to mobile shopping.
 Digital payments have simplified online transactions.
 Convenience is a major factor encouraging online shopping.
 Consumers benefit from wider product choices and price comparisons.
 Online reviews influence consumer purchasing decisions.
 The COVID-19 pandemic accelerated online-shopping adoption.
 Improved logistics and delivery networks have supported market expansion.
 Rural and semi-urban markets provide further opportunities for online businesses.
 Cybersecurity and privacy remain important concerns.
 Fake reviews and counterfeit products can affect consumer trust.
 Delivery and return-related problems remain challenges.
 Digital literacy is important for safe and effective online shopping.
SUGGESTIONS
Based on the study, the following suggestions are made:

 E-commerce companies should strengthen cybersecurity systems.
 Consumer personal information should be protected through appropriate security
measures.
 Platforms should improve mechanisms for detecting fake reviews.
 Sellers should provide accurate product descriptions and images.
 Return and refund procedures should be simple and transparent.
 Delivery infrastructure should be improved in rural and semi-urban areas.
 Consumers should be educated about online fraud and cybersecurity.
 E-commerce platforms should provide effective customer support.
 Measures should be taken to reduce the sale of counterfeit products.
 Digital literacy programmes should be strengthened to encourage safe online transactions.
LIMITATIONS OF THE STUDY
 The study is based only on secondary data.

CONCLUSION
Online shopping has emerged as an important component of India’s rapidly developing
digital economy. The growth of internet connectivity, smartphones, digital payments, e -
commerce platforms and logistics infrastructure has created a favorable environment for
online retail. Consumers increasingly value convenience, product variety, price comparison,
home delivery and digital payment facilities. At the same time, challenges such as
cybersecurity, privacy, counterfeit products, fake reviews, delivery issues and return -related
problems need to be addressed. The future development of online shopping depends not only
on technological expansion but also on consumer trus t, digital literacy, secure transactions,
efficient logistics and responsible business practices. With continued development of India’s
digital infrastructure, online shopping is expected to remain an important channel in the
Indian retail sector.

ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN TAXATION AND GST: AUTOMATED
COMPLIANCE, FRAUD DETECTION, AND INPUT TAX CREDIT
OPTIMIZATION
E. Gayathri
 III BCOM (CS)
Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
ABSTRACT
The integration of Artificial in telligence (AI) and machine Learning (ML) into corporate
finance and tax administration has fundamentally reshaped indirect taxation systems
particularly within the Goods and services tax GST framework. As commercial transactions
volume grows exponentially  across digital networks, corporate secretaries and compliance
officers face significant challenges in managing complex regulatory requirements, tax
reconciliation, and risk exposure. This paper investigations the strategic development of AI
technologies across the GST lifecycle, evaluating how automatic analytics, natural language
processing and graph algorithms transform corporate tax management from a reactive
administrative chore into a proactive, real - time risk mitigation discipline. By developing
machine learning models and the GST lifecycle management optical character Recognition
(OCR), tax authorities and corporate compliance teams can be used reconciliation e -invoices,
e-Way bills, and statutory return filings such as GSTR1, GSTR -3B and GSTR -2B.
Advanced network graph enables the idea of inputting tax credit pass through chains, circular
trading schemes and existing shell companies before revenue or leakage of legal penalty
occurs. Furthermore, AI models assist corporate decision makers in vendor s compliance
ratings, credit claims and the statutory default risks. Despite the operational efficiency the
adoption of AI in taxation presents, corporate governance challenges regarding data privacy,
model explain detail statutory compliances under tax re gulations. The study concludes by the
role of corporate governance professionals in establishing human the loop controls
framework that the balance AI administration automatic with the legal ethics and regulations
accountability.
KEY WORDS: GST, Artificial intelligence, Input tax, credit Tax and corporate governance.
INTRODUCTION
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is one of the most important technological developments of the
modern business world. It refers to the use of computer systems and software that can
perform tasks that normally require human intelligence, such as learning, analyzing data,
identifying patterns and making predictions. Taxation involves the collection of revenue by
the government from individuals and businesses. In India, the introduction of the Goods and
Services Tax (GST) created a technology -based tax administration system. GST generates a
large amount of digital information through invoices, returns, e -way bills, e -invoices and
payments. Artificial Intelligence can help tax authorities analyze this large volume of
information quickly and accurately. AI -based systems can identify unusual transactions,
detect possible tax evasion, improve compliance and support faster decision-making.

Commerce and Management

OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
 To understand the concept of Artificial intelligence (AI) and its role in taxation.
  To study the applications of AI in GST administration.
 To examine the role of AI in detecting tax fraud and tax evasion.
 To understand how AI helps with analytics data and transactions.
 To study role of AI improving tax compliance
 To identify the benefits of AI in taxation such as saving time, reducing errors and
improving efficiency.
REVIEW OF LITERATURE
OECD – Tax Administration 3.0 OECD publications explain how digital technologies; data
analytics and Artificial Intelligence can transform tax administration. These technologies can
support better compliance, risk management and taxpayer services. Goods and Services Tax
Network (GSTN).  GSTN provides information on India's technology -based GST system .
GST registration, return filing, e -invoicing, e -way bills and other digital services generate
large amounts of data that can support data -driven tax administration. Central Board of
Indirect Taxes and Customs (CBIC) CBIC publications provide information about GST
administration, compliance and measures used to identify tax evasion. Digital information
and data analysis can assist tax authorities in identifying irregular transactions.   GST Council
Reports and publications of the GST Council provide informa tion about GST policy,
administration and developments in the Indian GST system. These sources are useful for
understanding the changing technological environment of GST. Ministry of Finance,
Government of India Government reports discuss digitalization of  tax administration and the
increasing use of technology for improving tax compliance, transparency and revenue
administration. Taxman.  Publications Axmann books and reference materials provide
detailed information about GST law, compliance procedures, inp ut tax credit, tax
administration and taxation practices in India. They provide the conceptual background
required to study the application of AI in GST.
SUMMARY OF LITERATURE:
The literature indicates that AI and advanced data analytics can support mode rn tax
administration by analyzing large volumes of information, identifying unusual transaction
patterns, supporting risk -based compliance and improving taxpayer services. At the same
time, literature highlights concerns relating to data privacy, cybersec urity, data quality,
transparency and human oversight. Therefore, the existing literature provides a useful
foundation for studying how Artificial Intelligence can be applied to taxation and GST in
India.

digital technologies in improving tax administration and GST compliance.

IMPORTANCE OF AI IN TAXATION AND GST
 Improves efficiency – AI can process large amounts of tax data quickly.
 Fraud detection – It can identify unusual transaction patterns and potential tax fraud.
 Improves GST compliance  – AI can help identify discrepancies in GST returns and
invoices.
 ITC verification  – AI can assist in analyzing Input Tax Credit claims and matching
transaction data.
 Reduces manual work – Routine data analysis can be automated.
 Better decision -making – Tax authorities can use data -based insights for risk
assessment.
 Improves taxpayer services – AI chatbots can provide quick answers to common GST
queries.
 Revenue protection – Better identification of  suspicious transactions can support
effective tax administration.
CHALLENGES
 Data privacy – Taxpayer information is sensitive and must be protected.
 Cybersecurity risks – Digital tax systems may face cyber threats.
 Data quality – Incorrect or incomplete data can affect AI results.
 High implementation cost – Developing and maintaining AI systems can require
substantial resources.
 Lack of skilled professionals – Employees need training in AI and data analytics.
 False identification – AI may flag genuine transactions as suspicious.
 Lack of transparency – Some AI-based processes may be difficult to understand.
OPPORTUNITIES
 Advanced fraud detection – AI can identify complex patterns across large datasets.
 Predictive analysis – AI can help identify emerging compliance risks.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

 Automated compliance monitoring – Routine GST compliance checks can be
supported by AI.
 Improved taxpayer support – AI-based virtual assistants can provide faster assistance.
 Real-time data analysis – Digital GST information can be analysed more efficiently.
 Better risk management – Resources can be focused on transactions requiring further
examination.
 Integration of tax data – Different sources of digital tax information can be analysed
together.
 Future digital transformation – AI can support the development of more technology -
driven tax administration.
RECOMMENDATIONS
 Improve Data Quality:
Accurate, complete and consistent GST data should be maintained because AI analysis
depends on the quality of available data.
 Provide Training for Tax Officials:
Tax officials should receive regular training in Artificial Intelligence, data analytics and
digital tax systems.
 Maintain Human Oversight:
AI should be used as a supporting tool. Important tax decisions should in clude appropriate
human review and verification.
 Improve AI-Based Fraud Detection:
AI and data analytics should be continuously improved to identify suspicious transactions,
fake invoices and unusual ITC claims.
 Increase Taxpayer Awareness:
Taxpayers and businesses should be educated about GST digital services, compliance
requirements and the responsible use of technology.
 Ensure Transparency:
Where AI -supported systems flag transactions or create risk indicators, suitable
explanations and review mechanisms should be available.
 Regularly Update AI Systems:
AI models and software should be regularly updated to respond to changing tax rules,
fraud patterns and technological developments.
 Protect Taxpayer Privacy:
Personal and financial information sh ould be collected and processed only for legitimate
tax-administration purposes, with appropriate privacy safeguards.
 Encourage Responsible Use of AI:
AI should be implemented in a way that promotes efficiency, fairness, transparency,
security and Strengthen Data Security
CONCLUSION
Based on the secondary data collected from sources such as GSTN, CBIC, GST Council,
Ministry of Finance, OECD reports, Tax man publications, research papers and official

government websites, the study concludes that Art ificial Intelligence has significant potential
to improve taxation and GST administration in India.  AI can help in analyzing large volumes
of GST data, identifying unusual transactions, detecting potential tax evasion, analyzing Input
Tax Credit claims, mo nitoring compliance and improving taxpayer services. The use of
digital information such as GST returns, invoices and e -way bills provides opportunities for
more efficient and data -driven tax administration.  The secondary data also highlights
challenges such as data privacy, cybersecurity, data accuracy, implementation costs, lack of
skilled professionals and the possibility of incorrect AI alerts. Therefore, AI -based systems
should be supported by appropriate human review, transparency and security measure s.
Overall, the study concludes that Artificial Intelligence can support a faster, more efficient
and technology -driven taxation system, provided it is implemented responsibly. A
combination of AI technology, reliable data and human expertise can help stre ngthen GST
compliance and improve the effectiveness of tax administration in India.

IMPACT OF UPI ON THE INDIAN BANKING SECTOR: AN OVERVIEW
MS. S. Padmavathy
 Assistant professor, Department of corporate Secretaryship
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
Keerthika. T
I BCOM (CS)
Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
Abstract:
The Unified Payments Interface (UPI) has emerged as one of the most significant
developments in India's digital payment ecosystem. Introduced by the National Payments
Corporation of India (NPCI), UPI enables instant bank-to-bank fund transfers through mobile
devices and has substantially transformed the way individuals and businesses make
payments. This study examines the impact of UPI on the Indian banking sector using
secondary data collected from the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), NPCI and other published
sources. The study analyses the growth of UPI transaction volume and value and examines its
implications for banking operations, digital banking adoption, payment efficien cy, financial
inclusion and customer convenience. The study adopts a descriptive and analytical research
design. Trend analysis, percentage growth and compound annual growth rate are proposed for
analyzing the data. RBI data indicate that UPI accounted for  79.6% of retail-payment volume
in 2023 –24. The findings indicate that UPI has contributed significantly to the digital
transformation of Indian banking by increasing the speed, accessibility and convenience of
payment services. At the same time, increasin g digital transactions create challenges related
to cybersecurity, fraud prevention, technological dependency and customer awareness. The
study concludes that UPI has strengthened the digital banking ecosystem and is likely to
remain an important component of India's future payment infrastructure.
Keywords:
UPI, Digital Payments, Indian Banking Sector, NPCI, RBI, Financial Inclusion, Digital
Banking.
Introduction:
The Indian banking sector has undergone considerable transformation with the rapid
development of digital technologies. Traditional banking services have increasingly been
complemented by mobile banking, internet banking, digital wallets and instant payment
systems. Among these developments, the Unified Payments Interface (UPI) has emerged as a
major innovation in India's payment ecosystem. UPI was developed by the National
Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) to facilitate instant bank -to-bank payments through a
single digital platform. It enables users to transfer money using mobile applications w ithout
requiring traditional bank account details for every transaction.  The expansion of
smartphones, internet connectivity and digital banking has contributed to the rapid adoption
of UPI. The Reserve Bank of India has identified UPI as a major force beh ind the

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 173

transformation of India's retail -payment landscape. In 2023 –24, UPI accounted for 79.6% of
retail-payment volume and 25.3% of retail -payment value. The rapid expansion of UPI has
important implications for banks. It has changed customer expectation s, reduced dependence
on cash -based transactions, encouraged mobile banking and increased the importance of
digital infrastructure. Therefore, studying the impact of UPI on the Indian banking sector is
important from both academic and practical perspectives.
Statement of the Problem:
The Indian banking industry is moving rapidly towards digital banking. Customers
increasingly expect banking services to be instant, convenient and available 24 hours a day.
UPI has played an important role in this transformation. However, the rapid growth of digital
payments also creates challenges relating to cybersecurity, payment fraud, system reliability,
customer awareness and technological infrastructure. Therefore, this study attempts to
examine the growth of UPI and its impact on the Indian banking sector using secondary data.
Need for the Study:
The study is important because UPI has become a major component of India's payment
system; digital payments are changing traditional banking practices; UPI has increased the
speed and convenience of banking transactions; it has encouraged customers to adopt
mobile and digital banking; it supports wider access to formal financial services; the growth
of UPI creates opportunities for banks and fintech companies; and increasing digi tal
transactions create cybersecurity and fraud-related challenges.
Objectives of the Study:
 To examine the growth of UPI transactions in India.
  To analyze the growth in the volume and value of UPI transactions.
 To study the impact of UPI on the Indian banking sector.
 To examine the role of UPI in promoting digital banking
  To study the contribution of UPI towards financial inclusion.
  To identify the major challenges associated with UPI-based banking.
 To suggest measures for strengthening the UPI ecosystem.
Research Questions:
 How rapidly has UPI grown in India?
 How has UPI changed the Indian banking system?
 Has UPI strengthened digital banking adoption?
 What role does UPI play in financial inclusion?
 What are the major challenges associated with UPI?
Scope of the Study:
The study focuses on the impact of UPI on the Indian banking sector. It covers UPI
transaction volume, transaction value, participating banks, digital payment growth, financial
inclusion, digital banking, benefits and challenges. The study uses India-level secondary data.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 174

Research Methodology:
 Research Design:
The study adopts a descriptive and analytical research design.
 Nature of Data:
The study is based entirely on secondary data.
 Sources of Data:
Data are collected from the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), National Payments Corporation
of India (NPCI), RBI Annual Reports, RBI Payment System Indicators, RBI Bulletin,
Government of India reports, published research papers, books and journals.
 Period of Study:
The recommended period is 2018 –19 to 2025–26, subject to availability and consistency
of annual data.
 Statistical Tools:
Percentage analysis, trend analysis, Compound Annual Growth Rate (CAGR), mean,
standard deviation, correlation and regression analysis may be used where appropriate.
Growth of UPI in India:
UPI has experienced exceptionally rapid growth since its introduction. Increasing
smartphone usage, internet connectivity, mobile banking and the convenience of instant
account-to-account transfers have supported this growth.  According to RBI, UPI
processed more than 13 billion transactions in March 2024 alone. The RBI has also
observed a reduction in the average ticket size, indicating increasing use of UPI for
everyday retail payments rather than only high-value transfers.
Source: National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI), UPI Product Statistics.
UPI and Digital Banking:
UPI has changed the way customers interact with banks. Customers can initiate payments
directly through mobile applications, making banking services increasing ly mobile -oriented.
Major effects include 24×7 payment availability, faster fund transfers, reduced dependence on
cash, increased mobile banking usage, greater digital payment acceptance among merchants and
easier person -to-person payments. RBI's analysis shows that digital retail transactions have
become dominant in India's payment ecosystem. In 2023 –24, digital retail transactions
accounted for 90.9% of total retail-payment value.
UPI and Financial Inclusion:
Financial inclusion refers to ensuring access to useful and affordable financial services for
individuals and businesses. UPI can support financial inclusion by making digital payments
accessible through mobile phones and bank accounts. Its contribution includes easier access
to digital payment servic es, reduced transaction costs, increased formal financial activity,
greater merchant acceptance, convenient small -value transactions and support for rural and
semi-urban digital payments.  RBI has implemented programmes such as the Expanding and

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 175

Deepening of Digital Payments Ecosystem (EDDPE) to increase access to digital payment
methods across districts.
Impact of UPI on the Banking Sector:
 Digital Transformation:
UPI has accelerated the transformation from traditional banking to digital
banking.
 Customer Convenience:
Customers can transfer money instantly without visiting a bank branch.
 Reduced Cash Dependence:
The growth of digital payments can reduce dependence on physical cash for
everyday transactions.
 Increased Digital Transactions:
UPI has significantly expanded the volume of electronic retail payments.
 Financial Inclusion:
UPI provides a convenient digital payment mechanism for individuals with
bank accounts and mobile access.
 Innovation:
Banks have developed mobile applications, payment services and  digital
products around the UPI ecosystem.
 Merchant Adoption:
QR-code-based UPI payments have enabled even small merchants to accept
digital payments.
Challenges of UPI:
 Cybersecurity:
The rapid increase in digital payments creates a need for stronger cybersecurity.
 Digital Fraud:
Fraudulent links, impersonation, social engineering and unauthorized
transactions remain concerns.
 Digital Literacy:
Some users may not have sufficient knowledge to identify fraudulent payment
requests.
 Technology Dependence:
UPI transactions depend on mobile devices, internet connectivity, banking
infrastructure and payment-system availability.
 Privacy:
Increasing digital transactions raise questions regarding the protection and
responsible use of customer data.
Findings of the Study:
 UPI has recorded rapid growth in transaction volume and value.
 UPI has become the dominant retail-payment channel by transaction volume.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 176

 UPI has accelerated the digital transformation of Indian banking.
 Mobile-based banking has become increasingly important.
 UPI has increased customer convenience.
  QR-based payments have expanded digital-payment acceptance among merchants.
  UPI has supported the broader financial-inclusion objective.
 Banks have increasingly invested in digital payment infrastructure.
 Cybersecurity and digital fraud have become important concerns.
 Continued innovation, security improvements and customer awareness are necessary for
sustainable growth.
Suggestions:
 Banks should strengthen cybersecurity infrastructure.
 Customers should be educated about digital payment fraud.
 Banks should conduct regular digital financial-literacy programmes.
  Fraud monitoring systems should be continuously improved.
 Banks should improve grievance-redressal mechanisms.
 Digital-payment infrastructure should be strengthened in rural areas.
 Banks should improve transaction reliability and system availability.
 Customers should be encouraged to use safe digital payment practices.
 Regulatory institutions should continue monitoring emerging digital-payment risks.
 Greater awareness should be created regarding fraudulent UPI links, calls and payment
requests.
Conclusion:
UPI has fundamentally changed India's retail -payment ecosystem and has become an
important component of the country's digital banking transformation. Its rapid growth has
made instant bank -to-bank payments accessible, convenient and increasingly common for
individuals and businesses. The RBI's data demonstrate the scale of this transformation: UPI
represented 79.6% of retail-payment volume in 2023–24, while NPCI statistics show that the
system continues to expand rapidly. the impact of UPI on the banking se ctor is not limited to
payment transactions. It has encouraged banks to develop stronger digital platforms, changed
customer expectations and supported wider adoption of electronic banking. At the same time,
cybersecurity, fraud, digital literacy and syste m reliability remain important challenges.
Therefore, UPI can be considered a major driver of India's transition towards a digitally
enabled banking system. Continued investment in technology, security, financial literacy and
consumer protection will be essential to ensure sustainable growth of the UPI ecosystem.
Limitations of the Study:
 The study is based entirely on secondary data.
 The accuracy of the study depends on the reliability of published sources.
 The study does not directly measure individual customer perceptions.
 Changes in regulations and technology may influence future UPI trends.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 177

  UPI's impact cannot always be separated completely from the broader growth of digital
banking.
Scope for Future Research:
 Future researchers may examine UPI and financial inclusion in rural India
  UPI fraud and cybersecurity
  UPI adoption among senior citizens
 UPI and consumer spending behavior
 UPI and small-business growth;
 UPI adoption among college students; comparative performance of UPI and other digital -
payment systems; and UPI and profitability of commercial banks.
