A STUDY ON IMPACT OF BANKING SERVICES ON BANKING HABIT OF
PEOPLE
MS.T. Nadhiya
 Assistant professor of commerce
 Sriram College of Arts & Science, Perumalpattu, Tiruvallur-602024.
Abstract:
India has strong banking habits that are getting stronger day by day. The banking
habit of the people speaks about the country’s wealth. Nowadays the banks are encouraging
people to use financial servic es offered by it by providing various schemes. Hence the study
is made on the impact of banking services on banking habits of people. The study consists of
60 samples conducted among the population of North Chennai. The global economic
uncertainties and high inflation in India made many individuals prefer banking services.
Introduction:
 The financial system of a country shows the prestige of the country and its wealth.
After 1991, adoption of New Economic Policy there was a tremendous change in the
financial system in India. The financial system mainly includes financial Institution, financial
market, financial instrument and financial services. In this era the financial Institution and the
financial market play a vital role in people’s financial thoughts.  The financial Institutions are
divided into Banking and Non -Banking financial Institution. But people always afford
banking financial institutions based on safety and security factors. According to Section 5(b)
of the Banking Companies Act, 1949 banking i s defined as accepting for the purpose of
lending or investment of deposits of money received from the public, repayable on demand
and withdrawal by cheque, draft, order or otherwise  The Indian banking sector is broadly
classified into scheduled banks and  non-scheduled banks. All the banks included in the
Second Schedule to the Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934 are Scheduled Banks. These banks
comprise Scheduled Commercial Banks and Scheduled Co -operative Banks. Scheduled Co -
operative Banks consist of Schedu led State Co -operative Banks and Scheduled Urban
Cooperative Banks. Scheduled Commercial Banks in India are categorized into five different
groups according to their ownership and/or nature of operation:
 State Bank of India and its Associates (8)
 Nationalized Banks (19)
 Private Sector Banks (30)
 Foreign Banks (40)
 Regional Rural Banks. (196)
Banking services and banking habits:
 The study has made impact on banking services on banking habits of people. The
banks are providing various banking services to the people. In the past, people often visited
banks to get their services. Today people receive banking services at anytime and a nywhere
because of the technological advancement in banking services. The banks are competing in
providing services to the people to get large number of people as their customers.
Technological advancement has increased the services of the banks in new dim ensions like
ATM, internet banking, mobile banking, ECS Payment etc. The bank also reduced the risk of

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 179

carrying cash by offering credit cards and debit cards. Therefore, banking services have a
great impact on banking habits of the people.
 The banks act a s a financial intermediary among the people. The primary services of
banks are to accept deposits and to provide loans to public. Today it has tremendous growth
in providing services to the people. The service of the bank attracts people to open a bank
account in that bank. In earlier days, people stood by queue for all the banking related
services. But now people are using most of the banking services at their home through
internet and mobile banking. Human nature is to be safe and secure in all activities  they
involve. So, people prefer banking institutions.  With the advancement in technology, banks
are meeting the customers’ needs in best way compared to other financial institutions. The IT
revolution had a great impact on the Indian banking system. It ea sily connects bank and its
customers anywhere and at any time. With the use of information technology, the Indian
Banks can compete with the international banks in terms of customer services. The various
banking services are deposits, loan facility, taking  DD, fund transfer, ATM services, pension
payment, ECS payment, Safety Locker, Internet Banking, Mobile banking, mutual fund,
demat services, credit card services, foreign exchange services, investment services.
Insurance products etc. According to J Orhin Banking, the extent of willingness of the people
in given economy to make use of the facilities and sense of banking. Banking habit refers to
the practice of utilizing banking services effectively. In earlier days people utilized banking
services to increase their saving and then for availing loan services. But now banking habits
of people are increasing because of the various modern services provided by the banks. Thus,
the study is made on impact of banking services on banking habits of the people.
Objectives:
The main objective of the study is to know the impact of banking services on banking
habits of the people. As per this, the following specific objectives have been set for the study.
 To study the banking habit of people
 To identify the factors which increase the banking habits of the people.
 To identify the problem faced by the people in getting banking services.
Methodology:
The study was conducted among people residing in North Chennai. The data are
collected from primary and secondary sources. Sec ondary data is taken from published
books, project reports, periodicals, related websites etc. These sources are also used to frame
questionnaires required for collecting primary data. Primary data are collected through survey
method from a sample of 60 re spondents belonging to different occupational groups. This
study information was gathered about the demographic characteristics of the respondents,
their banking habits, factor motives for them to use the banking services, and problems faced
by them. The data are analyzed by using Percentage Analysis.
Review of the literature:
 Deepa Paul 2012-AStudy on the banking habit of people of Kerala - The objective
of the study was to identify the customers level of awareness in four major banking delivery
channels such as ATM, internet banking, mobile banking and credit card and their adoption.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 180

The study concludes that there is technology advancement and people have good awareness
in ATM and credit card but at the same time level of awareness is low in case of intern et and
mobile banking. ATM service was easily adopted by the people compared to other three
channels.
 Divya Joseph 2014 - A study on financial Inclusion and financial literacy - This
study measured the intensity of financial inclusion and financial awarenes s among the people
and stated that many policies and programmes are conducted by banks to overcome the
financial exclusion but still there is a lot to do. Many initiative and experimental services
should be taken to create more financial literacy among the  people, and it will eliminate
financial exclusion.
 R. Iswarya 2015 - A study on customer awareness on Modern Banking Services
in Theni (DT) - The study stated that the customer needs of various banking services are
increasing according to technology advancement, so the bank should introduce the innovative
services according to the customer needs and it should also create awareness about the usage
pattern of modern banking services.
 Divya Prabhu and Dr. G.S. Vijaya 2014 - A conceptual study on banking
technology and quality management in rural India - The study reveals that rural banking
has long way to go and the government should take necessary steps to improve technological
advancement in the rural banking services in order to achieve complete growth in the
country.
Gap Identified/Scope of the study:
 By reviewing the literature, it comes to know that the previous research was made on
banking habit of people and banking technology and  quality management in rural India and
made on customer awareness on modern banking services. Most of the researchers spoke
about the awareness and satisfaction of customers and need for improvement in rural area
banking.  Nowadays many factors like safety , security, easy access, financial protection etc.
have made people prefer financial services offered by bank. At the same time people are
facing problems in availing of banking services, it affects banking habits, i.e. utilization of
banking services in effective manner.  Thus, study is made on impact of banking
services on banking habit of people. It helps in knowing the banking habits of people. It
examines which factor motivates them to use financial services offered by the bank. The
study also helps to  identify the problems faced by people in availing banking services. The
target groups are people residing in North Chennai and it includes housewife’s, government
employees, non- government employees and people engaged in the business.

 Findings:
 Out of 60  respondents 58(97%) have bank accounts and only 3 respondents do not
have bank account.
 Most of the respondents use ATM services of banks (21 respondents). The
respondents have saving motive, so they visit banks to deposit their money.
 While visiting bank s respondents are getting much information from the banker and
they get more knowledge on financial services offered by banks through their friends,
relatives and online.
 The banking services satisfied the respondents and most of them reveal that they are
not facing problems in availing of banking services, but still certain percent of
respondents have problems. The safety, future needs etc. motivate them to use
financial services offered by banks.
Suggestions:
 The banks need to educate laymen more than the  upper-level respondent and
make them to use the modern banking services.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 184

 The government bank should update their technology equal to private sector
banks. The minimum balance maintenance should be as low as possible so that
low-income groups also open a bank account.
 The loan process to be quick and easy so that it will help people to avail more
loans from banks.
 There is no e - token system in semi urban and rural area banks. By providing e -
token system it will avoid queues and reduce time.
 The communication between banks and customers should be improved.
Conclusion:
 The Banking habits of the people are increasing because of technological
development. As said above, banking habits mean effective utilization of banking services.
Easy access services, mobile banking, ATM, internet banking improves the banking habits of
people. But still people are finding problems in availing of banking services. So, banks
should have direct communication with their customers by conducting programmes and
camps to find out about their problems and improve their services.  The modern services have
great impact on banking habit of the people. But it has not reached low -income group of
people. The people are not highly satisfied with the services offered by banks, so the bank
should create awareness among the people for the continuous usage of service. The factors
like financial protection, confidence in the ability of financial institution etc. motivate people
to use banking services.

ROLE OF AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
Ms. R. Sneka
 II M. Com (General)
 Valliammal College for Women, Chennai, India,
Ms. S. Kaleeswari
 Assistant Professor, Department of Commerce (General)
 Valliammal College for Women, Chennai, India, lankeshyogi@gmail.com
ABSTRACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is increasingly becoming an important technology in the financial
sector by improving the way financial information is analyzed and used for decision -making.
Financial analytics involves examining financial data to identify patterns, assess risks,
forecast future outcomes and support business decisions. The application of AI enabl es
organizations to process large volumes of financial data quickly and identify meaningful
insights that may be difficult to detect through traditional methods. AI -based financial
analytics can support areas such as financial forecasting, risk assessment, fraud identification,
investment analysis and performance evaluation. It can also help businesses improve the
accuracy and timeliness of financial decisions. However, the adoption of AI also creates
challenges related to data quality, privacy, security, t ransparency and the need for skilled
professionals. This paper examines the role of AI in financial analytics and highlights its
major applications, benefits and challenges in the modern business environment. The study is
based on secondary sources and dis cusses how AI can contribute to more efficient and data -
driven financial decision-making.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Financial Analytics, Financial Data, Risk Analysis,
Financial Decision-Making, Forecasting, Technology
INTRODUCTION
The financial s ector generates enormous quantities of structured and unstructured data
through banking transactions, investments, financial statements, customer activities, market
movements, digital payments and other financial operations. Traditionally, financial
institutions relied heavily on statistical techniques, historical data and human expertise to
analyze this information. The growth of Artificial Intelligence (AI), Machine Learning (ML)
and Big Data Analytics has changed the way financial information can be proc essed and
interpreted.
Artificial Intelligence refers to technologies that enable computer systems to perform tasks
that normally require human intelligence, such as learning, pattern recognition, prediction and
decision-making. Machine Learning, a major c omponent of AI, allows systems to identify
relationships within data and improve their performance through experience.  Financial
analytics involves collecting, processing and interpreting financial information to support
decisions. AI can strengthen this process by processing large datasets at high speed,
identifying complex patterns and generating predictions. Recent developments have increased
the importance of AI in financial markets. AI applications include improved efficiency,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 186

portfolio investment, return forecasting and risk analysis. Therefore, understanding the role of
AI in financial analytics is important for businesses, financial institutions, investors and
financial professionals.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
 To study the major applications of AI in financial analytics.
 To study the role of AI in financial forecasting.
 To examine the contribution of AI to financial risk management.
 To analyze the role of AI in fraud detection and prevention.
 To understand the benefits of AI-based financial analytics.
STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM
Financial institutions deal with increasingly large and complex volumes of financial data.
Traditional analytical methods may require significant time and human effort and may have
limitations when dealing with rapidly changing or highly complex datasets. At the same time,
financial decisions require accuracy, speed and effective risk management. Errors in
forecasting, fraud detection, credit assessment or investment analysis can result in significant
financial losses. AI provides o pportunities to improve financial analytics, but its
implementation also introduces challenges such as data quality problems, data privacy
concerns, cybersecurity risks, algorithmic bias, lack of transparency, model risk, shortage of
skilled professionals,  dependence on technology providers, and regulatory and compliance
requirements.
SCOPE OF THE STUDY
The study focuses on the application of AI in financial forecasting, risk management, fraud
detection, investment analysis, performance evaluation and financial decision-making.
LIMITATION OF THE STUDY
The study is based mainly on secondary information. AI technologies are developing rapidly,
so applications and risks may change over time. The effectiveness of AI can differ between
financial institutions and markets. The study does not conduct primary surveys among
financial professionals and does not evaluate a particular AI model using proprietary financial
datasets.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
Research Design: The study follows a descriptive and conceptual researc h design. It
examines the role, applications, benefits and challenges of Artificial Intelligence in financial
analytics.
Nature of the Study: The research is based primarily on secondary data.
Sources of Data: Information has been collected from research papers, academic publications,
reports of international financial institutions, OECD publications, IMF publications, Financial
Stability Board publications, official institutional reports, and relevant online sources and
literature.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 187

CONCEPT OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
Artificial Intelligence combines computational techniques that enable machines to perform
analytical and decision-support activities.
In financial analytics, AI can be used to analyze historical and real -time data and iden tify
relationships that may not be easily visible through conventional approaches.
Major technologies associated with AI -based financial analytics include Machine Learning,
Deep Learning, Natural Language Processing, Predictive Analytics and Generative AI.
AI and Financial Analytics: Financial Data → AI/ML Processing → Pattern Identification →
Prediction/Insight → Financial Decision.
APPLICATIONS OF AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
 Financial Forecasting: Financial forecasting involves estimating future financial
outcomes using historical and current information. AI can analyze historical sales,
revenue, expenses, cash flows, market conditions, customer behavior and economic
indicators. Machine-learning models can identify relationships in historical data and
generate forecasts.
 Benefits include faster forecasting, ability to analyse large datasets, identification of
complex relationships and continuous updating when new information be comes
available. Forecasts remain subject to uncertainty because unexpected economic and
market events can affect actual outcomes.
 AI in Financial Risk Management: Risk management is one of the most important
areas of financial analytics. Financial institu tions face credit risk, market risk,
liquidity risk, operational risk, cybersecurity risk and fraud risk. AI can analyse
historical and real-time information to identify potential risk indicators.
 AI in Fraud Detection: Financial fraud is a major concern f or banks, payment
companies, insurance companies and other financial institutions. AI -based systems
can complement rule -based approaches by identifying unusual patterns in large
volumes of transactions. AI can support real -time monitoring and reduce manual
monitoring.
 AI in Investment Analysis: Investment analysis requires consideration of market
prices, company financial statements, economic indicators, industry developments,
news, historical performance and investor behaviour. AI can process these differe nt
types of information and assist investment professionals in identifying patterns and
generating analytical insights.
 AI in Performance Evaluation: Financial performance evaluation involves analysing
whether an organization is achieving its financial obj ectives. Common measures
include revenue growth, profitability, return on investment, cash flow, cost efficiency,
asset utilisation, liquidity and financial ratios. AI can help analyse these indicators
across different periods and identify trends.
 AI in Cr edit Analysis: Credit analysis evaluates the ability of an individual or
organization to repay borrowed funds. AI can assist in analyzing income, repayment

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 188

history, transaction behavior, existing liabilities and cash -flow patterns. Models
should be careful ly monitored because biased or poor -quality data can contribute to
inappropriate outcomes.
 AI in Cash -Flow Management: AI can analyze historical cash inflows and outflows
and assist in predicting future cash requirements. Businesses can use AI -supported
analytics to identify expected cash shortages, payment patterns, collection trends,
excess cash and working-capital requirements.
BENEFITS OF AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
 Faster Data Processing: AI can process large quantities of information much faster
than manual analysis.
 Improved Analytical Capability: AI can identify complex patterns and relationships
within datasets.
 Better Forecasting Support: AI can support forecasting by analysing historical and
current information.
 Fraud Detection: AI can identify unus ual transaction patterns and support fraud
monitoring.
 Improved Risk Analysis: AI can provide additional analytical information for risk -
management decisions.
 Operational Efficiency: Automation can reduce repetitive analytical tasks and allow
employees to focus on higher-value activities.
 Real-Time Analysis: AI systems can support analysis of continuously generated
financial data.
 Data-Driven Decision -Making: AI can provide decision -makers with analytical
insights that complement human judgement.
CHALLENGES OF AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
 Data Quality: AI models depend heavily on the quality of their data input. Incomplete,
inaccurate or inconsistent data can produce unreliable results.
 Data Privacy: Financial information is highly sensitive. AI systems may pro cess bank
transactions, customer information, financial records, personal information and
investment information. Improper handling can create privacy risks.
 Cybersecurity: AI can support cybersecurity but can also introduce new risks.
Organizations requir e strong cybersecurity controls and incident -response
mechanisms.
 Lack of Explainability: Some advanced AI models can be difficult to interpret.
Financial institutions need to understand why a model produces a particular output,
especially for high-impact decisions.
 Algorithmic Bias: If training data contain historical biases, AI systems may reproduce
or amplify them. Models should be regularly tested for fairness and inappropriate
outcomes.
 Lack of Skilled Professionals: Successful implementation requires professionals who
understand both finance and technology.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 189

 Regulatory Challenges: AI technology is developing rapidly, creating challenges for
regulators and financial institutions.
AI AND FINANCIAL DECISION-MAKING
AI can transform financial decision -making from a largely historical and manually intensive
process into a more data-driven and technology-supported process.
Traditional approach: Data Collection → Manual Analysis → Human Interpretation →
Decision.
AI-supported approach: Data Collection → Automate d Processing → AI Analysis →
Prediction/Insight → Human Review → Decision.
The important point is that AI should generally be considered a decision -support technology
rather than automatically replacing human responsibility. Human professionals provide
context, professional judgement, ethical consideration, regulatory interpretation, validation of
unusual results and accountability.
ROLE OF HUMAN EXPERTISE
Despite the growth of AI, human expertise remains important in financial analytics. AI may
process data quickly, but financial decisions can involve factors that are difficult to capture
completely through historical datasets.
A balanced approach can therefore be represented as: AI Capabilities + Human Expertise +
Governance = Responsible Financial Analytics.
Human professionals should monitor AI outputs and investigate unexpected or high -impact
results.
RESPONSIBLE AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
Responsible AI refers to the development and use of AI systems in ways that consider
accuracy, transparency, accountability, privacy, security and fairness.
Financial institutions should establish appropriate governance frameworks. Important
principles include data governance, model validation, human oversight, transparency, privacy
protection, cybersecurity, regular monitoring, regulatory compliance and accountability.
FUTURE SCOPE OF AI IN FINANCIAL ANALYTICS
 Real-Time Financial Analytics: Organizations may increasingly use AI to analyse
financial information in real time.
 Generative AI: Generative AI may assist financi al professionals with financial -
document summarization, research assistance, report preparation, data interpretation
and information retrieval. Outputs require verification.
 Advanced Fraud Detection: AI systems may become increasingly capable of
identifying complex transaction patterns.
 Automated Risk Monitoring: AI can support continuous monitoring of financial and
operational risks.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 190

 AI-Based Financial Planning: AI may increasingly assist organizations in forecasting,
budgeting and scenario analysis.
 RegTech and SupTech: AI can support regulatory compliance and supervisory
analytics.
 Greater Integration with Cloud and Big Data: AI -based financial analytics may
increasingly operate alongside cloud computing, big -data platforms and digital
financial services.
FINDINGS OF THE STUDY
1. The study finds that Artificial Intelligence has become an increasingly
important technology in financial analytics.
2. The study finds that AI can process large volumes of financial information
efficiently.
3. The study finds that AI can support financial forecasting and predictive
analysis.
4. The study finds that AI can assist financial institutions in identifying potential
risks.
5. The study finds that AI can support fraud detection through pattern
recognition.
6. The study finds that AI can assist investment and portfolio analysis.
7. The study finds that AI can improve the speed of financial information
processing.
8. The study finds that AI can support data-driven financial decision-making.
9. The study finds that data quality is an important factor a ffecting AI
performance.
10. The study finds that privacy and cybersecurity are major concerns in AI -based
financial analytics.
11. The study finds that explainability and model validation remain important
challenges.
12. The study finds that financial institutions re quire skilled professionals to
implement AI effectively.
13. The study finds that AI adoption requires appropriate governance and
regulatory oversight.
14. The study finds that human judgement remains important, particularly for
high-impact financial decisions.
15. The study finds that responsible AI practices are necessary to obtain the
benefits of AI while managing associated risks.
CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence is transforming financial analytics by improving the speed and scale at
which financial information c an be processed and analysed. Its applications extend across
financial forecasting, risk management, fraud detection, investment analysis, credit analysis,
cash-flow management and performance evaluation. The study shows that AI can provide

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 191

important benefits, particularly in processing large datasets, identifying patterns, supporting
predictions and improving the timeliness of financial analysis. These capabilities can
contribute to more data -driven financial decision -making. At the same time, AI adoption
creates significant challenges. Data quality, privacy, cybersecurity, algorithmic bias,
explainability, model risk, regulatory requirements and shortage of skilled professionals must
be carefully addressed. Broader concerns involving financial stability an d dependence on
common technology providers also require attention.  Therefore, the effective use of AI in
financial analytics requires a balance between technological innovation and responsible
governance. AI can provide powerful analytical support, but it s value ultimately depends on
the quality of data, reliability of models, appropriate human oversight and effective risk -
management practices.  The future of financial analytics is likely to involve greater
collaboration between Artificial Intelligence and human financial expertise. Financial
institutions that develop appropriate technological capabilities while maintaining strong
governance and professional oversight can use AI to support more efficient, timely and data -
driven financial decision-making.

E-COMMERCE AND DIGITAL MARKETING: AI TOOLS IN SOCIAL MEDIA
MARKETING
Mrs V. PRIYA
.Assistant Professor, PG Department of Commerce
 Sriram College of Arts and Science, Perumalpattu, Thiruvallur – 602024
ABSTRACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has become an important technology in the transformation of
digital marketing and e -commerce. The rapid growth of social media, mobile commerce and
online shopping has created new opportunities for businesses to understand customers and
communicate with them effectively. AI technologies such as machine learning,  natural
language processing, generative AI, chatbots, recommendation systems, predictive analytics
and automated advertising are increasingly used in marketing.  This paper examines the role
of AI tools in social media marketing and e -commerce through a co nceptual and review -
based approach. It discusses the applications of AI in personalization, content creation,
customer engagement, social-media analytics, targeted advertising, product recommendations
and marketing automation. The paper also examines the b enefits, challenges and ethical
issues associated with AI -based marketing, including data privacy, algorithmic bias,
transparency, consumer trust and copyright. Recent developments such as generative AI,
conversational commerce, AI -powered product discover y and social commerce are also
discussed. The paper concludes that AI can improve digital marketing efficiency and
customer experience when combined with responsible data practices, transparency and
human supervision.
KEYWORDS: Artificial Intelligence, Dig ital Marketing, Social Media Marketing, E -
Commerce, Generative AI, Machine Learning, Chatbots, Personalization, Social Commerce.
INTRODUCTION
Digital marketing refers to the use of digital technologies and online communication channels
to promote products,  services and brands. Websites, search engines, social media, mobile
applications, email and e -commerce platforms have become important components of
modern marketing. The widespread use of smartphones, internet connectivity and social
media has significan tly changed the relationship between businesses and consumers.
Artificial Intelligence has introduced a new stage in digital marketing by enabling
organizations to process large amounts of data, identify patterns, predict consumer behaviour
and automate marketing activities. AI has increasingly become part of customer relationship
management, advertising, product recommendation, content creation and customer service.
Social media is particularly important because consumers continuously generate information
through comments, reviews, likes, searches, posts and other interactions. AI can process this
information and provide insights into consumer preferences and behaviour. Recent research
has identified AI and generative AI in social media as an expanding area  of research
involving consumer behaviour, marketing opportunities and risks.The development of

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 193

generative AI has further expanded the role of AI in marketing. Generative AI can assist in
creating written content, images, advertisements and campaign ideas.  AI chatbots can
communicate with customers and provide product information, while recommendation
systems can suggest products based on customer activity.  E-commerce has therefore moved
towards an increasingly intelligent and personalized environment. Howe ver, the use of
customer data and automated decision -making also creates concerns relating to privacy,
fairness, transparency and consumer trust.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
The objectives of the study are:
1. To examine the role of Artificial Intelligence in digital marketing and e-commerce.
2. To study the major AI tools used in social media marketing.
3. To understand the role of AI in customer personalization and engagement.
4. To examine the application of generative AI in digital marketing.
RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
The present study follows a conceptual and review -based approach . It does not involve
primary data collection, questionnaires, interviews or statistical analysis. The paper is based
on relevant academic literature and recent developments relating to Artifi cial Intelligence,
digital marketing, social media marketing and e -commerce. Recent research and scholarly
studies have been considered to understand developments in AI personalization, generative
AI, customer engagement, social commerce, AI chatbots, cons umer behaviour, privacy and
ethical issues.
LITERATURE REVIEW
Grewal et al. (2025) examined the future role of generative AI in marketing and discussed its
potential to influence customer communication, content creation, product development and
sales activities.
Madanchian (2024) examined the impact of AI marketing on e -commerce sales and
highlighted applications including chatbots, personalization and predictive analytics. The
study also identified privacy and bias as important concerns.
Behera and Rana (2024) examined AI-enabled e-marketing from an e-retailer perspective and
discussed the role of AI in customer engagement, customer analysis and personalized
marketing communication.
Gastmann and Bastos (2025) studied generative AI in social -media marketing and
highlighted its potential to improve productivity in social -media marketing strategy and
content-related activities.
Borimnejad and Borimnejad (2026) reviewed recent research on AI applications in marketing
and identified important themes such as AI pe rsonalization, customer engagement, predictive
analytics, generative AI, ethics and privacy.

CONCEPT OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN DIGITAL MARKETING
Artificial Intelligence in digital marketing refers to the use of intelligent computational
systems to anal yze information, identify patterns, predict consumer behaviour, automate
marketing activities and support marketing decisions.
Major technologies used in AI marketing include:
 Machine Learning
 Natural Language Processing
 Deep Learning
 Generative AI
AI TOOLS IN SOCIAL MEDIA MARKETING
AI-Based Content Creation
Generative AI can assist marketers in creating social -media captions, advertisements, product
descriptions, images and video concepts. It can reduce the time required for repetitive content
creation. Ho wever, human review is necessary to identify inaccurate, inappropriate or
misleading content.
Personalized Marketing
AI can analyze customer interests and online behaviour to provide personalized content,
advertisements and product recommendations. Persona lization can help businesses
communicate with customers more effectively.
Sentiment Analysis
Natural Language Processing can analyze customer comments, reviews and social -media
posts to identify positive, negative or neutral opinions. This can help busines ses understand
customer reactions to products and campaigns.
AI Chatbots
AI chatbots can communicate with customers through websites and social -media platforms.
They can answer frequently asked questions, provide product information and support
customers during the purchase process.
Targeted Advertising
AI can analyze customer information and behaviour to identify relevant audience groups for
advertising campaigns. This supports targeted marketing and campaign optimization.
Recommendation Systems
AI recommendation systems suggest products or content based on customer preferences and
previous activities. They are widely relevant to e -commerce platforms with large product
catalogues.

Social-Media Trend Analysis
AI can identify emerging topics, keywords and consumer interests from social -media data.
Businesses can use these insights to develop relevant marketing campaigns.
ROLE OF AI IN E-COMMERCE
AI has become increasingly important throughout the e-commerce customer journey.
Product Discovery
AI-based search and recommendation systems can help customers identify suitable products
from large online catalogues.
Personalized Shopping
AI can provide personalized recommendations and communication based on customer
interests and purchasing behaviour.
Customer Service
AI chatbots can provide customer assistance, answer routine questions and offer product
information throughout the day.
Demand Prediction
Predictive analytics can assist businesses in understanding demand patterns and making
decisions relating to marketing and inventory.
Customer Retention
AI can identify customer behaviour patterns and support personalized communication
designed to maintain customer engagement.
BENEFITS OF AI IN DIGITAL MARKETING AND E-COMMERCE
The major benefits of AI-based digital marketing include:
1. Improved personalization – AI supports individualized marketing communication.
2. Faster content creation – Generative AI can assist in producing marketing content.
3. Better customer engagement  – Chatbots and personalized communication sup port
continuous interaction.
4. Data-based decision making – AI can identify patterns from large datasets.
5. Marketing automation – Repetitive activities can be automated.
6. Improved customer understanding  – AI can analyze customer preferences and
behaviour.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in

CHALLENGES AND ETHICAL ISSUES
Although AI offers many advantages, its use in marketing creates several challenges.
Data Privacy
AI marketing frequently depends on customer information. Businesses must ensure that
customer data is collected and used responsibly.
Algorithmic Bias
AI systems may produce biased outcomes when the data used by the system contains existing
biases. Regular monitoring is therefore important.
Lack of Transparency
Customers may not always understand why a particular recommendation or advertisement is
shown to them.
Consumer Trust
Excessive automation or inappropriate use of personal information may reduce consumer
confidence.
Copyright
AI-generated content raises questions concerning originality, ownership and the use of
existing materials.
Cybersecurity
Organizations using large amounts of customer information must protect data against
unauthorized access and cyber threats.
EMERGING TRENDS IN AI AND DIGITAL MARKETING
Generative AI Marketing
Generative AI is becoming an important market ing technology for creating text, images,
advertising content and campaign ideas.
Conversational Commerce
AI chatbots and conversational systems are increasingly being integrated into online
shopping and customer service.
AI-Powered Product Discovery
Large language models are creating new ways for consumers to discover products through
AI-generated responses and recommendations.
AI and Social Commerce
AI is strengthening the connection between social -media interaction, product discovery and
online transactions.
FINDINGS OF THE CONCEPTUAL REVIEW
The review highlights the following key findings:

5. AI reduces repetitive marketing tasks and improves productivity.
6. Privacy, data security, bias and transparency remain important concerns.

9. Future research increasingly focuses on AI, consumer behaviour and social media.
CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence is transforming digital marketing and e -commerce through
personalization, content creation, customer service, predictive analytics, recommendation
systems and generative AI. Social media provides valuable customer data t hat helps
businesses understand consumer behaviour and develop targeted marketing strategies.  At the
same time, AI -based marketing must address issues such as privacy, transparency, fairness,
cybersecurity, copyright and consumer trust. The future of digit al marketing will increasingly
depend on human -AI collaboration, where AI supports efficiency and innovation while
human judgment ensures responsible and customer -oriented decision -making. Thus, AI is
becoming an important part of the evolving digital commerce ecosystem.

Artificial Intelligence in Branding: Reshaping Consumer Engagement and Brand
Equity in the Indian Market
Mathimalar. S
 III BBA, Department of Business Administration
 Sriram College of Arts and science
Abstract
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has emerged as a transformative force in the field of branding,
altering the manner in which organizations conceive, communicate, and manage brand
identity. This paper examines the role of AI -enabled technologies —including mach ine
learning, natural language processing, predictive analytics, and generative AI —in shaping
brand strategy, consumer engagement, and brand equity, with particular emphasis on the
Indian market. Drawing upon a review of existing academic literature and il lustrative case
evidence from Indian brands across e -commerce, banking, and food -technology sectors, the
study identifies key application areas such as hyper -personalization, conversational
commerce, sentiment analytics, and AI -assisted creative design. Th e paper further discusses
the challenges of data privacy, algorithmic bias, and the erosion of human creativity that
accompany AI adoption in branding. The study concludes that while AI substantially
enhances the efficiency and precision of branding effort s, sustainable brand equity in the
Indian context continues to depend on the judicious integration of AI capabilities with
human-centered storytelling and cultural sensitivity.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Branding, Brand Equity, Consumer Engagement,
Personalization, Indian Market, Digital Marketing
I. Introduction
Branding has traditionally been regarded as a human -driven exercise rooted in storytelling,
symbolism, and emotional connection between an organization and its consumers. The rapid
diffusion of Artificial Intelligence across marketing functions, however, has begun to
redefine this paradigm. AI -powered systems now assist brand managers in analyzing vast
volumes of consumer data, predicting purchase behavior, personalizing communication at
scale, and even generating creative content such as taglines, visuals, and advertisements. In
the Indian context, the confluence of a large digitally connected population, a rapidly
expanding e-commerce ecosystem, and increasing investment in AI infrastructur e has made
the country a fertile ground for AI-led branding innovation.
Global bibliometric evidence confirms that India is among the leading contributors to
research on AI and branding, alongside the United States and the United Kingdom, reflecting
the growing academic and industry interest in this intersection within the country.  Branding
in the pre -AI era relied heavily on market research surveys, focus groups, and creative
intuition to shape positioning and messaging. These methods, though valuable, wer e often
slow, resource-intensive, and limited in their ability to capture real -time shifts in consumer
sentiment. AI-driven analytics compress this cycle considerably, allowing brand managers to

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 199

observe, interpret, and respond to consumer behavior almost i nstantaneously. This shift from
episodic to continuous brand management represents one of the most significant structural
changes introduced by AI into the discipline of branding.
Against this backdrop, this paper seeks to present a structured understandin g of how AI
technologies are being applied within the branding function, the extent to which Indian
brands have adopted such technologies, and the strategic and ethical considerations that
accompany this shift. The paper is organized as follows: Section II  reviews relevant
literature; Section III states the objectives of the study; Section IV outlines the research
methodology; Section V discusses key AI applications in branding; Section VI presents
illustrative Indian case evidence; Section VII discusses ch allenges; and Section VIII
concludes the paper with directions for future research.
II. Literature Review
The relationship between AI and branding has attracted growing scholarly attention over the
past decade. Vishwakarma synthesized findings from a large body of peer-reviewed literature
on AI -mediated brand communication, employing bibliometric mapping and the Theory -
Context-Characteristics-Methodology (TCCM) framework, and reported that AI -enabled
touchpoints are increasingly central to how brands intera ct with consumers across the
customer journey.
In the Indian context, Malhan, Mewafarosh and Agnihotri examined how the perceived
usefulness and perceived ease -of-use of AI constructs influence consumer satisfaction, trust,
loyalty, and repurchase intention, using the case of sports footwear brands, and found that AI-
driven usefulness significantly strengthens consumer trust and loyalty toward a brand.
Similarly, Thakur and Kushwaha undertook a science -mapping and bibliometric clustering
exercise on AI in m arketing, highlighting how Indian FMCG companies are adapting AI
within a Marketing 4.0 framework to enhance dynamic capabilities and customer
responsiveness.
Kapoor and Kapoor studied the transition of Indian hospitality businesses from traditional to
digital marketing, observing that AI and e -marketing tools have reshaped guest engagement
and brand visibility in the Indian hotel industry. Khandelwal, Malhotra, Sharma and Sarin
conducted a bibliometric analysis of AI in digital marketing and noted that alg orithmic
branding through platform assemblages represents an emerging research direction with strong
implications for brand management practice.
Earlier consumer -behavior research by Sarkar, Sarkar and Phau explored brand devotion
among young consumers in the emerging Indian market, providing a behavioral baseline
against which the influence of AI -personalized engagement can be assessed. At a global
level, a bibliometric review using PRISMA guidelines covering more than five decades of
literature confirmed that India, alongside the United States and United Kingdom, is among
the leading countries contributing to research on the impact of AI on branding, underscoring
the relevance of India-focused inquiry such as the present study.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 200

Collectively, this literature suggests three broad themes: (a) AI as an enabler of personalized
and trust-enhancing brand communication, (b) AI as a strategic capability influencing brand -
related decision -making, and (c) the need for context -specific, India -centric research given
the country's distinct consumer diversity and digital adoption patterns.  A related stream of
scholarship situates AI within the broader consumer -experience literature. Puntoni, Reczek,
Giesler and Botti proposed an experiential framework for understanding how  consumers
perceive and respond to AI across data -capture, classification, delegation, and social
interaction roles, a framework that has since informed subsequent branding research on AI -
mediated touchpoints. Haenlein and Kaplan traced the historical evol ution of AI and
cautioned that managerial adoption often outpaces conceptual understanding, a gap that is
particularly salient in emerging markets such as India where AI capability is being adopted
rapidly across sectors. Kozinets further introduced the no tion of 'algorithmic branding
through platform assemblages', arguing that brand meaning is increasingly co -produced by
algorithms, platforms, and consumers acting together rather than by the firm in isolation —an
insight with direct relevance to Indian digital-first brands operating on e-commerce and social
media platforms.
III. Objectives of the Study
● To examine the key areas of AI application within contemporary branding practice.
● To analyze how Indian brands are leveraging AI technologies to enhance consu mer
engagement and brand equity.
● To identify the challenges and ethical concerns associated with the adoption of AI in
branding.
● To suggest directions for future research and practice at the intersection of AI and
branding in India.
IV. Research Methodology
This study adopts a descriptive and exploratory research design based on secondary data.
Relevant academic literature was drawn from peer -reviewed journals, bibliometric studies,
and industry reports pertaining to AI and branding, with particul ar attention to research
situated in the Indian context. In addition, publicly available secondary information on the
AI-related branding initiatives of select Indian companies was reviewed to develop
illustrative case evidence. The study does not employ p rimary data collection and is intended
to provide a conceptual synthesis rather than a statistically generalizable analysis; this
constitutes an acknowledged limitation of the paper.
V. AI Applications in Branding
A. Hyper-Personalization
Machine learning algorithms enable brands to analyze browsing history, purchase patterns,
and demographic data to deliver individualized product recommendations, offers, and
content, thereby strengthening the perceived relevance of the brand to each consumer.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 201

B. Conversational AI and Chatbots
Natural language processing has enabled brands to deploy chatbots and virtual assistants that
maintain consistent brand voice while handling customer queries at scale, contributing to
both service efficiency and brand personality reinforcement.
C. Predictive Analytics for Consumer Insight
Predictive models allow brand managers to forecast demand, anticipate churn, and identify
emerging consumer segments, enabling proactive rather than reactive brand positioning
strategies.
D. AI-Assisted Creative Content Generation
Generative AI tools are increasingly used to draft advertising copy, generate visual concepts,
and produce localized content variants for diverse linguistic and cultural markets, a capability
of particular relevance in India's multilingual consumer landscape.
E. Sentiment and Social Listening Analytics
AI-based sentiment analysis of social media conversations enables brands to monitor
reputation in real time and respond swiftly to shifts in consumer perception, suppor ting more
agile brand reputation management.
F. AI in Visual Identity and Design
Generative design tools now assist brand designers in rapidly prototyping logos, packaging
concepts, and colour palettes, and in testing multiple visual identity variants agai nst predicted
consumer response before finalization, thereby shortening design cycles while preserving
room for human creative judgement.
G. Voice Search and Conversational Discovery
As voice -assistant usage grows in India, brands are optimizing their digi tal presence for
conversational and voice -based search queries, which requires rethinking brand naming,
keyword strategy, and content structure to remain discoverable in an AI -mediated search
environment.

VI. Illustrative Evidence from Indian Brands
Several Indian brands illustrate the practical application of AI in branding. E -commerce
platforms such as Myntra and Nykaa employ AI -driven recommendation engines and visual
search to personalize the shopping experience and reinforce brand stickiness. In the banking
sector, HDFC Bank's virtual assistant 'EVA' handles a large volume of customer queries
while maintaining a consistent conversational brand tone, illustrating how conversatio nal AI
can be woven into a legacy financial brand's personality. Food -technology platforms such as
Zomato and Swiggy use AI for personalized recommendations, dynamic offer targeting, and
demand prediction, all of which shape consumer perceptions of brand responsiveness.
In the fashion and lifestyle segment, Tata CLiQ and Titan have experimented with AI -based
styling recommendations and virtual try -on features to bridge the gap between online and in -
store brand experience. Telecom and technology brands such as Jio have used AI -driven
customer analytics to personalize service bundles and retention offers, reinforcing brand
loyalty in a highly competitive market. Meanwhile, several Indian FMCG and D2C (direct -
to-consumer) brands have begun to use generative AI tools for regional-language advertising
copy, enabling more culturally resonant brand communication across India's linguistically
diverse consumer base. These examples, while illustrative rather than exhaustive, indicate
that AI adoption in Indian branding  spans multiple sectors and touchpoints, reflecting a
broader industry trend consistent with the patterns identified in the literature reviewed in
Section II.
VII. Challenges and Ethical Considerations
● Data privacy and consent: Extensive use of consumer da ta for personalization raises
concerns regarding informed consent and compliance with data protection regulations.
● Algorithmic bias: AI systems trained on non -representative data may generate biased
recommendations or messaging, potentially alienating certain consumer segments.
● Erosion of creative authenticity: Overreliance on AI -generated content may dilute the
distinctiveness and emotional authenticity traditionally associated with strong brands.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 203

● Consumer trust and transparency: Undisclosed use of AI in c onsumer interactions, such
as chatbots impersonating human agents, can undermine brand trust if not managed
transparently.
● Skill and infrastructure gaps: Smaller Indian enterprises may face resource constraints in
adopting sophisticated AI branding tools compared to large, well-funded organizations.
● Homogenization of brand voice: When multiple brands rely on similar generative AI
models and training data, there is a risk that distinct brand voices converge toward a
generic tone, weakening differentiation.
● Regulatory uncertainty: Evolving data protection frameworks in India, including
obligations under the Digital Personal Data Protection Act, require brands to continuously
adapt their AI-driven personalization practices to remain compliant.
Taken together, these challenges suggest that successful AI -enabled branding is not merely a
matter of technological adoption but requires parallel investment in governance, talent, and
ethical oversight.
VIII. Conclusion and Future Scope
This paper has examined the evolving role of Artificial Intelligence in branding, with specific
attention to the Indian market context. The review of literature and illustrative industry
evidence indicates that AI is reshaping branding practice through personalization,
conversational engagement, predictive insight, and creative content generation. At the same
time, the study underscores those challenges relating to data privacy, algorithmic bias, and
creative authenticity that must be addressed for AI -enabled branding to generate sustainab le
brand equity. Future research may adopt primary, survey -based, or experimental designs to
empirically test the influence of specific AI applications on brand trust and loyalty among
Indian consumers and may further examine sector -specific and regional v ariations in AI -
driven branding adoption across India.

ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN HUMAN RESOURCE MANAGEMENT:
TRANSFORMING RECRUITMENT, ENGAGEMENT AND RETENTION
STRATEGIES
Hariharasudhan
 III BBA, Sriram College of Arts and Science
ABSTRACT
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is rapidly redefining the way organizations manage their most
valuable resource — people. Human Resource Management (HRM), traditionally regarded as
a people-centric and administratively heavy function, is undergoing a paradigm shift with the
infusion of AI-enabled tools across recruitment, onboarding, training, performance appraisal,
employee engagement and attrition management. This paper examines the extent and nature
of AI adoption in HRM practices, with specific reference to the Indian corporate and IT
sector, which has emerged as one of the fastest adopters of intelligent HR technologies. Using
a descriptive research design based on secondary data drawn from peer -reviewed journals,
industry reports and case studies of Indian organizations, the paper identifies key application
areas of AI in HRM, evaluates the benefits reported by adopting firms, and discusses the
ethical, legal and human -centric challenges that accompany automation of people -decisions.
The study finds that while AI significantly improves efficiency, objectivity and speed of HR
operations, its success depends on responsible governa nce, algorithmic transparency and the
retention of human judgment in sensitive decision areas. The paper concludes with a
conceptual framework and recommendations for Indian organizations seeking to adopt AI in
HRM in a balanced and employee-centric manner.
Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Human Resource Management, Recruitment, Employee
Engagement, Attrition Prediction, Indian IT Sector
1. INTRODUCTION
Human Resource Management has historically relied on manual, judgment -based processes
for hiring, evalu ating and retaining employees. The last decade, however, has witnessed a
sharp rise in the deployment of Artificial Intelligence across almost every HR sub -function.
Chatbots screen candidates, machine learning models predict attrition, natural language
processing tools analyze employee sentiment, and predictive analytics guide workforce
planning. In India, this shift has been particularly visible in the IT, ITeS, banking and e -
commerce sectors, where the scale of hiring and the availability of digital infr astructure have
accelerated AI adoption. Organizations such as Infosys, TCS, Wipro and several new -age
start-ups have integrated AI -based applicant tracking systems, virtual interview bots and
learning-recommendation engines into their HR ecosystems.  This transformation raises
important questions for both academicians and practitioners. Does AI genuinely improve the
quality of HR decision -making, or does it merely automate existing biases at a faster pace?
What is the appropriate balance between algorithmic  efficiency and human empathy in
people-management? This paper attempts to address these questions by reviewing the current
state of AI adoption in HRM, with a focus on the Indian context.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 206

2. REVIEW OF LITERATURE
A growing body of Indian and international literature has examined the intersection of AI and
HRM. Hiran, Kothari and Singh (2023) studied private organizations in India and found a
statistically significant impact of AI -based HR practices on organizational performance,
particularly in reducing tim e-to-hire and improving decision accuracy. Similarly, Bhardwaj,
Singh and Kumar (2020), in their empirical study presented at the International Conference
on Computation, Automation and Knowledge Management, reported that AI adoption
positively influenced core HR functions including recruitment and training need
identification.
Pandey, Chitranshi, Nagendra and Lawande (2020) examined HR practices in a large Indian
institutional setting and recommended structured implementation of AI to modernize HRM
processes. Naik (2024) observed that AI technologies in Indian IT companies have improved
workplace efficiency by automating data collection and analysis, thereby reducing the
administrative burden on HR teams. Dutta, Mishra and Tyagi (2023) demonstrated that AI -
enabled chatbots enhance employee voice and engagement through continuous, low -friction
feedback channels. Shukla and Singh (2025), in a bibliometric analysis published in
Prabandhan: Indian Journal of Management, noted a sharp rise in global research outp ut on
AI in HRM after 2018, though they identified limited cross -country collaboration among
researchers, indicating scope for more India-specific empirical work.
Vinitha (2024) focused specifically on recruitment and performance management, concluding
that AI enables more equitable and data -driven hiring decisions. Agarwal, in a study on AI
adoption antecedents, highlighted that organizational readiness and employee trust are critical
determinants of successful AI -HRM integration. Collectively, this litera ture suggests that
while the operational benefits of AI in HRM are well documented, Indian research
increasingly calls for closer attention to ethical safeguards, algorithmic bias and the human
dimension of HR technology adoption.
Globally, Gong, Fan and B artram (2024) note that the integration of AI into HRM is best
understood not as a single technology adoption but as an ongoing organizational
transformation requiring changes in skills, processes and governance structures. Pan and
Froese (2023) similarly argue that interdisciplinary perspectives — drawing on computer
science, organizational behavior and ethics — are necessary to fully understand the
implications of AI for the employment relationship. Within the Indian context specifically,
researchers have  repeatedly flagged the need for context -sensitive frameworks, since HR
practices, labor regulations and workforce expectations in India differ meaningfully from
those in Western economies where much of the foundational AI -HRM literature originates.
This g ap provides the motivation for the present paper's focus on Indian organizational
experience.
3. OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
● To identify the key functional areas of HRM where Artificial Intelligence is being applied
by Indian organizations.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 207

● To examine the benefits and outcomes associated with AI adoption in recruitment,
training, performance management and employee engagement.
● To analyze the ethical, legal and human -centric challenges arising from AI -driven HR
decision-making.
● To propose a conceptual framework for responsible and balanced adoption of AI in HRM.
4. RESEARCH METHODOLOGY
This paper adopts a descriptive, exploratory research design based entirely on secondary data.
Data has been compiled from peer -reviewed journal articles, conference proceeding s,
industry white papers and published case studies relating to AI adoption in HRM, with
particular emphasis on studies conducted in the Indian corporate and IT sector. The literature
was analyzed thematically to identify recurring application areas, benef its and challenges,
which form the basis of the discussion and conceptual framework presented in this paper.
Given its conceptual and review -based nature, the study does not involve primary data
collection or statistical hypothesis testing.
5. APPLICATIONS OF AI IN HRM
5.1 Recruitment and Talent Acquisition
AI-powered Applicant Tracking Systems (ATS) now screen thousands of resumes within
seconds, matching candidate profiles to job requirements using natural language processing.
Chatbots conduct preliminary interviews and answer candidate queries round the clock, while
predictive algorithms rank candidates on the likelihood of job success. Indian IT firms have
widely adopted such tools to manage high-volume campus and lateral hiring.
5.2 Onboarding and Training
AI-driven learning management systems personalize training content based on an employee's
role, skill gaps and learning pace. Virtual assistants guide new joinees through
documentation, policy queries and induction schedules, reducing the administrativ e load on
HR personnel and improving the new-hire experience.
5.3 Performance Management
Machine learning models aggregate data from multiple sources — project outcomes, peer
feedback and behavioral indicators — to generate continuous, evidence -based perfo rmance
insights, moving organizations away from purely annual, subjective appraisal cycles.
5.4 Attrition Prediction and Retention
Predictive analytics models flag employees at high risk of leaving by analyzing patterns such
as engagement scores, tenure, c ompensation benchmarks and workload indicators, enabling
HR teams to design proactive retention interventions rather than reactive exit management.
5.5 Employee Engagement and Sentiment Analysis
AI-enabled sentiment analysis tools mine employee surveys, in ternal communication and
feedback channels to gauge morale and satisfaction in near real time, allowing organizations
to respond to emerging concerns before they escalate into disengagement or attrition.

6. CASE ILLUSTRATIONS FROM INDIAN ORGANISATIONS
A review of secondary sources reveals several illustrative instances of AI adoption in HRM
within Indian organizations. Large IT services firms such as Infosys and TCS have deployed
AI-based virtual recruiters that shortlist candidates and schedule interview s without human
intervention for high -volume entry -level hiring, substantially reducing the average time -to-
hire. Wipro has used machine learning models to map internal skill inventories against
emerging project requirements, enabling more efficient intern al redeployment rather than
external hiring. In the banking and financial services sector, several private banks employ AI-
based chatbots to handle first -level employee queries related to leave, payroll and policy,
freeing HR business partners to focus on higher-value advisory work. Start -ups in the e -
commerce and logistics space, which experience extremely high attrition among frontline
staff, have adopted predictive attrition models that flag at -risk employees to their managers
weeks in advance, allowing timely retention conversations. These examples collectively
suggest that Indian adoption of AI in HRM is concentrated most heavily in high -volume,
transactional processes, with more cautious adoption in judgment -intensive areas such as
promotions and disciplinary decisions.

7. CHALLENGES AND ETHICAL CONCERNS
● Algorithmic bias: AI systems trained on historical data may inadverten tly perpetuate
existing biases related to gender, age or educational background.
● Transparency and explainability: Many AI models function as “black boxes,” making it
difficult for HR professionals to explain the rationale behind an automated decision to
affected employees.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management

ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 209

● Data privacy: Continuous monitoring of employee data for engagement or attrition
prediction raises significant privacy and consent concerns.
● Employee trust and acceptance: Resistance to AI -driven evaluation can arise where
employees feel reduced to data points rather than being treated as individuals.
● Skill and infrastructure gaps: Small and mid -sized Indian organizations often lack the
technical capability and budget required for robust AI-HRM implementation.
8. FINDINGS AND DISCUSSION
The review indicates that AI adoption in HRM delivers measurable gains in speed, cost -
efficiency and data -driven objectivity, particularly in high -volume functions such as
recruitment and training. Indian studies consistently report improved organizational
performance and reduced administrative workload following AI implementation. However,
the same body of literature cautions that these efficiency gains must be balanced against the
risks of algorithmic bias, reduced transparency and erosion of employee tru st. The evidence
suggests that the most effective adopters are organizations that treat AI as a decision -support
tool that augments, rather than replaces, human judgment — retaining human oversight at
critical decision points such as final hiring calls, di sciplinary actions and career progression
decisions. A second observation from the literature is the uneven pace of adoption across
organization size. Large IT and financial services firms, with greater digital infrastructure and
analytics capability, have  moved furthest in embedding AI across the employee lifecycle.
Small and medium enterprises, by contrast, report slower adoption, constrained primarily by
cost, limited in -house technical expertise and lower data maturity. This suggests that policy
and industry-body support — for instance, shared AI-HR platforms or sector-level guidelines
— could help narrow this gap and enable more equitable diffusion of AI -HRM capability
across the Indian corporate landscape.  A third theme relates to employee perception. Studies
reviewed here indicate that acceptance of AI in HRM is highest where its role is explicitly
framed as supportive rather than substitutive — for example, AI that speeds up screening but
leaves final selection to a human panel is viewed far more favo rably than AI that issues an
unexplained final decision. This reinforces the case for a “human -in-the-loop” design
philosophy in Indian AI-HRM implementations.
9. PROPOSED CONCEPTUAL FRAMEWORK
Based on the review, this paper proposes a three-layer framework for responsible AI adoption
in HRM: (i) the Efficiency Layer, where AI automates high-volume, low-judgment tasks such
as resume screening and query handling; (ii) the Augmentation Layer, where AI generates
insights and recommendations — such as attrition risk scores or training recommendations —
that are reviewed and acted upon by HR professionals rather than being auto -executed; and
(iii) the Governance Layer, comprising bias audits, data privacy safeguards, explainability
requirements and periodic human  review of algorithmic outcomes. Organizations that
structure their AI -HRM initiatives around these three layers are better positioned to capture
efficiency gains while managing the associated ethical and reputational risks.

10. SCOPE FOR FUTURE RESEARCH
This paper is conceptual and review -based in nature; it does not test hypotheses using
primary data. Future research could usefully undertake sector -wise empirical surveys across
Indian manufacturing, banking, healthcare and public -sector organizations to c ompare the
pace and pattern of AI -HRM adoption. Longitudinal studies tracking employee trust and
attrition outcomes before and after AI implementation would also strengthen the evidence
base. Further work could examine regulatory readiness in India — including data protection
compliance under the Digital Personal Data Protection Act — as it applies specifically to
employee data used in AI-driven HR systems.
11. CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence is no longer a peripheral experiment in Human Resource Managem ent;
it has become a mainstream enabler of efficiency across the employee lifecycle, from
recruitment to retention. For Indian organizations, the opportunity lies in leveraging AI to
reduce administrative burden and improve decision quality, while consciou sly safeguarding
fairness, transparency and the human element that remains central to people management. A
hybrid model — combining algorithmic efficiency with human empathy and ethical
oversight, as outlined in the conceptual framework above — offers the most sustainable path
forward for AI -enabled HRM in India. Future empirical research, particularly primary
survey-based studies across Indian industry sectors, would help validate and refine this
framework.
