STUDYING ETHICAL IMPLICATIONS AND INNOVATIONS IN AI-DRIVEN
MARKETING: A COMPREHENSIVE LITERATURE ANALYSIS
Ms. P. MENAKA
Assistant Professor
 Department of Business Administration
  Valliammal College for Women Chennai1.
MS.D.ANITHA DEVI
Student
 Department of Business Administration
 Valliammal College for Women.Chennai2.
ABSTRACT
This review of the literature considers on what extent Artificial Intelligence (AI) is
fundamentally changing marketing practices, zeroing in on personalized marketing, customer
engagement, and decision -making practices. The use of AI, such as mac hine learning,
predictive analytics and natural language processing, transforms holistic marketing strategy -
scape by making it possible for corporations to deal with issues concerning personalized
customer experience, optimizing campaigns and improving ope rational efficiency. This
review integrates various studies arriving at findings through delving into the following
methods: case studies, meta-analyses in use of bibliometric analysis, surveys, and opinions of
recognized experts operating in different loc al territories like India, the USA, Canada, and
Europe. Important aspects are the realization that AI can be used in prediction of consumer
behaviour, marketing automation, and also personalized recommendations being considered
in the context of ethical is sues arising from AI inclusion. This paper provides a thorough
understanding of the current space in which AI is leveraged within marketing and its likely
implications on future marketing strategies thereby contributing useful insights to businesses
and th e professionals interested in studying about how to continue navigating in the area.
This has revolutionized how companies interact with customers, optimizing strategies, and
enabling them to have individual experiences.
1.INTRODUCTION
This shift happened over the last ten years. AI ceased to be a part of the science fiction of the
future and broke out to become one of the major essentials for today's marketing
professionals. Powered by AI data-capturing tools and platforms, businesses now get strength
through analysing vast stores of customer data, forecast behaviour, predict trends, automate
routine jobs, and show relevant content to the consumer with a click. This advancement not
only improves marketing efficiency but makes it possible to delve further i nto consumer
preferences and behaviour, thereby creeping deep even further by providing consumers an
increasingly personalized and impactful visit.  Despite these advances, several challenges
present themselves in the field of marketing, especially the appl ication of AI. These include
issues of data privacy, ethical considerations as well as the requisite manpower that could
effectively implement some of the AI tools. Furthermore, as AI refines, businesses

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 284

everywhere need to ensure that they stay innovative by incorporating the latest AI tools while
evaluating the risks and benefits balancing the innovative leap.
1.1 Rationale
Artificial intelligence has lately escalated into a powerful transformational force across
industries such as marketing. It has truly renovated things from personalized content into
accurate predicted analytics. The AI has opened up doorways for dynamic ma rketing strategy
possibilities coming from colossal datasets infusing fresh life back into companies.
Technologies like this invite automation which can be utilized in elaborate processes,
optimize interactions, and provide valuable insights of how consume rs behave. The several
dimensions of marketing where AI is employed include customer segmentation, marketing
automation, and such other areas as content personalization.
1.2 Objectives
The purpose of the present review is to provide a comprehensive review  of research existing
at the current moment concerning the impact of AI on marketing. It may aim, for example, to
explore the integration of AI in marketing strategies as well as its effectiveness, challenges,
and potential future development. The paper ai ms to synthesize existing theory to suggest
exactly how AI could potentially reshape marketing practices and point out directions for
further academic inquiry.
1.3 Scope of the study
This review comprises evidence from various regions, business sectors, and methodologies. It
presents some cases from e -marketing companies, B2B marketing scenarios, and service
industries in order to explore the role of AI as an enabler in enhancing marketing outcomes as
building customer relationships and organizational performance. The study also covers issues
of ethics and practice to which marketers are probably exposed for adopting AI.
2. LITERATURE REVIEW
Actually, there is an essential role played by Artificial Intelligence (AI) and this could be the
most recent technological development in the area of marketing because it changes the
complete process of interactions with customers, data -oriented decision -making and an
optimization of strategies within a marketing plan. This article investigates the developing
literature and aims to discuss the key themes, methodologies and findings of various
researches done over the years on AI and its role in marketing.
2.1 AI Applications in marketing
AI in marketing has been done in various domains, ranging from personal recommendations
to predictive analytics and automation of analyses over the majority of such work related to
content optimization. Machine Learning al gorithm’s efficiency in forecasting customers’
behaviour and their high level of targeting and personalized customer experience in targeting
advertisements has been identified as two of the obvious examples (Kumar et al., 2024).
These predictive models, of ten run on AI, prove to be one of the important things while
figuring out consumers’ preferences in segmentation, and this has contributed to better client

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 285

retention (Thakur & Kushwaha, 2023). Another significant example is given by Babatunde et
al. (2024) who discuss the capabilities of AI that could be implemented in remora for SMEs
in data analysis as well as creating personalized marketing campaigns; that drastically
increases customer engagement and sales in an SME.
2.2 Theoretical Foundations, Methodologies
Research on AI in marketing generally covers a broad spectrum of quantitative and
qualitative methodologies (structured as well as semi -structured interviews, in -depth
interviews, case study designs, structured survey designs (with Likert Scale), et c.): case
studies, surveys, and bibliometric analyses (Batista et al., 2021). For instance, Odetunde et al.
(2024) discuss the theoretical underpinnings of AI -driven consumer engagement strategies.
Several studies, exemplified by Sheshadri et al. (2024), c onduct empirical research into the
impact of AI on consumer interaction across various service sectors -like banking and retail,
among others. Another way to exhibit how bibliometrics can provide gold nuggets to research
ideas is by highlighting how much ri ght bibliometrics can tell about the intellectual structure
of a field also in thematic evolutions; see Thakur & Kushwaha (2023), Schiessl et al., (2021).
2.3 Benefits of AI Marketing
AI's marketing benefits were really uplifting since it minimized process ing time and resource
requirements of organizations as it could automate numerous marketing tasks and
consequently enhance efficiency. Such AI -driven automatic devices are an effective
application for chatbots to provide customer assistance more rapidly by  creating a seamless
customer experience impression (Abu Daqar & Smoudy, 2019). In addition, AI technologies
lay the basis for assuming lucrative business decisions. Here the defining aspect of AI looked
towards big data itself and saw prediction of more p recise trends. The marketing side which
uses AI has a lot of bottlenecks. Undouthedly price, security is one of the most frequent
major problems of AI adoption in any industry: this is because by far less traditional tools
open up to more consumer database s in need of these concentrated platforms. So much so
that according to Nouri et al. (2023), a much better alternative instead is cracking down on
ethics alliances or framework development. Meanwhile, the focus would be on transparency
in AI marketing as usual. Moreover, cases are put up accusing biases to accompany the use of
software that discriminated between certain consumer groups by rendering them invisible
during marketing campaigns (Odetunde et al., 2024).
2.4 AI and e-commerce
AI technologies have been critically enriching within modern marketing practice, as it is
offering various applications in the personal marketing, recommendation systems, predictive
analytics, and automating customer service control. However, the point of convergence is
probably on developing the deep individual customer experience that helps to ease the entire
marketing processes. The new automated tools may help to bring about more enhancement to
customer responsibility and how services are delivered to t he customer. According to the

belief, it really aptly collects and analyses large data sets to predict, and consequently, market
will be able to predict the buying patterns of the customers and revamp their products'
features. Indeed, with the new innovati ons, AI is proving itself very effective in enhancing
customer-based technologies for marketing applications.  AI is considered to bring about a
tremendous transformation for the marketing team and business as it relates to a digital move.
This is what was highlighted in the work by Rathore (2016) revealing that AI is of suspicious
importance as it was shown that AI is addressing marketing operations through new digital
skills needed by the marketers. The move to ascend to AI marketing indeed gets to realize
several key difficulties that need to be understood as per organizational readiness. Such tools
as "suites" of many new tools particularly bespoke for negotiating this new, more difficult
terrain, and acquire by mastering the necessary skills within it to  respond to the same
strategic partnership with artificial intelligence for full marketing benefit. AI in everyday e -
commerce. There’re downsides there, which unfortunately are pretty scary for humanity.
Guidelines on the use of Artificial Intelligence rel ate to the prevention of negative
implications of AI evil in terms of enhancing people.
 2.5 Changing the Customer Behaviour
Artificial Intelligence (AI) has contributed in providing various grounds for company

AI-ENABLED WORK AND WOMEN’S WORK–LIFE INTEGRATION:
REIMAGINING FLEXIBLE WORK AND THE FUTURE OF BUSINESS

Dr.Devi Shree.V
, Department of Business Administration
Valliammal College for Women, Chennai
Ms.P.Ammu
Department of Business Administration
Valliammal College for Women, Chennai
ABSTRACT
The growing participation of women in professional employment has changed how
organisations and families und erstand work, career and responsibility. Yet professional
commitments continue alongside childcare, household management and other family
responsibilities. The rapid expansion of artificial intelligence (AI), digital technologies,
remote work and flexible work arrangements is changing how, when and where work is
performed. These developments may create flexibility and autonomy, but they may also
increase work intensity, digital connectivity and uncertainty about career progression.  This
conceptual paper examines the relationship between AI-enabled work, women’s employment
and work–life integration. Drawing on work–family conflict theory, work–family enrichment
theory and boundary-management perspectives, it explores the conditions under which digital
technologies can support rather than intensify challenges faced by working women. A
conceptual framework links AI -enabled technologies with work redesign, flexibility,
employee autonomy, boundary management and work –life integration. It also recognises a
risk pathway involving excessive connectivity, work intensification, unequal access to AI and
inadequate organisational support. The paper concludes that AI -enabled business should be
approached through a human-centred perspective that combines technological efficiency with
autonomy, inclusion and sustainable working practices.

Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Women at Work, Work –Life Integration, Work –Family
Conflict, Digital Work, Flexible Work, Boundary Management, Future of Work

1. INTRODUCTION
The nature o f work has changed considerably with the growth of women's participation in
paid employment, changing family structures and the expansion of digital technologies.
Professional responsibilities increasingly coexist with childcare, household management and
other family demands. Traditional work –family perspectives often treat these domains as
competing for limited time and energy, while work –family enrichment highlights the
possibility that resources from one domain can support the other. Boundary -management
research further shows that the relationship between work and home depends on how
boundaries are created and negotiated.  Artificial intelligence is adding a new dimension to
this relationship. AI can automate routine tasks, support flexible work and change  how jobs
are organised. At the same time, it can increase digital connectivity, work intensity,
surveillance and unequal access to opportunities. The effect on women's work –life
experiences therefore depends on organisational choices as much as on technol ogy itself.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 292

This conceptual paper examines how AI -enabled work may influence women's work –life
integration and develops a framework linking technology, work redesign, flexibility,
employee autonomy and boundary management. It also considers organisational support,
digital skills, family support and inclusive HR practices as important contextual conditions.
2. OBJECTIVES
1. To examine the changing nature of women's professional and family roles.
2. To explain the movement from the traditional concept of wor k–family balance towards
work–life integration.
3. To examine how AI and digital technologies may influence women's participation in
employment.
4. To identify the opportunities and challenges associated with AI -enabled and flexible work
arrangements.
5. T o develop a conceptual framework linking AI -enabled work, flexibility, boundary
management and women's work–life integration.
6. To discuss the role of organisations and human resource practices in creating inclusive and
sustainable AI-enabled workplaces.
3. CHANGING ROLES OF WOMEN IN WORK AND FAMILY
Women's participation in paid employment has expanded across professional, managerial and
service occupations, while family responsibilities have not necessarily been redistributed to
the same extent. Women may  therefore manage employment alongside childcare, household
work and other forms of care. This makes the organisation of work important for both career
continuity and family well -being. Digital technologies have changed the location and timing
of work, but  flexibility does not automatically remove role pressures. The value of AI -
enabled work depends on whether technology gives employees meaningful control over tasks
and time or simply extends work into personal hours.
4. FROM WORK–LIFE BALANCE TO WORK–LIFE INTEGRATION
The concept of work –life balance has been widely used to describe the relationship between
professional and personal responsibilities. It generally suggests the need to maintain an
appropriate equilibrium between work and other aspects of life.  While this concept remains
useful, it may also imply that work and personal life are two separate domains that must
constantly be kept in balance.  The concept of work –life integration provides a somewhat
different perspective. Integration recognises that professional and personal responsibilities
may overlap and interact throughout everyday life. Individuals may move between these
domains depending on their circumstances, responsibilities and available resources.  Grady
and McCarthy (2008) demonstrate the c omplexity of work –life experiences among
professional working mothers. Similarly, Wattis, Standing and Yerkes (2013) emphasise that
mothers' experiences of work–life balance involve contradictions and negotiations rather than
a simple process of maintaining equal time between work and family.
Young (2018) also highlights the importance of flexibility in negotiating motherhood and
careers. For the purpose of this paper, work–life integration refers to:
“The process through which individuals, families and org anisations negotiate professional,

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 293

family and personal responsibilities in ways that enable meaningful participation across
different areas of life.”  Integration does not mean that work and family must receive equal
amounts of time. Rather, it concerns the  creation of conditions in which professional and
personal responsibilities can coexist without one domain consistently undermining the other.
This perspective is particularly relevant in the context of digital work because technology can
make the boundaries between professional and personal life more flexible.
5. THEORETICAL FOUNDATIONS
Three perspectives guide the conceptual framework. Work –family conflict explains how
incompatible demands, limited time and competing expectations can create strain when wo rk
and family roles overlap. Work –family enrichment provides a complementary view by
recognising that skills, resources and positive experiences gained in one domain can benefit
the other. Boundary management focuses on how individuals and organisations cr eate,
maintain or negotiate boundaries between work and non -work domains. Together, these
perspectives suggest that AI -enabled flexibility may have positive or negative consequences
depending on job design, autonomy, organisational support and employees' a bility to manage
boundaries.
6. AI AND THE CHANGING NATURE OF WORK
Artificial Intelligence is increasingly becoming part of organisational work. Its applications
include information processing, communication, documentation, scheduling, data analysis,
customer service and decision support.  The significance of AI for employees extends beyond
automation. AI can influence the design of jobs, allocation of tasks, required skills,
performance expectations and patterns of communication.  For women, particularly tho se
managing substantial responsibilities outside employment, several potential benefits are
relevant.
6.1 Reduction of Routine Work
AI can assist with repetitive administrative and information -processing tasks. Where such
assistance genuinely reduces workl oad, employees may be able to devote more time to
complex professional activities or responsibilities outside work.
6.2 Greater Flexibility
Digital tools can enable employees to complete some tasks independently of a fixed
workplace. Flexible work may redu ce commuting requirements and provide greater control
over when and where particular activities are performed.
6.3 Productivity Enhancement
AI can assist employees in completing certain activities more efficiently. OECD research
indicates that AI can gener ate productivity benefits, although outcomes depend substantially
on how technology is introduced and managed.
6.4 New Forms of Employment
Digital technologies have contributed to the expansion of remote, hybrid, freelance and
project-based work. Such arra ngements may provide additional options for individuals who

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 294

require flexibility.
6.5 Career Continuity
Flexible digital work may help some women remain connected to their professions during
periods when conventional full -time arrangements become difficult because of family
responsibilities. However, these possibilities should not be interpreted as evidence that AI
automatically improves women's work –life experiences. The same technology may increase
workload, accelerate work processes or create expectations of constant availability.
7. WOMEN AND AI IN THE FUTURE OF EMPLOYMENT
The relationship between women and AI is complex because technological change affects
occupations, tasks and workers differently.  OECD research has highlighted gender
differences in acc ess to AI -related employment opportunities and productivity -enhancing AI
tools. Lane (2024) also discusses the potential effects of AI on women and other groups of
workers. The ILO's recent research draws attention to the relationship between generative AI ,
occupational segregation and gender equality. Exposure to generative AI should not
automatically be interpreted as job elimination. In many occupations, technological change
may instead transform tasks, skills and working conditions. Two broad possibilit ies therefore
emerge.
Potential opportunities
AI may:
• reduce repetitive work;
• support flexible working;
• improve productivity;
• create new forms of employment;
• facilitate remote work;
• support career continuity; and
• allow employees to devote more time to higher-value activities.
Potential risks
AI may also:
• intensify work;
• increase expectations of availability;
• reproduce existing inequalities;
• create unequal access to technological opportunities;
• increase workplace surveillance;
• widen digital skill gaps; and
• transform occupations in which women are highly represented.
The important question is therefore not simply whether AI will create or eliminate jobs.
A broader question is:
What kind of work will women perform, under what cond itions, with what degree of
autonomy, and with what opportunities to combine professional and personal
responsibilities?
This shifts the discussion from technology alone towards the design and governance of work.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 295

8. DIGITAL WORK AND FLEXIBILITY: OPPORTUNITY OR NEW PRESSURE?
Flexible work arrangements have become an important organisational response to changing
employee needs.
Flexible work may include:
• flexible working hours;
• hybrid work;
• remote work;
• compressed work schedules;
• part-time employment;
• job sharing;
• flexible leave arrangements; and
• technology-enabled work from alternative locations.
For working mothers, such arrangements may reduce commuting time and provide greater
control over the organisation of work.  However, flexib ility does not necessarily produce
positive outcomes in every situation.  Consider a situation in which an employee is permitted
to work from home but is expected to remain digitally available throughout the day. Although
the employee is technically working  flexibly, the arrangement may extend rather than reduce
working time. The ILO has highlighted both the potential of flexible work to support work –
life balance and the risks associated with inequality, career progression, social protection,
discrimination and isolation.  Therefore, flexibility needs to be accompanied by autonomy,
manageable workloads and clear organisational boundaries.  This is particularly important in
AI-enabled workplaces, where digital systems can make it easier to allocate tasks, monito r
activity and communicate continuously.
9.THE ROLE OF ORGANISATIONS IN AI -ENABLED WORK –LIFE
INTEGRATION
Technology alone cannot create sustainable work–life integration.
Organisations determine how AI is implemented, how performance is evaluated, how
employees are expected to communicate and whether flexible work is genuinely supported.
9.1 Flexible Work Policies
Organisations should establish transparent flexible -work policies rather than relying entirely
on informal managerial discretion.
9.2 Outcome-Based Performance
Where appropriate, employee performance should be assessed through clearly defined
outcomes rather than constant physical or digital availability.
9.3 Equal Access to Digital Skills
Women should have equitable access to AI training, digital learning and technology -related
career opportunities.
9.4 Protection from Excessive Connectivity
Organisations should establish reasonable expectations concerning communication outside
formal working hours.
9.5 Inclusive AI Implementation
AI adoption shoul d consider differences across gender, occupation, age, career stage and

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 296

other relevant dimensions.
9.6 Career Development
Flexible working should not become an invisible barrier to promotion. Employees using
flexible arrangements should continue to receive  professional development, mentoring and
advancement opportunities.
9.7 Human-Centred Technology
AI implementation should consider employee autonomy, privacy, fairness and work quality
alongside organisational efficiency. These organisational practices det ermine whether
technology functions primarily as a source of support or becomes another mechanism for
increasing work intensity.
10. CONCEPTUAL FRAMEWORK FOR WOMEN'S WORK –LIFE INTEGRATION
IN THE AI ERA
The proposed framework positions AI -enabled technologies as the starting point of change.
AI can influence work redesign and flexibility, which may increase employee autonomy and
boundary-management capacity, leading to improved women's work –life integration. The
relationship is shaped by organisational suppo rt, digital skills, family support and inclusive
HR policies.  The framework also recognises a risk pathway: AI -enabled technology may
contribute to work intensification, excessive connectivity, surveillance or unequal access to
skills and opportunities. Th ese conditions can blur boundaries and increase work –family
conflict. Thus, AI is treated as an enabling technology whose effects depend on the
organisational and social conditions surrounding its use.
11. FROM BALANCING ROLES TO INTEGRATING ROLES
Work–life integration moves beyond the idea that work and family must always be kept
separate. In AI-enabled workplaces, professional and personal responsibilities may overlap in
practical ways. The objective is therefore not to eliminate boundaries but to  make them
manageable. Integration is more sustainable when employees have autonomy, predictable
expectations, supportive policies and the ability to disconnect. For women, this approach
recognises that career participation and family responsibilities can coexist when work is
designed around human needs rather than continuous availability.
12. IMPLICATIONS FOR MANAGEMENT AND BUSINESS
The paper has several implications for business and HRM. First, productivity should be
considered alongside employee autonomy  and sustainable workloads. Second, AI
implementation should include transparent processes, digital -skills development and equal
access to opportunities. Third, organisations should protect employees from excessive
connectivity through clear communication and boundary practices. Finally, career
development and flexible -work policies should account for different life stages. AI strategy
should therefore include work design, inclusion and employee well -being rather than
focusing only on technological efficiency.
13. CHALLENGES AND LIMITATIONS
This paper is conceptual and does not test the proposed relationships with primary data. The
effects of AI -enabled work may also differ across occupations, industries, organisational
cultures and family circumstances. Fle xible work can provide autonomy but may also
intensify workloads or reproduce existing inequalities. The framework should therefore be
empirically tested using diverse samples and validated measures before broad conclusions are

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 297

drawn.
14. FUTURE RESEARCH DIRECTIONS
The conceptual arguments presented in this paper provide several opportunities for future
empirical research.
1. The relationship between AI adoption and women's work–life integration.
2. Whether AI -supported productivity actually reduces working  hours or instead increases
output expectations.
3. The relationship between hybrid work and career progression among working mothers.
4. Gender differences in access to AI training and workplace AI tools.
5. The influence of organisational culture on flexible-work outcomes.
6. The relationship between digital connectivity and work–family conflict.
7. How AI can be designed and implemented in ways that support employee autonomy and
inclusion.
8. Differences in AI -enabled work experiences across managerial, professional,
administrative and other occupational groups.
9. Whether organisational support moderates the relationship between AI -enabled flexibility
and work–life integration.
10. How women themselves negotiate boundaries in AI -enabled hybrid and remote
workplaces.
Empirical research across different industries and occupational groups would help determine
whether the conceptual relationships proposed in this paper hold across different
organisational contexts.
15. CONCLUSION
Artificial Intelligence is ch anging how work is organised, performed and connected across
time and place. For women, these changes can create flexibility and support career continuity,
but they can also intensify work and blur boundaries. The conceptual framework proposed
here links A I-enabled technologies with work redesign, flexibility, autonomy and boundary
management, while recognising organisational support, digital skills and family context. The
central implication is that technology alone does not determine work –life outcomes.
Organisations need to design AI -enabled work around sustainable workloads, inclusive
access, employee autonomy and clear boundaries. Such an approach can connect
technological innovation with more sustainable forms of participation in the future of
business.

A STUDY ON THE FUNNEL COLLAPSE IN SOCIAL COMMERCE AND ITS
IMPACT ON GENERATION Z IMPULSE BUYING BEHAVIOR.
KRUTHIKA.N
Assistant Professo
Department of Business Administration
Valliammal College for Women,Chennai
MONISHA .P
II MBA, Department of Business Administration
 Valliammal College for Women, Chennai
ABSTRACT
The emergence of e -commerce platforms has largely changed the level of consumer buying
behavior especially the generation Z consumers who are heavily concentrated in the digital
world. Interactive capabilities, which have been actively used on online shopping platforms
in recent years, are personalized recommendations, influencer marketing, flash sales, and
targeted advertisements, to increase u ser engagement and output buying. Although these
solutions enhance convenience and accessibility, they can also form what can be termed as
the E-Commerce Experience Trap where consumers fall into a psychological trap of being
driven by design and persuasion mechanism of online shopping settings. In this review paper,
the psychological impacts of online shopping on Generation Z are analyzed considering the
critical factors driving this phenomenon, which include emotional triggers, fear of missing
out (FOMO), social proof, personalization algorithm, and instant gratification. Other aspects
examined in the study include the impact of social media and influencer marketing on
consumer decision-making. Through synthesis of the available literature, the review poin ts
out the opportunities and threats linked to immersive e-commerce experiences and stresses on
the necessity of responsible digital marketing and enhanced consumer awareness.
Keywords: Consumer Behavior, Online Shopping Psychology, Social Media, Influence r
Marketing, Buying Behavior
INTRODUCTION
Social commerce has transformed the traditional way consumers discover and purchase
products by combining social media interaction with online shopping. Platforms such as
Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, and Facebook allow consumers to discover products through
influencers, short videos, reviews, livestreams, and personalized content. Traditionally,
consumers moved through a purchase funnel from awareness to interest, consideration,
evaluation, and purchase. However, in social commerce, these stages can occur
simultaneously or within a very short period, creating what can be described as “funnel
collapse.” Impulse buying refers to an unplanned and immediate purchasing decision made
without extens ive prior consideration. In social commerce, interactive content, social
influence, emotional stimulation, personalized recommendations, and promotional techniques
can create an environment in which consumers make quick purchasing decisions. Therefore,
the collapse of the traditional purchase funnel may have an important relationship with the
impulse buying behavior of Generation Z.  The study focuses on understanding how social

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 300

commerce compresses the traditional consumer decision -making journey and how thi s
transformation affects the impulse buying behavior of Gen Z. It also considers important
factors such as influencer influence, social proof, FOMO, trust, engaging content, and
promotional stimuli. Understanding these factors can help researchers and busi nesses better
understand the changing nature of consumer behavior in the digital marketplace.
LITERATURE REVIEW
Nasution, Indria &Febriaty (2022)examined the impact of social commerce on the impulse
buying behavior of Generation Z. The researchers collecte d data from 51 Gen Z respondents
through an online questionnaire and used regression analysis. The study concluded that social
commerce has a significant impact on the impulse buying behavior of Generation Z. This
study establishes the basic relationship b etween social-commerce activities and spontaneous
purchasing among Gen Z.
Tran & Khoa (2026) studied the consumer decision journey of Gen Z in cross -platform
commerce. Their research explains that Gen Z consumers often discover products through
influencer content and livestream shopping on social -commerce platforms and may then
move to e -marketplaces to complete their purchases. The study challenges the traditional
linear purchase funnel by describing a more complex, multi -platform and non -linear
consumer journey. This study is particularly relevant to the concept of funnel collapse in
social commerce.
Li, Arham & Majid (2026) investigated consumer perceived value and impulsive buying in
social commerce, focusing on the moderating role of FOMO. Using data from 398 social -
commerce users in China, the study found that platform interactivity and social cues increase
both utilitarian and hedonic perceived value, which subsequently increase the urge to buy
impulsively. The study also found that FOMO strengthens the relationship between perceived
value and impulsive buying, particularly for hedonic value.
Gera, Ahluwalia & Nagpal (2026) examined the relationship between FOMO and impulse
buying among urban Indian Gen Z consumers during Myntra  flash sales. Based on an online
survey of 100 Gen Z consumers, the study reported that FOMO was a strong predictor of
impulse purchases. Limited -stock alerts and countdown timers were also found to amplify
FOMO. This study is useful for understanding how urgency, scarcity and digital promotional
techniques can shorten the consumer decision -making process and encourage impulse
buying.
OBJECTIVES OF THE STUDY
1. To examine the concept of funnel collapse in social commerce and understand how it
changes the traditional consumer purchase journey.
2. To analyze the impact of social -commerce factors such as influencers, FOMO, social
proof, discounts, and engaging content on Gen Z impulse buying behavior.
3. To identify the relationship between funnel collapse and impulse b uying behavior
among Generation Z in the social-commerce environment.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 301

NEED FOR THE STUDY
The rapid growth of social commerce has changed the way Generation Z discovers and
purchases products. Unlike the traditional buying process, social -media platforms al low
consumers to move quickly from product awareness to purchase through influencers, short
videos, reviews, livestreams, discounts, and personalized recommendations. This creates a
need to understand funnel collapse and how the shortening of the purchase journey may
encourage impulse buying. The study is needed to understand the factors that influence Gen
Z's spontaneous purchasing decisions, particularly FOMO, social influence, influencer
marketing, attractive content, and promotional offers. The findings  can help marketers
understand changing consumer behavior and design responsible and effective social -
commerce strategies, while also contributing to academic research on the relationship
between funnel collapse and impulse buying.
METHODOLOGY
This study i s conducted based on both primary and secondary data sources. The correlation
method of study are used in this research paper.The change in value of one variable produces
a changes in the value of other variables. In that case it is said that the variables are correlated
or there is correlation between the two variables. The population of the study consists of
Generation Z consumers (born between 1997 and 2012) who use social -commerce platforms
such as Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, and other online shopping p latforms. The data were
collected using a structured non -disguised questionnaire and analysed the responses using
descriptive statistics, correlation methods.
ANALYSIS AND DISCUSSIONS

The correlation analysis shows that funnel collapse factors have a positive relationship with
Gen Z impulse buying behaviour. The significance value of 0.000 shows that the relationship
is statistically significant. Therefore, funnel collapse factors are associated with increased
impulse buying behaviour among Gen Z respondents.The Pearson correlation value of 0.581
indicates a positive relationship between the two variables.
CONCLUSION
The study concludes that the collapse of the traditional social commerce funnel has a
significant influence on Gen Z’s impulse buying behaviour. Social media platforms have
changed the buying journey by allowing co nsumers to discover, evaluate, and purchase
products within a short period of time.  For Gen Z consumers, attractive content, influencer
recommendations, personalized advertisements, limited -time offers, discounts, social proof,
and seamless in-app purchasing can reduce the time between product discovery and purchase.
This makes impulse buying more immediate and frequent.  Overall, the study shows that
funnel collapse is reshaping the social commerce journey and contributing to changes in Gen
Z impulse buying behaviour. Understanding these changes can help businesses develop more
effective and consumer-focused social commerce strategies.

THE PARADOX OF PROGRESS: A STUDY ON INCREASING HUMAN
DEPENDENCY ON AI IN DAILY BUSINESS OPERATIONS, WITH REFERENCE
TO SMALL-SCALE BUSINESS

Dr. V NITHYA PRIYADHARSHINI
Associate Professor of Business administration
Valliammal College for Women

BHARATHI P
II MBA, Valliammal College for Women
ABSTRACT

Artificial Intelligence (AI) is becoming common in today's business world. Earlier, AI was
mostly used by big companies. Now, small businesses are also using AI in their daily work.
AI is used for marketing, billing, customer service, writing, data handling and decision -
making. It helps businesses save time and complete work faster. It can also reduce repeated
work done by employees. But the increasing use of AI can also create human dependency.
When people use AI for many activities, they may slowly depend on it more. At the same
time, A I cannot completely replace human thinking and experience. This paper studies the
use of AI in small -scale businesses and explains its benefits and limitations. It also looks at
the role of AI in productivity, decision -making, customer service, marketing a nd billing.
OECD research found that 31% of the SMEs surveyed were already using generative AI.
However, the OECD research also focuses on employee skills, problems, preparation and
proper use of AI. Therefore, this study does not simply say that small bus inesses are
dependent on AI. It studies how AI is helping businesses and how increasing use of AI can
affect human involvement.

KEYWORDS Artificial Intelligence, AI, Small Business, SMEs, Human Dependency,
Business, AI Adoption, Productivity, Decision -Making, Customer Service, Marketing,
Billing, Automation, Human Involvement, Technology

INTRODUCTION

Artificial Intelligence, also called AI, is becoming an important part of business today. AI is
not used only by big companies. Small businesses can also us e AI in their daily work. For
example, AI can help in marketing, customer service, billing, writing, data work and many
other activities. AI tools are also easy to use now. Because of this, small businesses can use
AI without having a large technical team.  AI can save time and help employees finish their
work faster. But there is also another side to this progress. AI is made to help humans, but
people may slowly start depending on it too much. For example, a person may use AI even
for simple work that they can do by themselves. This can reduce human involvement in some
activities. However, we cannot say that all small businesses are already dependent on AI.
OECD research found that 31% of the SMEs surveyed were using generative AI. The

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 305

research also studied  employee skills, problems, preparation and proper use of AI. So, this
paper studies both the good and bad sides of AI and looks at how humans and AI can work
together.

REVIEW OF LITERATURE

Schwaeke et al. (2025)

Schwaeke et al. studied the use of AI in small and medium businesses. Their study explained
that businesses need money, knowledge, employees and proper technology to use AI. Having
AI technology alone is not enough. Employees should also know how to use it properl y. This
is related to the present study because the present study also looks at AI use in small
businesses. However, this paper mainly looks at the daily use of AI and how regular use of
AI may increase human dependence.

OECD (2025)

The OECD studied more  than 5,000 SMEs from seven countries. The study found that 31%
of the SMEs surveyed were already using generative AI. It also found that 65% of the SMEs
using generative AI said that AI helped improve employee performance.  This study is useful
for the present paper because the OECD did not simply say that SMEs are dependent on AI.
It also studied employee skills, problems and preparation. The present paper also follows this
idea and studies whether regular use of AI can slowly increase human dependence.

D'Amico et al. (2026)

D'Amico et al. studied the use of AI in small and medium businesses. Their study explained
that businesses need proper knowledge and skills to use AI. Employees should understand
how to use AI correctly.  This is related to the present  study because employees should not
simply depend on AI. They should understand the work and use AI only when it is helpful.
The present paper also studies whether AI is being used as a helping tool or as a replacement
for human work.

Indian MSME Study (2025)

A study on Indian MSMEs discussed the problems faced by small businesses while using AI.
Some businesses may face problems related to money, technology and lack of knowledge.
This is important for the present study because small businesses may not ha ve the same
money and resources as large companies. They may use simple AI tools for marketing,
customer service and other work. Human employees are still needed to use and check the AI
tools.
Landini and Machado (2026)
Landini and Machado studied the use of AI in SMEs. Their study showed that AI can help
businesses improve their work and decision -making. At the same time, businesses can face

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 306

problems such as lack of money, lack of knowledge and difficulty in using new technology.
The present study also lo oks at both sides of AI. AI can help businesses, but humans still
need to check the information given by AI and make the final decisions.

METHODOLOGY

This study is mainly based on secondary data. Information is taken from research papers,
journals, reports and other published sources. The study mainly focuses on the use of AI in
small-scale businesses.  The study looks at different uses of AI in business. These include
marketing, customer service, billing, office work, productivity and decision -making. It also
looks at the benefits and problems of using AI. Human involvement is also studied because
the main topic of this paper is human dependency on AI.  The information from different
sources is studied and compared. The study does not say that every small b usiness is
dependent on AI. Instead, it studies the increasing use of AI and how this use may increase
human dependency. It also explains why humans are still important even when AI is used.

RESULTS AND DISCUSSION

Use of AI in Small Businesses

AI is becoming easier for small businesses to use. Many AI tools are available online and are
easy to use. Small businesses can use AI for writing, marketing, customer communication,
data work and other activities.  But using AI does not mean that a business is c ompletely
dependent on it. OECD research found that 31% of the SMEs surveyed were using generative
AI. This shows that AI use is increasing, but it does not mean that every small business uses
AI. So, we can say that AI adoption is increasing, but complete  dependency cannot be
assumed.

AI in Daily Business Work

AI can help employees with many daily activities. It can help write emails, prepare
documents, create reports and organise information. It can also help answer simple customer
questions. This can b e very useful for small businesses because they may have only a few
employees. AI can reduce their workload and save time. But if employees use AI for every
small task, they may slowly become dependent on it. Therefore, employees should also know
how to do the work without AI.

AI and Productivity

AI can help employees finish their work faster. It can reduce repeated work and save time.
For example, AI can help prepare content, reports and simple information. According to
OECD research, many SMEs using gen erative AI said that it improved employee
performance. But doing work quickly does not always mean that the work is good. AI can
sometimes give wrong information. Employees need to check the information before using it.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 307

Therefore, AI can improve productivity when humans also check the work.

AI and Decision-Making

AI can help businesses make decisions by looking at information. It can help understand
sales, customer choices and business trends. This can give business owners more information
before making a  decision.But AI should not make every decision by itself. Business owners
have their own experience and knowledge about their customers. AI may not understand
everything about the business. Therefore, AI can give suggestions, but humans should make
the final decision.

AI in Customer Service

AI can help businesses provide customer service. Chatbots and automatic replies can answer
simple questions. This can save time for employees and help customers get quick answers.
But some customer problems are not si mple. Customers may need to speak with a real
person. AI may also not understand a customer's feelings or special situation. So, AI can
handle simple questions, while employees should handle difficult customer problems.

AI in Marketing

AI can help small businesses with marketing. It can create social media posts, advertisements,
product descriptions and other promotional content. This is useful for small businesses
because they may not have a separate marketing team.  But AI -generated content may
sometimes look similar to the content of other businesses. It may also not understand the
business and its customers completely. Human creativity is still important in marketing.
Therefore, AI can help create content, but humans should decide what is suitable for t heir
business.

AI in Billing and Office Work

AI and other digital tools can help businesses with billing and office work. They can help
prepare bills, enter information, organise records and manage documents. This can save time
and reduce manual work. But billing and financial information need careful checking. If there
is a mistake, it can affect the business. Employees should check the bills and records before
finalising them. AI can make the work easier, but humans should still check it.

Human Involvement and AI Dependency

The main point of this study is human involvement. AI can make work easier and faster. But
when people use AI for almost everything, they may slowly start depending on it. For
example, a person may use AI to write even a simp le message or to get ideas for every small
problem. This does not mean that AI should not be used. AI is useful when it supports human
work. But humans should still think, check the information and make important decisions. AI

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 308

should be treated as a helpin g tool and not as a complete replacement for humans. A balance
between AI and human work is important for small businesses.

CONCLUSION
AI is becoming useful for small businesses. It can help in marketing, customer service,
billing, office work, productivi ty and decision -making. It can save time and reduce the
workload of employees. But increasing use of AI can also create human dependency if people
start using AI for every activity. However, we cannot say that all small businesses are already
dependent on AI. The OECD research shows that AI use is increasing, but problems like lack
of skills, preparation and proper use are still present. Therefore, AI should be used as a
helping tool and not as a complete replacement for human work. Humans are still importa nt
because they provide thinking, creativity, experience and final decisions. The main idea is
that AI and humans should work together. This can help small businesses get the benefits of
AI while keeping important human involvement.

Influencer Marketing and Consumer Buying Behaviour in the Cosmetic Industry: A
Review of Social Media Influence
Ashwin A, Rohith Kumar E, Guna C
First year B.Com. General,
 John Bosco Arts & Science College, Thiruvallur-602001.
Abstract
The rapid growth of social media has transformed the way cosmetic and beauty brands
communicate with consumers. Influencer marketing has emerged as an important digital
marketing strategy through which brands use influencers to create awareness, establish
credibility, engage audiences, and influence purchasing decisions. This study reviews
existing literature on the role of social media influencers in the cosmetic and beauty industry,
with particular emphasis on influencer credibility, authenticity, content  quality, consumer
trust, engagement, and purchase intention. The reviewed studies indicate that consumers
increasingly rely on influencer recommendations, product demonstrations, reviews, and
interactive content when evaluating beauty and cosmetic products. Instagram and other social
media platforms provide brands with opportunities to reach targeted consumer segments,
particularly younger and digitally active consumers. However, excessive promotional
content, lack of authenticity, and inadequate transpare ncy may reduce consumer trust. The
study concludes that effective influencer marketing depends on credible influencers, relevant
content, authentic communication, and strong alignment between influencers and cosmetic
brands. The review provides useful impl ications for cosmetic marketers seeking to develop
consumer-centred and digitally integrated promotional strategies.
Keywords: Influencer Marketing, Social Media, Cosmetic Industry, Consumer Buying
Behaviour, Influencer Credibility, Digital Marketing.
1. Introduction
The expansion of social media has significantly changed consumer behaviour and marketing
communication. Platforms such as Instagram, YouTube, and other digital channels have
enabled cosmetic brands to communicate directly with consumers through  visual content,
reviews, demonstrations, recommendations, and interactive campaigns. Within this
environment, social media influencers have become important intermediaries between brands
and consumers.  Influencer marketing is particularly relevant to the cosmetic and beauty
industry because consumers often seek product demonstrations, reviews, tutorials, and
personal recommendations before purchasing beauty products. Influencers can create
perceived authenticity and establish relationships with followers, thereby affecting consumer
perceptions and purchase intentions. Studies by collectively demonstrate the growing
importance of influencer and social media marketing in cosmetic purchasing behaviour.  The
literature suggests that influencer credibility, attra ctiveness, authenticity, content quality,
engagement, trust, and product recommendations are important factors affecting consumer
responses. At the same time, consumers may become sceptical when influencer content

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN: 978-81-180170-6-6                       DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 310

appears excessively commercial or lacks tr ansparency. Therefore, influencer marketing
effectiveness depends not only on the popularity of influencers but also on the quality and
authenticity of their communication.  This study therefore reviews the existing literature to
understand how influencer m arketing contributes to consumer buying behaviour in the
cosmetic industry and to identify important areas for further research.
2. Objectives of the Study
1. To examine the role of social media influencers in promoting cosmetic and beauty
products.
2. To identify the major influencer-related factors affecting consumer buying behaviour,
particularly credibility, authenticity, engagement, trust, and content quality.
3. To identify research gaps and future opportunities for influencer marketing research
in the cosmetic industry.
3. Limitations of the Study
The study is primarily based on previously published literature and therefore does not
generate new primary consumer data. Differences in research methods, sample sizes,
geographical contexts, and social media platforms across the reviewed studies may limit
direct comparison of findings. Several studies focus on specific consumer groups, such as
Generation Z or users in particular countries, which may restrict generalization to the wider
cosmetic consumer popula tion. In addition, social media trends, algorithms, influencer
practices, and consumer preferences change rapidly, meaning that findings may require
continuous updating.
4. Review of Literature
Xin, T. (2024), the study “Optimizing Influencer Marketing Strategies in the Beauty Industry:
Case Analysis”  (2024) examines the strategic role of influencers in promoting beauty
products. It highlights that influencer credibility, audience engagement, content quality, and
appropriate influencer–brand alignment can sig nificantly improve consumer awareness and
purchasing intentions. The study emphasizes selecting influencers whose image and audience
match the target market and developing authentic, engaging content rather than relying solely
on promotional messages. It c oncludes that systematic influencer selection and strategic
content planning can strengthen brand communication and marketing effectiveness in the
beauty industry.
Roach et al. (2025)  examine the dual impact of social media influencer marketing in the
cosmetic industry. The study finds that influencer credibility, authenticity, and consumer
engagement can enhance brand awareness and purchasing behaviour, while excessive
promotion and perceived inauthenticity may reduce consumer trust. The study emphasizes the
importance of credible and transparent influencer –brand partnerships for effective cosmetic
marketing.

Raj and Sundar (2024)  examine the impact of influencer marketing on consumers’ purchase

decisions. I t suggests that effective influencer communication can strengthen consumer

Mishra, Ilahi, and Maqbool (2026) examine the role of social media and influencer marketing
in promoting cosmetic brands. The study highli ghts that influencer credibility, engaging
social media content, and consumer interaction contribute to brand awareness and shape
consumer behaviour. It emphasizes influencer marketing as an important promotional
approach for cosmetic brands, particularly in reaching younger consumers.
Talreja and Chaturvedi  examine the effectiveness of Instagram influencers in advertising
cosmetic brands. The study finds that influencer credibility, authentic content, product
explanations, and engaging videos can influence consumers’ purchase decisions. It highlights
Instagram influencer advertising as an important promotional tool for increasing cosmetic
brand awareness, engagement, and sales.
Castillo, Jaramillo, and Sy (2022)  examine the effectiveness of social media inf luencers in
the cosmetic and skincare industry among Generation Z Filipinos. The study highlights that
influencer credibility, attractiveness, content quality, and engagement can shape consumers’
purchase intentions. It demonstrates the importance of influ encer marketing in reaching
Generation Z and promoting cosmetic and skincare products through social media.
Kaur and Chandra (2025)  examine the impact of influencer marketing on consumer
purchasing decisions in the cosmetic industry. The study finds that influencer credibility,
authenticity, engagement, and trust  are important factors shaping consumer purchase
intentions. It highlights Instagram, YouTube, and TikTok as key platforms for beauty -related
content and notes that consumers respond more positively  to genuine product reviews,
demonstrations, and educational content than overtly promotional messages. The study
emphasizes transparency and authentic influencer –brand relationships  as important for
effective cosmetic marketing. The research used a questi onnaire with 25 respondents, so its
findings provide exploratory rather than broadly generalizable evidence.
Bandyopadhyay and John  examine the effect of social media, influencer marketing, and
digital technologies on cosmetic buying behaviour. The study h ighlights that social media
engagement, influencer recommendations, and digital tools  can shape consumer awareness,
preferences, and purchasing decisions. It emphasizes the growing role of digital platforms in
influencing cosmetic consumers and supporting brands in developing targeted marketing
strategies.
Deepika (2023) examines the influence of social media influencers on customers’ purchase
decisions for beauty products. The study highlights that influencer credibility, product
recommendations, engaging content, and social media interactions can shape consumer
perceptions and purchasing decisions. It emphasizes the growing role of influencers in
creating awareness and influencing beauty-product choices among social media users.
Grover and Arora (2024)  examine the influence of Instagram and influencer marketing on

consumer perception and public relations in the beauty industry. The study highlights that
influencer credibility, Instagram engagement, visual content, and product recommendations
can shape consu mer perceptions and strengthen brand communication. It emphasizes
Instagram’s role as an effective platform for beauty brands to build awareness, engage
consumers, and develop favourable brand relationships.
Krishnan and Jamunarani (2025)  examine the influ ence of social media marketing on
consumer buying behaviour in Chennai City. The study highlights that social media
engagement, online promotional content, product information, and digital interactions
influence consumers’ awareness, perceptions, and purch ase decisions. The findings
emphasize the growing importance of social media as a marketing channel for shaping
consumer buying behaviour.
Sindhu (2025)  examines the impact of influencer marketing on consumer behaviour in the
cosmetic industry. The study h ighlights that influencer credibility, engaging content, product
recommendations, and social media interaction  can influence consumers’ perceptions and
purchasing behaviour. It emphasizes that influencer marketing has become an important
promotional strate gy for cosmetic brands seeking to build consumer awareness, trust, and
purchase intention.
Singh, Telukdarie, and Mongwe (2024)  investigate the impact of digital social media
influencers on beauty and personal care purchases in South Africa. The study high lights that
influencer credibility, engaging digital content, trust, and social media interaction  can
influence consumers’ product perceptions and purchase behaviour. The findings emphasize
the growing importance of digital influencers in shaping beauty an d personal care purchasing
decisions.
5. Conclusion
Based on the above literature it is concluded that Influencer marketing has become an
important component of digital promotion in the cosmetic and beauty industry. The reviewed
literature consistently indica tes that influencers can affect consumer awareness, perceptions,
engagement, trust, and purchase intentions. Credibility, authenticity, quality of content,
product demonstrations, recommendations, and audience engagement  emerge as important
elements of eff ective influencer marketing.  The literature also indicates that influencer
popularity alone does not guarantee marketing effectiveness. Consumers increasingly value
genuine opinions, relevant information, transparency, and meaningful interaction. Excessive
promotional content or perceived inauthenticity may weaken consumer trust. Cosmetic
brands should therefore focus on selecting influencers whose expertise, values, audience, and
content are compatible with the brand.Overall, influencer marketing provides cosmetic
brands with opportunities to create personalized communication, reach targeted audiences,
strengthen brand relationships, and influence purchasing behaviour. Future strategies should
combine influencer credibility with authentic content, transpare nt communication, and data -
driven digital marketing practices.
6. Scope for Future Study
Future research can examine influencer marketing using primary data and quantitative
models to establish stronger relationships between influencer characteristics and con sumer

ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE IN MODERN COMMERCE AND BUSINESS:
FROM BUSINESS AUTOMATION TO INTELLIGENT DECISION-MAKING
S. Hemavathy
B. Com (CA)
Sriram College of Arts and Science
Perumalpattu
Abstract
Artificial Intelligence (AI) is fundamentally transforming modern commercial
ecosystems by automating operational workflows, optimizing strategic decision -making, and
elevating customer engagement models. By processing massive enterprise datasets in real
time, cognitive technologies enable businesses to achieve hi gher efficiency, precise market
forecasting, and personalized consumer experiences. However, the widespread integration of
AI also introduces operational challenges regarding data security, algorithmic fairness,
regulatory compliance, and workforce transit ion. This seminar paper examines the primary
applications, key benefits, societal challenges, and future trajectories of AI in commerce,
emphasizing the critical balance between technological innovation, ethical governance, and
human strategic supervision required for long-term organizational success.
KEYWORDS: Artificial Intelligence, Digital Commerce, Machine Learning, Automation,
Customer Experience, Business Strategy, Data Governance.
 Introduction
  Modern corporate entities are rapidly transitioning f rom legacy operational models to
intelligent, algorithm -driven frameworks that leverage cloud computing and big data
analytics to drive sustained growth.  Artificial Intelligence empowers enterprise systems to
process high -velocity consumer data streams ins tantaneously, unlocking continuous
operational visibility and automated decision -making. AI is no longer limited to specialized
IT departments; it is now deeply integrated across core commercial functions including
inventory governance, digital marketing, dynamic pricing, and supply chain management.
Objectives of the study
 Evaluate Functional Scope: To analyze the expanding role and operational
mechanisms of Artificial Intelligence across modern enterprise and digital commerce
touchpoints.
 Assess Benefits and Risks: To evaluate the operational gains in business productivity
alongside the critical data privacy, cybersecurity, and regulatory risks associated with
automated systems.
 Identify Future Competencies: To define the essential digital skills, technical
capabilities, and ethical leadership practices required by commerce graduates in an
automated economy.
Review of Literature
McKinsey Global Institute (2023):  McKinsey reported that Generative AI could add $4.4
trillion annually to the glo bal economy. Their study highlighted that 50% of current work

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN:978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 315

activities could be automated by 2030, forcing commerce to shift from manual to intelligent
automation.
World Economic Forum - Future of Jobs Report (2023 & 2025) : WEF identified that AI
will di splace 83 million jobs but will create 69 million new roles requiring skills like
analytical thinking, AI literacy, and data management. This proves the future of commerce
needs a new skilled workforce.
Salesforce State of Commerce Report (2026):  The recent report states that AI agents now
handle 20% of global e -commerce traffic and consumer use of AI shopping assistants grew
by 200% from 2025 to 2026. This shows the shift from Business Automation to Intelligent
Decision-Making.
Hyper-Personalized marketing and sales:
Machine learning algorithms analyze individual browsing behaviors, transaction histories,
and user preferences to deliver dynamic product recommendations.
Intelligent Customer Service Support:
Conversational AI agents and natural language chat bots provide continuous 24/7
service assistance, handling routine customer inquiries instantly and optimizing overall
engagement.
Accelerated Executive Decision-Making:
Advanced business intelligence platforms summarize complex market data rapidly, provid ing
executive leadership with actionable insights to make swift, data-backed decisions.
Data Privacy and Cybersecurity Vulnerabilities:
Data privacy and cybersecurity vulnerabilities have become critical concerns for
banks as digital transactions, mobile banking, and AI -based systems handle vast amounts of
sensitive customer information. Cyberattacks such as phishing, malware, ransomware,
account takeover, and data breaches can compromise personal data and cause severe financial
and reputational losses. We ak authentication, unencrypted data storage, third -party
integrations, and human errors further increase vulnerability. In AI -powered fraud detection,
large datasets used for training models may themselves become targets if not properly
secured. Privacy ri sks also arise from excessive data collection, profiling, and lack of
customer consent. Banks must therefore adopt strong encryption, multi -factor authentication,
continuous monitoring, and strict access controls

AI will make future businesses smarter and more automated through better inventory
management, real-time pricing, risk management, market research, and personalized content.
However, human thinking, critical thinking, and e thical judgment will continue to be
important.
SUGGESTIONS
 Small shops should use cheap and easy AI tools like Tally Prime, Zoho Books, and
Shopify. It will save time and reduce work. Companies must keep customer data safe. They
should use strong password s, OTP, and encryption to avoid data theft  and companies should
openly share the customers when they are using AI. It will be creating awareness among the
customers.
 CONCLUSION
  Artificial Intelligence represents a foundational technology that significantly enhances
enterprise productivity, dynamic scalability, and competitive market positioning.  Achieving
sustainable commercial success requires balancing cutting -edge computational intelligence
with robust privacy safeguards, fair algorithms, an d proactive risk oversight.  Commerce
professionals who cultivate strong data literacy, operational flexibility, and ethical
responsibility will lead the next generation of digital business growth.

Artificial Intelligence for Banking Security: Detecting Transaction Anomalies and
Digital Payment Fraud
R. Hemnath
Sriram College of arts and science
Perumalpattu
 Abstract
Swindle is one of the major risks faced by banks in the digital era. The rapid growth
of online banking and mobile payments has created new fraud methods, while Artificial
Intelligence helps institutions analyse large volumes of transaction data to detect unusual
patterns. Machine learning models learn from historical transactions and generate alerts when
behaviour differs from normal, supporting real-time monitoring and faster response.

Introduction
Digital banking provides speed and convenience but incre ases fraud risk including
phishing, account takeover, and card fraud (Kognitos). Traditional rule -based detection
struggles with changing patterns and large volumes, producing high false positives, while AI
weighs hundreds of signals at once to score risk in milliseconds (Fraudio). Machine learning
models learn from historical transactions and detect unusual patterns. Machine learning
models learn from historical transactions and generate alerts when behaviour differs from
normal, supporting real-time monitoring and faster response.

Objectives of the study
1. To understand the concept and evolution of AI -powered fraud detection in banking and
financial services.
2. To explain the working mechanism of AI and machine learning models in identifying
suspicious transactions and behavioural anomalies.
3. To examine the major types of banking and digital payment fraud including card fraud,
account takeover, phishing, and identity fraud.
Review of Literature
Kokkalakonda (2022) studied machine learning models for banking fraud        detection and
found neural networks achieved 96.1% accuracy with fast real -time response, proving more
effective than traditional rule-based methods.
Fraudio (2026)  explains that AI scores transactions using hundreds of signals like device,
location and history in milliseconds, helping banks reduce both false declines and
chargebacks.
 LexisNexis Risk Solutions (2026)  highlights self -calibrating AI with explainable reason
codes, helping analysts understand alerts and adapt to new fraud patterns.
ETBFSI (2026) reports Mastercard's use of continuously learning AI to detect fraud across
its global network while maintaining high approval rates for genuine customers.
     Ai-Powered Fraud Detection Works
 A transaction occurs, the system evaluates amount, location, device, time and history to
produce a risk score (Fraudio). This process of data collection, pattern analysis, risk scoring
and alert generation mirrors industry models for real-time authorization.

National Level Seminar on Application of Artificial Intelligence in
Commerce and Management
ISBN:978-81-180170-6-6                 DOI:10.65647/msp/141/2026 Page 318

      Ai And Machine Learning Techniques
Supervised learning uses labelled historical transactions, while unsupervised learning
identifies anomalies without labels (Kokkalakonda 566). Studies show neu ral networks
achieved 96.1% accuracy with 32 ms response time, outperforming decision trees and
random forests (Kokkalakonda 569). Explainable AI with reason codes is now used to help
analysts understand automated decisions (LexisNexis Risk Solutions).
Benefits Of Ai-Powered Fraud Detection
Banks like DBS Bank process over 1.8 million transactions per hour with AI, achieving
90% reduction in false positives and 60% improvement in detection accuracy (Byrne).
Mastercard similarly uses AI across its global network to learn continuously from new data
and reduce false declines (ETBFSI). Future systems will use more behavioral analytics,
graph-based detection and adaptive learning to create a connected view of risk, while
improvements in explainable AI will aid investigators.
Conclusion
AI powered fraud detection has emerged as a vital component of modern banking and
financial services. The present study shows that by analysing large volumes of transaction
and behavioural data in real -time, AI and machine learni ng models can identify unusual
patterns, generate risk scores, and support faster responses to potential fraud more effectively
than traditional rule -based systems. Techniques such as supervised learning, unsupervised
learning, and anomaly detection enhance speed, scalability, and continuous monitoring across
digital channels. At the same time, AI is not without limitations. Issues related to false alerts,
data quality, privacy, cybersecurity, and evolving scam tactics require careful attention.
Effective fraud prevention therefore depends on a balanced approach that combines reliable
technology, secure data practices, clear regulatory compliance, and human oversight. When
implemented responsibly, AI can significantly strengthen financial security and foster  safer,
more trusted digital banking services.
